# What sixty agents found on a morning audit, 2026-11-15

## What this is

The last independent readings of the whole package were the audits of 2026-09-18,
2026-09-20 and 2026-09-25. The incompatibility notes (`notes/INCOMPATIBILITIES*.md` and
`notes/incompat/`) record narrower reviews. Since 2026-09-26 about 1700 commits landed,
among them the quantum metric, Wannier functions, the kernel polynomial method, the orbital field and route U of the
magnetic ultracell (P161 to P177). This audit reads the whole package again at commit
`b5b88c5c`, with a weight on what changed after 2026-09-26.

The package was cut into 30 slices: 24 by subsystem, 3 across it (the recurring traps of
`CLAUDE.md` by grep, the tests, the docs against the code) and 3 for optimization only
(recompilation and host syncs, peak memory, repeated work across calls). Each slice had
one finder on Opus 5.5, reading only, and behind it one skeptic on Fable 5.2, told to
refute every finding unless it could quote the code path and name a valid input that
reaches it without being refused upstream. Where a finder returned nothing, the second
agent was a second reader on another regime instead, because a reading that could not
have returned a surprise proves nothing by finding none. 60 agents in total.

Nothing was run. This was a reading audit only, by the user's instruction ("no runs,
just reading"): no Python, no pytest, no check script, on this workstation or anywhere
else. So every entry below is a claim about what the source says and not a measurement
of what it computes, and none of it belongs in `PLAN.md` until it has a number. The
last section is the list of what to address, in order, for a later session that can
run the code.

The agents' transcripts were searched afterwards for anything executed. Of about 3000
shell commands, none ran the package, pytest or ssh. One agent ran
`python3 -c "print(1)"`, which imports nothing. The models were read from the agents'
own records: 30 on Opus and 30 on Fable.

The line numbers are those of `b5b88c5c`. If a line no longer holds what an entry
describes, grep for the symbol.

## The count

60 agents, no errors, 32 minutes. The finders made 72 claims: the skeptics confirmed 67,
left 3 as plausible and refuted 2. The skeptics and the second reader added 25 more,
which nobody else checked. A few of the 97 are the same defect seen from two slices: the
dropped `zeeman` on the ultracell route was found by three slices on their own, and the
strain slot of `get_electrostriction` by two.

| kind | confirmed | plausible | refuted | raised by a skeptic, unchecked |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| bug | 25 | 1 | 2 | 7 |
| hole | 35 | 1 | 0 | 13 |
| optimization | 7 | 1 | 0 | 5 |

The refutation rate, 2 of 72, is the same warning as on 2026-09-18 (3 of 48). Either
these defects are easy to establish by reading, or the skeptics were not adversarial
enough to surprise themselves, and this audit cannot tell the two apart. What the
skeptics did do is change the severity: of the 72, they kept the finder's severity on
53, lowered it on 18 (most often to `minor` or to "diagnostic only") and raised it on 1.
So the severity in each entry is the skeptic's. The only slice where the finder found
nothing was the sweep of the recurring traps by grep, and there the second reader found
one (a literal `complex128` in the symmetry phases).

The label "known: new" means the finder grepped `OPEN.md`, `PLAN.md` and the three audit
files and found nothing. It does not cover the incompatibility notes. I grepped those
afterwards for eleven of the ranked symbols (`spin_dos`, `default_nbnd`, the mean Fermi
level, `_projection_tetrahedra`, `with_spin`, `dis_froz`, `fft_factors`, vc-relax under
`nosym`, `grad_m` NaN, left-handed cells, `zeeman`), and none of their hits is about the
defect named here.

Each entry below is the record the agents wrote, kept as they wrote it, with the code
they quoted. The list at the end, "What to address, in order", is the part written for
a later session.


## Confirmed by the skeptic: bugs (25)

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:2824`: with_spin(4) carries a calculator-level nbnd unchanged into the spinor run, where it beats the doubled system.nbnd

Slice `calculator`, bug, severity `wrong-answer for a smeared metal only; a crash (loud) at fixed occupations`, status **confirmed**, known: new. _SPINOR_LEG_OPTIONS records the same mechanism for the force theorem only; OPEN.md, PLAN.md and the three audits have nothing on with_spin and nbnd..

System.with_spin doubles system.nbnd going into nspin = 4 (builder.py:606, 'a spinor band holds one electron'). But Calculator._derived rebuilds the child with **self.defaults, nbnd included, and every entry point resolves `nbnd or system.nbnd or default_nbnd(...)` (driver.py:4405, :8369), so the stated value wins over the doubled one. The spinor SCF therefore runs with the collinear band count, roughly half the bands it needs. The facade already knows this defect: _SPINOR_LEG_OPTIONS (calculator.py:232-249) withholds nbnd on the force-theorem leg for exactly this reason ('the second leg ran with roughly half the bands it needs'). The with_spin, with_moments and with_turning_moments route has no such guard. I found no capacity refusal in scf/ that would catch nbnd < nelec on the spinor side. For a metal with enough bands for the electrons but too few empty ones for the smearing, this converges silently to a wrong energy, which is what default_nbnd's docstring (driver.py:1806) says too few bands do.

Trigger: `Calculator.from_file('al-metal.in', nbnd=8).with_spin(4).get_scf()`, or any calculator with nbnd passed at construction (the first SHARED_OPTIONS entry) and then promoted to nspin = 4. Going the other way, with_spin(1) from a spinor calculator with nbnd set keeps twice the bands, which only costs time.

```
calculator.py:2824 `derived = Calculator(system, self.pseudos, announce=self.announce, basis=basis, **self.defaults)`; builder.py:602-606 `if nbnd is None and self.nbnd is not None: ... nbnd = int(round(self.nbnd * factor))`; driver.py:4405 `return nbnd or system.nbnd or default_nbnd(`
```

Skeptic: The path is as stated. calculator.py:2742-2769 `with_spin` calls `self.system.with_spin(nspin, **options)` and then `_derived`, and calculator.py:2824-2825 rebuilds the child with `**self.defaults`, nbnd included (it is the first SHARED_OPTIONS member, calculator.py:104). builder.py:602-606 doubles only `self.nbnd` of the System. `_defaults_for(run_scf)` forwards the default by name (calculator.py:2892-2896, nbnd has no callee-chosen value), and driver.py:8369 `nbnd = nbnd or system.nbnd or default_nbnd(` lets it win over the doubled system value. I found no guard in `_derived`, `with_spin` or `get_scf`. The facade's own comment at calculator.py:234-249 calls the same mechanism a defect for the force theorem. Reach is wider than the finder says: `from_keywords` sends its own keyword `nbands` to the System (io/keywords.py:270) but a `nbnd=` given there falls into `options` and so into `defaults` (calculator.py:686-697), so the two spellings behave differently under `with_spin(4)`. Two corrections. First, at fixed occupations the run is not silent: occupations.py:96-97 raises `electrons need N bands but only M were computed`, so an insulator with nbnd equal to the collinear occupied count stops with a clear message. The silent case is smearing with nelec <= nbnd, for instance nickel (10 electrons) with `nbnd=12` and `with_spin(4)` as in docs/guide/magnetism.md:256, which runs on 12 spinor bands with 2 empty ones. I did not find a warning on an occupied top band, and I did not run it, so the size of the energy error is not measured. Second, `with_moments` and `with_turning_moments` do not change the regime, so the factor is 1 there and they are not affected. tests/unit/test_continuation_machinery.py:713 pins the doubling on System only; no test covers the calculator default, and OPEN.md and notes/AUDIT-2026-09-18.md have nothing on it.

Fix: In `Calculator.with_spin` (or `_derived`), rescale `defaults['nbnd']` by the same factor as builder.py:605 when the spinor flag changes, or drop it with a warning naming the value. Add a test that `Calculator(..., nbnd=n).with_spin(4)` runs with 2n bands.

### `defuqumat/kpm/dos.py:357`: spin_dos is summed over a symmetry-reduced wedge with plain weights, neither completed nor refused

Slice `kpm`, bug, severity `wrong-answer`, status **confirmed**, known: new. PLAN P168 (around line 27899) and P169 (around 28056) give the whole-grid rule for the per-orbital, per-point and tensor traces only. The guide Limits note says the opposite for the DOS page as a whole..

run_kpm_dos keeps the k-set of the run (kpm_setup never calls whole_grid_calculation), and spin_resolved_dos contracts Tr[sigma_i T_n(H_k)] with the wedge weights. That is an axial vector per k, so the weighted wedge sum is not the zone sum. The PDOS, the LDOS and the conductivity all move a wedge to its whole grid for exactly this reason (traces.py:139, conductivity.py:719). This path does not, and the guide says a wedge is fine (docs/guide/dos.md:495, written for the scalar DOS). A nonmagnetic spinor without inversion gets a nonzero spin DOS where time reversal forces all three components to zero. A magnet gets a nonzero value in the components its group forbids, for example s_x and s_y of a z magnet with C4z. The component the group allows survives. I read that a noncollinear run is reduced, with time reversal when nonmagnetic, from builder.py:498-503 and kpoints.py:511-525. I did not run it. No test under tests/ calls the KPM spin_resolved path, and the P168 check (uniform b sigma_i on Si2) cannot show the error.

Trigger: Calculator.get_kpm_dos(spin_resolved=True) on a noncolin + lspinorb nonmagnetic crystal without inversion (zincblende GaAs, a Rashba slab) on an automatic grid with symmetry on. The zone sum is exactly zero and the wedge sum is not. Also a magnetic spinor run with symmetry (bcc Fe, m along z), for the transverse components.

```
dos.py:357-359 `if spin_resolved:\n        spin = spin_resolved_dos(hamiltonians, weights, centre, half, n_moments, n_random, seed, kernel, energies, batch)` with `weights = np.asarray(calculation.system.kpoints.weights)` (the wedge); dos.py:440-441 `moments = np.einsum("k,kirn->in", np.asarray(weights) * live, raw) / n_random`; contrast traces.py:139-142 `if not kpoints.reduced: return calculation ... KPoints.automatic(kpoints.grid, ...)` used by PDOS, LDOS and conductivity only
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. Calculator.get_kpm_dos (calculator.py:1020-1023) passes calculation=self.calculation and no kpoints, so threaded_calculation (workflows/nscf.py:587-593) returns the run's own k-set. kpm_setup (dos.py:371-399) never calls whole_grid_calculation; in defuqumat/kpm only traces.py:168 and conductivity.py:721 do. require_a_kpm_regime (dos.py:222-249) refuses a spiral, the half sphere and an overlap, not a reduced set. The weights are the wedge's (dos.py:344) and spin_resolved_dos contracts them directly, dos.py:440-441: `moments = np.einsum("k,kirn->in", np.asarray(weights) * live, raw) / n_random`. The builder reduces a noncollinear automatic grid with the group and with time reversal when nonmagnetic (builder.py:1524-1529, `time_reversal=not noinv and not magnetic`; the finder's 498-503 is the with_spin docstring, the wrong lines). Tr[sigma_i delta(E-H_k)] is an axial vector per k, odd under time reversal, so the weighted wedge sum is the zone sum only after projection on the invariant part. The guide says the opposite for the whole page (docs/guide/dos.md:495) and so does the kpoints docstring (dos.py:296-297). No test reads spin_dos: the only test (tests/unit/test_kpm_traces.py:152-160) contracts spin_moments by hand on Si2 with a uniform field, where every k gives the same vector. Two corrections to the finder's trigger. A z magnet without spin-orbit coupling has zero s_x and s_y at every k, so it shows nothing; the trigger needs lspinorb or truly noncollinear moments. A spinor with an ultrasoft or PAW dataset is refused at overlap.py:260-262, so the dataset must be norm-conserving and fully relativistic; these exist and pass the builder (Se.rel-pbe-nc-sg15.UPF with noncolin and lspinorb in tests/data/qe/se-trigonal-soc.in, also Nb, Ni and I). Valid triggers: trigonal Se as in that file without nosym and noinv, or monolayer NbSe2 with spin-orbit coupling, where the Ising s_z has one sign inside the wedge and the zone sum is exactly zero. I did not run it.

Fix: Project the (3, N) spin moments on the invariant part of an axial vector with the group of the run: det(R) R with the t_rev sign, and zero when time reversal is a symmetry. This is exact because the wedge weight is the star size. The alternative is whole_grid_calculation. Correct the scalar-trace sentence at dos.py:296-297 and docs/guide/dos.md:495, and add a test on a spinor without inversion.

### `defuqumat/magnetic/fold.py:457`: The magnetizability takes the total energy of an SCF that did not converge, with no check and no warning

Slice `magnetic`, bug, severity `wrong-answer`, status **confirmed**, known: new (PLAN.md:30388 records the converged diamond run, which reached 4, 7 and 7 iterations).

run_magnetic_scf ends its loop at max_iterations (default 60) and returns converged=False without raising or warning. In that case total_energy is the non-variational sum: the Hartree and xc terms at the input density, deband against v[rho_in], and no descf. run_magnetizability builds chi as a second difference of the three total_energy values and never reads .converged. Calculator.get_magnetizability has no check either. So if one of the three field SCFs stops at its iteration budget, the chi it returns looks valid but is wrong. The second difference divides by dB^2 and does not cancel an error that differs between +B, 0 and -B. Fix: raise in run_magnetizability when any results[p].converged is False, or raise in run_magnetic_scf itself as the zero-field SCF does.

Trigger: calc.get_magnetizability(kmesh=..., nbnd=...) on an insulator whose field SCF needs more than 60 Anderson steps at mixing_beta = 0.7 (for example a larger supercell or a cell with vacuum), or any max_iterations=... passed through options that is too small

```
fold.py:327 `converged = accuracy < conv_thr`; :343-345 `if converged: final_potential = potential_used; break` with no else after the loop and no raise; :358 `total_energy=total, ... converged=converged`; :450-457 `results = {p: run_magnetic_scf(...) for p in (0, 1, -1)}` / `energies = {p: float(r.total_energy) for p, r in results.items()}`
```

Skeptic: In fold.py the loop `for iteration in range(1, int(max_iterations) + 1)` sets `converged = accuracy < conv_thr` and breaks only when it is true. There is no else branch, no raise and no warning after the loop, and the return passes total_energy=total and converged=converged as they stand. When not converged, the Hartree and xc terms are those of potential_in, the potential of the mixed input density, while eband is from the output states, so the total is not variational. run_magnetizability (fold.py:450-462) builds the second difference from float(r.total_energy) of the three runs and never reads r.converged. Calculator.get_magnetizability (calculator.py:1693-1696) adds no check, and **options forwards max_iterations, so max_iterations=3 is a concrete valid input that returns a chi from three unconverged energies. The zero-field state is protected by require_converged (calculator.py:946-947), so this is the one SCF on this path with no guard. Calculator.get_magnetic_bands(scf=True) (calculator.py:1669-1672) returns an unconverged MagneticSCF in the same silent way, although .converged is on it. With the default 60 iterations the trigger needs a cell that mixes slowly, which I could not settle by reading; PLAN records 4 to 7 iterations on diamond and graphene.

Fix: Raise in run_magnetizability when any of the three results has converged False, naming p and the last dr2. Warn by name at the end of run_magnetic_scf when the loop ends unconverged.

### `defuqumat/wannier/engine.py:132`: dis_froz=(x, None) silently turns the frozen window off, although run_wannier documents an open upper end

Slice `wannier`, bug, severity `wrong-answer (silent, on a rare input; Wannier90 refuses it)`, status **confirmed**.

run_wannier's docstring says each end of dis_win and dis_froz may be None "for open". run_wannier unpacks dis_froz into dis_froz_min and dis_froz_max (workflows/wannier.py:586). run_engine then turns frozen states on only when dis_froz_max is not None (engine.py:132). So dis_froz=(x, None) freezes nothing, and the dis_froz_min the user gave is dropped without a word. The disentanglement then runs on a different subspace, and the bands the user asked to keep exactly are not kept, so the spreads, the hoppings and the interpolated bands change. EngineOptions' own docstring (dis_froz_max = None means no frozen window) agrees with the code, so the two docstrings disagree. Nothing in run_wannier, Calculator.get_wannier or EngineOptions refuses this input. I found this by reading and did not run it. Repair: refuse dis_froz with an open top by name, or map the open top to dis_win_max (or to the top eigenvalue) and turn the frozen window on.

Trigger: get_wannier(projections=[...], num_wann=..., num_bands > num_wann, dis_win=(None, 17.0), dis_froz=(5.0, None)), on any cell with given projection lines (the 'auto' path refuses dis_froz first; SCDM with dis_froz reaches the same engine line).

```
defuqumat/workflows/wannier.py:283 `eV, either end ``None`` for open; Wannier90's ``dis_win_min/max``` ; :586 `window["dis_froz_min"], window["dis_froz_max"] = dis_froz` ; defuqumat/wannier/engine.py:128 `froz_min = win_min if o.dis_froz_min is None else o.dis_froz_min` ; :132 `win_min, win_max, o.dis_froz_max is not None, froz_min,`
```

Skeptic: I followed dis_froz from the front door to the engine and found no guard. Calculator.get_wannier (defuqumat/calculator.py:1555-1593) forwards the options unchanged. run_wannier refuses dis_froz only with projections='auto' (defuqumat/workflows/wannier.py:374-382), then unpacks it at :585-586 (`window["dis_froz_min"], window["dis_froz_max"] = dis_froz`) into EngineOptions, a frozen dataclass with no validation (defuqumat/wannier/engine.py:42-67). run_engine passes `o.dis_froz_max is not None` as frozen_states (engine.py:132) and 0.0 as the top (engine.py:133), and dis_windows only fills the frozen mask under `if frozen_states:` (defuqumat/wannier/disentangle.py:65). So dis_froz=(5.0, None) freezes nothing and dis_froz_min is never read. The run_wannier docstring promises the opposite at workflows/wannier.py:282-284 ("either end ``None`` for open"), while EngineOptions says "dis_froz_max = None means no frozen window" (engine.py:48). The reference refuses this input: wannier90-3.1.0/src/parameters.F90:1691-1692, 'found dis_froz_min but not dis_froz_max'. No test and no doc uses an open top: every call in tests/, notebooks/ and docs/guide/ is (None, x) or (a, b). OPEN.md has no Wannier entry and PLAN.md P162 to P166 do not record it. The same path is reached with engine_options=dict(dis_froz_min=5.0) alone. I did not run it, by the rule of this audit.

Fix: In run_wannier, and in run_engine for the engine_options route, refuse dis_froz_min given with dis_froz_max None, with Wannier90's wording, and change the docstring at workflows/wannier.py:282-284 to say that only the lower end of dis_froz can be open. The alternative is to map the open top to win_max and turn the frozen window on.

### `defuqumat/scf/residual_split.py:75`: residual_bins reads a collinear (up, down) density as (charge, magnetization)

Slice `mixing`, bug, severity `wrong-answer (diagnostic only, off by default, feeds neither ethr nor conv_thr)`, status **confirmed**, known: new. OPEN.md Y2 (line 2128, closed) and PLAN.md:21119 describe the instrument and do not mention the channel convention..

This is a diagnostic only. It feeds neither ethr nor conv_thr. For a two-channel density, residual_bins sets the 'charge' bin from residual[0] and the 'longitudinal' bin from residual[1]. The loop hands it its own rho and rho_out, and at nspin = 2 these are (up, down), not (n, m_z). So the 'charge' bin is the norm of the change in the up channel, and the 'longitudinal' bin is the norm of the change in the down channel. A pure charge change (d_up = d_down) and a pure moment change (d_up = -d_down) give the same two numbers, so the split cannot tell charge sloshing from a moving moment in a collinear magnet, which is the question the instrument (MAGNETISM-NEXT F2) exists to answer. The fix is to rotate first, charge = r[0] + r[1] and moment = r[0] - r[1], as Kerker and _scale_magnetization already do. tests/unit/test_residual_split.py:137-146 builds (1.0, 0.5) -> (1.0, 0.52) and changes channel 1 only. On that input both conventions give a nonzero longitudinal bin, so the test cannot fail on this.

Trigger: Any nspin = 2 run with run_scf(residual_split=True), and tools/cluster/f2_option0.py, which prints row['charge'] and the bins.

```
residual_split.py:75 def residual_bins(...): bins = {"charge": _norm(residual[0], weight)}; if rho_in.shape[0] < 4: bins["longitudinal"] = (_norm(residual[1], weight) if rho_in.shape[0] == 2 else 0.0). The caller is driver.py:9663, iteration_split = residual_bins(rho, rho_out, calculation.system.cell, becsum_state, becsum_out). The convention is in driver.py:1597, magnetization = rho_r[0] - rho_r[1], and in mixing.py:943-949, where densities are carried as (up, down) and rotated into (charge, magnetization) around the screening.
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. The collinear branch is defuqumat/scf/residual_split.py:99-112: bins = {"charge": _norm(residual[0], weight)} and bins["longitudinal"] = _norm(residual[1], weight) if rho_in.shape[0] == 2. The only caller is driver.py:9663, residual_bins(rho, rho_out, calculation.system.cell, becsum_state, becsum_out), with the loop's own density. That density is (up, down) at nspin = 2: mixing.py:943-949 ('Densities are carried here as (up, down) ... rotated into (charge, magnetization) and back'), mixing.py:176-179 ('scaling channel 1 would scale down rather than the moment'), driver.py:5383-5389 and driver.py:1597 (magnetization = rho_r[0] - rho_r[1]). So 'charge' is |d rho_up| and 'longitudinal' is |d rho_down|, and d_up = d_down gives the same two numbers as d_up = -d_down. No guard stops it: residual_split appears in driver.py only at 8120 (the keyword), 9656 and 9998, and Calculator.get_scf(**options) at calculator.py:784 forwards it with no spin check. The guide sends a reader exactly there: docs/guide/ground-state.md:157-162 says get_scf(residual_split=True) writes 'how much of the residual of each iteration is in the charge and how much is in the magnetization' as the way to decide on mixing_beta_mag, and mixing_beta_mag acts at nspin = 2 through _scale_magnetization, which does rotate. PLAN.md:21708-21718 makes the same promise ('the number that tells them apart is residual_split'). The test at tests/unit/test_residual_split.py:137-146 changes channel 1 only and asserts longitudinal > 0, which holds under both conventions, so it does not protect the claim. One part of the finder's trigger is wrong: tools/cluster/f2_option0.py does not reach this branch. Its stage_split and stage_trip run fe-noncolin-pbe-stress.in (nspin_mag = 4, the correct branch), and --cell (line 226) is read only by stage_betamag, which runs with split=False. The record is also clean: the collinear '4 of 25' of PLAN.md:21165 and 21700 is an iteration count against the nonmagnetic twin of fe-mag-1k, not a reading of the bins, so no recorded number rests on the wrong split. The valid trigger is calc.get_scf(residual_split=True) or run_scf(residual_split=True) on any nspin = 2 cell.

Fix: In the shape[0] == 2 branch rotate first: charge = _norm(residual[0] + residual[1]), longitudinal = _norm(residual[0] - residual[1]). Add a test with an equal change in both channels asserting longitudinal == 0 and one with an opposite change asserting charge == 0, and fix the Args line that calls the input (nspin_mag, ...).

### `defuqumat/scf/residual_split.py:165`: becsum_residual's 'magnetic' half at nspin = 2 is the down channel's change, recorded on every run

Slice `mixing`, bug, severity `minor (a mislabelled diagnostic, on by default; the finder's wrong-answer is too strong)`, status **confirmed**, known: new. OPEN.md Y2 (line 2128) records that becsum_residual was added and closed, and says nothing about the nspin = 2 channel meaning..

This is a diagnostic only. It feeds neither ethr nor conv_thr. Unlike residual_bins, it is on by default: every iteration writes it to history as becsum_magnetic_accuracy and prints it on the log line as '/x mag'. becsum_residual takes difference[1:] as the magnetization channels and treats channel 0 as the charge. That holds at nspin_mag = 4. At nspin = 2 the becsum is (up, down), so the 'magnetic' number is |d becsum_down|. A step that changes only the augmentation charge (d_up = d_down) then reports a nonzero magnetic residual, and a step that only moves the moment reports half its size, wrongly normalised. The docstring sells this number as the one that 'can move while the charge sits still' on a PAW magnet, and at nspin = 2 it cannot show that. The confidence is lower than F1 on one point: that becsum is (up, down) internally rests on io/qe_density.py:32 (it 'stays (up, down) for nspin = 2'), and _becsum there reshapes paw.txt's (nspin, nat, ...) with no rotation, and on scope.md's P160 line. I did not read the code that builds becsum in the loop. tests/unit/test_residual_split.py:239-246 sets after[1] = 1.0 alone and asserts magnetic == sqrt(2). Its docstring says 'nspin = 2 has channel 1 as the magnetization', which encodes the wrong convention, and the input changes channel 1 only, so it cannot tell the two conventions apart.

Trigger: Any nspin = 2 run with an ultrasoft or PAW species. The number appears in SCFResult.history and on the log line at every iteration, with no option needed.

```
residual_split.py:165 def becsum_residual(becsum_in, becsum_out): ... difference = after - before; total += float(jnp.sum(difference**2)); if before.shape[0] >= 2: magnetic += float(jnp.sum(difference[1:] ** 2)). Docstring: 'Channel 0 is the charge and is the piece addusdens already routed onto the grid.' Callers: driver.py:9653 becsum_accuracy, becsum_magnetic = becsum_residual(becsum_state, becsum_out); driver.py:9997 entry["becsum_magnetic_accuracy"] = becsum_magnetic; driver.py:10049 the log line. Convention: io/qe_density.py:32 '... stays (up, down) for nspin = 2'.
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it, and I closed the link the finder left open. residual_split.py:217-220: difference = after - before; total += sum(difference**2); if before.shape[0] >= 2: magnetic += sum(difference[1:]**2). The arrays it receives are the unrotated per-channel stack: driver.py:9502 becsum_out = calculation.becsum(wavefunctions, wg), driver.py:5079-5082 calls density.becsum, and density.py:446-453 builds per_channel = [channel(psi[spin], weights[spin]) for spin in range(psi.shape[0])] and stacks it, with the comment 'sum_bec writes into becsum(:,:,current_spin)'. becsum_state is the mixed copy of the same representation (driver.py:10060, 10081). driver.py:1036-1049 (_addusdens) reads b[spin] per channel 'while the density is still in the (up, down) representation', which confirms it. So at nspin = 2 channel 1 is the down occupations and 'magnetic' is |d becsum_down|. Reach: driver.py:9653 runs every iteration with no option, driver.py:9996-9997 writes becsum_magnetic_accuracy to history, driver.py:10049 prints '/x mag'. A change of the augmentation charge alone (d_up = d_down) reports a nonzero 'mag', an unpolarized two-channel run reports one at every iteration, and a pure moment change reports half of |dm|. The docstring at residual_split.py:190-194 ('the one that can move while the charge sits still. Channel 0 is the charge') is false at nspin = 2. The test at tests/unit/test_residual_split.py:239-246 sets after[1] = 1 alone, which gives sqrt(2) under both conventions, and its docstring states the wrong one. I lower the severity because the number feeds nothing, still goes to zero at convergence and is only compared with itself across iterations; what is wrong is the label and the docstring. OPEN.md Y2 (line 2128, closed) repeats 'its magnetization channels alone' and says nothing about nspin = 2, so it is not recorded. Trigger: any nspin = 2 run with an ultrasoft or PAW species, for example a collinear PAW iron or nickel cell, at default keywords.

Fix: At before.shape[0] == 2 take the moment as difference[0] - difference[1] (and the charge as the sum if a charge half is wanted); keep difference[1:] at four channels. Add a test with an equal change in both channels asserting magnetic == 0, and correct the docstring of the test at line 239.

### `defuqumat/response/phononq.py:1637`: D(q != 0) keeps the Grimme-D2 Hessian at Gamma: only the Ewald sum is moved to q

Slice `phonon`, bug, severity `wrong-answer (narrow regime: D2 with an LDA at q != 0; size not measured)`, status **confirmed**, known: new (grepped OPEN.md, PLAN.md and the three audits for grimme, dispersion and d2ionq_disp together with phonon or q; PLAN.md P27 covers D2 in the energy, forces and stress only).

frozen_force_constants takes the Gamma frozen Hessian from _force_constants, whose energy is forces/energy.py:energy_at. That energy includes `"dispersion": moved.dispersion`, and at_positions recomputes it from the traced positions (driver.py:3833). swap_ewald then swaps only the Ewald sum: `frozen - ionic + ewald_dynamical_matrix(q)`. D2 is a pair sum across atoms and their images, so its Hessian at q carries e^{iqR} on every off-site and image term. ph.x adds it at the phonon's own q: dynmat0.f90:57-58 `CALL d2ionq_disp(alat,nat,ityp,at,bg,tau,xq,dynwrk)`. Here the q = 0 D2 force constants stand in for the q ones. The error is (1 - e^{iqR}) times the D2 pair force constants, nonzero even in a one-atom cell, and it falls on the interlayer modes D2 exists for. The k-chunked route has the same defect: its frozen Hessian is the Gamma walked passes, whose global_energy adds `moved.ewald + moved.dispersion` (chunked_phonon.py:633-634), and only swap_ewald and swap_core follow. Nothing on the q path refuses vdw_corr, and the frozen_force_constants docstring lists dynmat0(q) as dynmat_us + dynmatcc + d2ionq(q), leaving d2ionq_disp out.

Trigger: vdw_corr = 'grimme-d2' (or london = .true.) with an LDA dataset (the GGA is refused at q != 0, D2 is not), nosym and noinv, for instance graphite or a graphene bilayer on C.pz-vbc.UPF, with the phonon at q = (0, 0, 1/2) or any q != 0, on either route. Also electron_phonon_at_q on a D2 metal. The D2 test inputs use PBE, so no committed test reaches this.

```
phononq.py:1661 `return frozen - ionic + np.asarray(ewald_dynamical_matrix(calculation, q_cart))`; forces/energy.py:778 `"dispersion": moved.dispersion,`; chunked_phonon.py:633-634 `return (local_ + potential.ehart + potential.etxc + moved.ewald + moved.dispersion)`; QE PHonon/PH/dynmat0.f90:57-59 `IF (llondon .OR. lxdm) THEN CALL d2ionq_disp(...,xq,dynwrk)`
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. The frozen Hessian at q is the Gamma one: phononq.py:1625-1630 calls _force_constants, whose energy is frozen_energy (response/phonon.py:1896 and 1915), which returns `sum(contributions.values())` (forces/energy.py:793) with `"dispersion": moved.dispersion` among them (energy.py:778), and at_positions recomputes that entry from the traced positions (scf/driver.py:3832-3833), so the D2 pair Hessian at q = 0 is inside `frozen`. The move to q then touches the Ewald sum only: phononq.py:1661 `return frozen - ionic + np.asarray(ewald_dynamical_matrix(calculation, q_cart))`, where `ionic` is the Hessian of `calculation.ewald_sum.energy` alone (phononq.py:1649), and swap_core handles the core charge. The k-chunked route is the same: chunked_phonon.py:633-634 has `moved.ewald + moved.dispersion` in global_energy, and phononq.py:1976-1977 applies the same swap_core(swap_ewald(...)). QE adds the term at the phonon's q: PHonon/PH/dynmat0.f90:57-58 `IF (llondon .OR. lxdm) THEN / CALL d2ionq_disp(alat,nat,ityp,at,bg,tau,xq,dynwrk)`. The input is reachable: a grep of defuqumat/response/ for dispersion_sum and vdw_corr finds no refusal, require_a_two_sphere_regime (phononq.py:1663-1787) does not mention it, and the D2 builder has no condition on the functional (vdw/registry.py:114-123; vdw/grimme.py:115-117 says the default s6 is used for every functional), so D2 with an LDA dataset passes the GGA refusal at phononq.py:1756. The docstring at phononq.py:1585 lists dynmat0(q) without the dispersion term. Not recorded: d2ionq_disp appears nowhere in PLAN.md, OPEN.md, notes/, docs/ or defuqumat/, and no test file that reaches the q route uses vdw_corr (the D2 inputs are PBE, refused at q). The Gamma phonon is right, since there the q = 0 pair Hessian is the correct one.

Fix: Shortest: refuse `calculation.dispersion_sum is not None` by name in require_a_two_sphere_regime when q != 0. Complete: a swap_dispersion beside swap_ewald, which subtracts the Hessian of dispersion_sum.energy and adds the pair sum with e^{iqR} on the off-site and image terms (d2ionq_disp.f90), checked against the Gamma phonon of a doubled cell.

### `defuqumat/workflows/conductivity.py:225`: run_conductivity hands one mean Fermi level to both channels of a two-Fermi-level run, so each channel's Drude delta is built at the wrong energy

Slice `optics`, bug, severity `wrong-answer`, status **confirmed**, known: new (no hit for _fermi_level or the per-channel level in OPEN.md, PLAN.md or the three audits).

optical_conductivity accepts one Fermi level per channel when tot_magnetization fixes the moment, and builds each channel's Drude delta (smeared or tetrahedron) at levels[s]. The workflow never passes that pair. _fermi_level reads statistic['fermi_energy'], and Calculation.occupations sets that key, on a two-Fermi-energy run, to 0.5*(ef_up + ef_dw). QE prints that mean only so the printed value is not NaN (the driver's own comment says so). Calculator.get_optical_conductivity does not pass fermi_energy either. So on an nspin=2 smeared or tetrahedron run with tot_magnetization, both channels' delta(E_F - e) is taken at the mean level. A constrained moment is exactly the case where the two levels differ, so the plasma frequency and the Drude tensor come from the wrong Fermi surface in both channels, with no warning. The scissor 'above' test (conductivity.py:576-580) reads the same mean level.

Trigger: A collinear nspin=2 metal with occupations='smearing' (or 'tetrahedra*') and tot_magnetization set, so two_fermi_energies is True, then Calculator.get_optical_conductivity() with the default intraband=True and method='frequency'. require_a_conductivity_regime refuses only a spiral and an untileable or magnetic wedge, so nothing upstream stops it.

```
workflows/conductivity.py:187-188 `if fermi_energy is None:\n        fermi_energy = _fermi_level(calculation, eigenvalues)`; :236-239 `for key in ("fermi_energy", "homo"): value = statistic.get(key) ... return float(value)`; scf/driver.py:6816-6821 `levels["fermi_energy_up"] = float(ef[0]) ... levels["fermi_energy"] = 0.5 * (float(ef[0]) + float(ef[1]))` (and :6799-6802 for tetrahedra); response/conductivity.py:644-650 `levels = np.broadcast_to(np.asarray(fermi_energy, dtype=float).reshape(-1), (nspin,))` then `_fermi_surface_delta(calculation, float(levels[s]), shifted[s], ...)`; docstring :515-516 `fermi_energy: ... one per channel when a collinear run's moment is fixed.`
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. The path as it stands: workflows/conductivity.py:187-188 calls _fermi_level when fermi_energy is None, and _fermi_level (:225-240) reads only the keys "fermi_energy" and "homo" and returns float(value). On a two-level run Calculation.occupations sets "fermi_energy" to 0.5*(ef_up+ef_dw), at scf/driver.py:6816-6821 for a smearing and :6799-6802 for tetrahedra, with the per-channel keys beside it. optical_conductivity then broadcasts that one number to both channels (response/conductivity.py:646) and builds each channel's delta at it (:647-649; the smeared branch is x = (fermi_energy - energies)/degauss at :1072). The scissor mask reads the same number (:576-580). Calculator.get_optical_conductivity (calculator.py:1517-1533) passes no fermi_energy, and _call_options forwards only ns, tau, becsum, field and field_scale (_STATE_ARGUMENTS, calculator.py:356-362). The defect is bounded: the interband weights come from calculation.occupations(shifted) at response/conductivity.py:612, which uses the per-channel counts, so only the Drude term (plasma frequency, Drude tensor) and the scissor mask are wrong. Upstream guards: require_a_conductivity_regime (:409-433) refuses a spiral and what _wedge refuses, nothing about a fixed moment; fixed_density_hamiltonians (nscf.py:357) refuses only when calculation.magnetic_field is set, and tot_magnetization is two Fermi levels, not a field. The siblings show the claim is right: tddft/chi0.py:1054-1057 reads fermi_energy_up/down for the same delta, nesting was repaired for exactly this in notes/incompat/I19.md:69-72 ("read nscf.fermi_energy, which on a two_fermi_energies run is QE's printed mean"), and the KPM conductivity takes the pair (docs/guide/optics.md:404, :581). The conductivity workflow is the one left behind. No test covers it: tests/regression/test_spin_channels.py runs the conductivity at tot_magnetization = 0 with fixed occupations only. The guide's conductivity section also tells the reader to pass fermi_energy=scf.fermi_energy on a denser grid (optics.md, about line 239 to 240 of the file), which is the mean on such a run. Trigger stated on a whole grid (nosym) because I did not read wedge_group's magnetic-group refusal.

Fix: Make _fermi_level return (fermi_energy_up, fermi_energy_down) when the statistic carries them, as tddft/chi0.py:1054 does, and drop the float; refuse a scalar fermi_energy= on a two_fermi_energies run as run_nesting does (I19). Add a test that the workflow's Drude tensor on an nspin=2 smeared run with tot_magnetization equals optical_conductivity called with the explicit pair, and correct the guide sentence.

### `defuqumat/scf/tetrahedra.py:372`: The tetrahedron clamp is on products of energy differences, so it fires on a live branch once a band spans under about 1e-4 Ry across one tetrahedron

Slice `scf-small`, bug, severity `wrong-answer (narrow: only a band that is flat inside one tetrahedron; the Fermi level error is bounded by that span, under about 1e-4 Ry, but the count, the weights and the DOS weight of that band are wrong at order one)`, status **confirmed**, known: new: grepped `_positive`, `_DEGENERATE` and 'flat band' against OPEN.md, PLAN.md and the three audits. OPEN.md:812-830 (B2) and AUDIT-2026-09-20:1388 cover only `_average_degenerate`'s weight conservation..

`_positive` floors its argument at `_DEGENERATE = 1e-12`. In `_occupied_fraction` and `_bloechl_weights` it is applied to products of two or three corner-energy differences, not to each difference. A product of three differences falls below 1e-12 once each difference is under about 1e-4 Ry, while every difference is still positive and the branch is live. Then the clamped denominator replaces the true one. The docstring at :344 says the opposite ("it is exact because the clamp never fires on a live branch"); that holds only for single differences. I worked one case by hand: e = (0, 1e-5, 2e-5, 3e-5) Ry and E = 0.9e-5 Ry. The first branch then gives 7.29e-16 / 1e-12 = 7.3e-4, where the true fraction is 7.29e-16 / 6e-15 = 0.1215. As a result `integrated_states` becomes discontinuous at the branch boundaries, so the Fermi level bisected in `_bisect` solves the wrong count. `tetrahedron_dos`, which is `jax.grad` of this function, loses the weight of the jump, so the sum rule int D = N that the docstring at :356-362 says holds by construction does not hold there. The two kinds are hurt differently. For the linear and optimized kinds, `_linear_weights` guards each difference separately (`jnp.abs(delta) < _DEGENERATE`), so its weights are right at the Fermi level they are given; but that level came from the clamped count, so `sum(wg)` misses `nelec`. I did not check whether the driver at driver.py:6793 renormalises the weights or tests them against `nelec`. For Bloechl, :528, :546-555 and :575 use the same product clamp, so the weights agree with the wrong count but are wrong themselves. A fix is to clamp each factor, as `_linear_weights` does. On a live branch that is exact, because the variable in the numerator is bounded by the smallest factor. I found this by reading the code and did not measure it.

Trigger: Any band that crosses E_F (in the SCF) or the DOS window (in `get_dos` with tetrahedra) and whose corner energies span less than about 1e-4 Ry (1.4 meV) inside one tetrahedron, with `occupations = 'tetrahedra'`, `'tetrahedra_lin'` or `'tetrahedra_opt'`. Examples are a molecule or point defect in a supercell with vacuum on a 2x2x2 or 4x4x4 mesh, where the localized level disperses by about 1e-6 Ry, or a 4f or moire flat band on a dense mesh. For Kawamura's method the 20-point combinations are what is compared, which does not change the scale.

```
tetrahedra.py:346 `return jnp.where(x > _DEGENERATE, x, _DEGENERATE)` with :91 `_DEGENERATE = 1.0e-12`; :372 `first = above1**3 / _positive((e2 - e1) * (e3 - e1) * (e4 - e1))`; :381 `third = 1.0 - below4**3 / _positive((e4 - e1) * (e4 - e2) * (e4 - e3))`; :378-379 two-factor products; :528 `d4 = _positive((e4 - e1) * (e4 - e2) * (e4 - e3))`; :575 `d1 = _positive((e2 - e1) * (e3 - e1) * (e4 - e1))`; against :610-614 in `_linear_weights`, which guards each `e_i - e_j` separately
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. tetrahedra.py:346 floors at _DEGENERATE = 1e-12 (:91), and :372 and :381 apply it to a product of three corner differences, :378-379 to products of two. I redid the finder's case by hand: e = (0, 1e-5, 2e-5, 3e-5) Ry gives a triple product of 6e-15, below the floor, so `first` at E = 0.9e-5 is 7.29e-16/1e-12 = 7.3e-4 where the exact value is 0.1215. The branch e1 <= E < e2 is live there, so the docstring at :343-344 ("the clamp never fires on a live branch") is false for products. Two corrections to the claim. In that example the two-factor products are 2e-10 to 6e-10 and are not clamped, so the middle branch is exact, and N(E) jumps upward at e2 (1e-3 to 1/6) and at e3 (5/6 to 0.999); the two-factor clamp needs spans under about 1e-6 Ry, where the fraction becomes a step at e3. N(E) stays monotone, so _bisect (:420-439) still converges, onto a jump. And an ordinary metal with a partial degeneracy (two corners equal, the others far) is not hurt, because the live window is then as narrow as the degenerate difference; all four corners must lie within about 1e-4 Ry. Upstream guards: tetrahedra_for (:303-307) refuses only an explicit k-list; the builder (builder.py:1790) and the driver (driver.py:6787-6803) take any cell, and the driver returns wg and ef with no test of sum(wg) against nelec and no renormalisation. So for the linear and optimized kinds (_linear_weights, :610-614, guarded per difference) the weights are exact at a level solved from the clamped count and sum(wg) misses nelec; for Bloechl (:528, :546-555, :575, same product clamp) the weights follow the wrong count. tetrahedron_dos (:935-938) is value_and_grad of the same function, so the jumps carry no DOS weight: one third of the band's weight is lost in the example. tetrahedron_projected_dos (:1007-1013) uses _linear_weights instead, so the projected and the total DOS disagree in this regime. Records: no hit for `_positive`, `clamp never fires` or `live branch` in OPEN.md, PLAN.md or notes/AUDIT*.md. tests/regression/test_tetrahedra.py runs only aluminium (sum of weights = 3 at :102-105), a dispersive band, so no test would see it. QE has no clamp (its IF/ELSEIF chain evaluates only the selected branch), so pw.x is exact here. Not measured, by the rule of this audit. A valid input that reaches it: an open-shell atom or molecule in a vacuum box, or a deep defect level in a large supercell, with occupations = 'tetrahedra' (or _lin, _opt) on a 2x2x2 automatic mesh and the partially filled level dispersing by less than 1e-4 Ry; for the DOS, a narrow band on a dense NSCF mesh with an energy step below the tetrahedron span.

Fix: Floor each difference before multiplying, `_positive(e2 - e1) * _positive(e3 - e1) * _positive(e4 - e1)` and the same in the two-factor products and in _bloechl_weights. It keeps the dead branches finite for grad and is exact on a live branch down to spans of 1e-12 Ry. Add a test with four corners within 1e-5 Ry against the closed-form cubic, and one that sum(wg) equals nelec there; correct the docstring.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1497`: get_electrostriction pairs a strain response solved on the SCF states with the refined states as primal

Slice `strain`, bug, severity `wrong-answer (size not measured; the part read from the code is the offset class that refined_states quotes at 7e-7 relative, the O(1) multiplet term is argued and needs an A/B)`, status **confirmed**, known: Partly known. notes/AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:371-375 records the options keying of _strain_response, which is now fixed. It calls the get_electrostriction half 'weaker still', a time cost only, and considers just the fresh-calculator case (strain=None). The mismatch between SCF and refined states, the multiplet gauge, and the response_options not reaching the reused response are new..

Calculator.get_electrostriction hands electrostriction() the cached strain response. That response was solved by get_strain_response on result.wavefunctions and result.eigenvalues, the SCF states, without refinement. electrostriction() then takes its primal states from refined_states, a fresh Davidson pass at ethr=1e-13 that starts with a Rayleigh-Ritz step. It reuses the cached dpsi as the state tangent of the third derivative, and with elastic=True also of the elastic constants. It checks only that the route matches (is_host_store) and never checks that the tangent belongs to these states. Inside a degenerate occupied multiplet the refined basis is psi_scf U with U arbitrary, because the in-pair matrix is set by round-off or by the rho_in/rho_out difference (P151 measured O(1) moves under such a rotation). Every band-diagonal pairing in the jvp then reads Tr(U^dagger M) instead of Tr(M). One example is the relaxation term sum_n <dH psi_n|dpsi_n> of C_ijkl. Another is <dpsi_n|u_n> in d(chi)/dx. For a 2x2 rotation by theta that scales the multiplet's share by cos(theta). Even without degeneracy this is the H[rho_in] versus H[rho_out] mismatch that refined_states says gives a systematic offset in a third derivative. The response_options given to get_electrostriction (tr2, alpha_mix) also never reach the reused strain response. I did not measure the size; the mechanism is from reading the code.

Trigger: silicon = Calculator.from_file('si-electrostriction.in', ...); silicon.get_elastic_constants() (or silicon.get_strain_response() for the deformation potentials, as in docs/guide/phonons.md); then silicon.get_electrostriction(). On an unshifted mesh, Gamma's valence triplet and the pairs on the Delta and Lambda lines are degenerate occupied multiplets. A fresh calculator that calls get_electrostriction first is not affected, since it solves the strain response on the refined states.

```
calculator.py:1497 `options.setdefault("strain", self._strain_response)`; calculator.py:1433-1436 `strain_response(self.calculation, result.wavefunctions, result.eigenvalues, result.density, result.becsum, **merged)`; electrostriction.py:1004 `eigenvalues, psi = refined_states(calculation, result, stream=walked)`; electrostriction.py:1037-1046 `if strain is not None and is_host_store(strain.dpsi[0, 0]) != bool(walked): strain = None` / `if strain is None: strain = strain_response(calculation, psi, ...)`; electrostriction.py:1052-1056 tangents `(strain_tangent(k, l), strain.dpsi[k, l], ...)`; electrostriction.py:1102-1105 `elastic_constants(calculation, psi, eigenvalues, density, strain, ...)`
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. What I read: (1) calculator.py:1497 `options.setdefault("strain", self._strain_response)` and _defaults_for returns `{**shared, **(options or {})}` (calculator.py:2896), so the cached object reaches electrostriction() unchanged. (2) The slot is filled at calculator.py:1433-1436 from `result.wavefunctions, result.eigenvalues`, the SCF states; strain.py, chunked_strain.py and elastic.py contain no `refined_states` and no `.diagonalize(` call, so the response is never re-solved on other states. (3) electrostriction.py:1004 takes `eigenvalues, psi = refined_states(...)`, a Davidson pass at REFINE_ETHR on H[rho_out] (electrostriction.py:351-367), and builds the field response b, u on that psi. (4) The only test on the handed-in response is the route, electrostriction.py:1037 `is_host_store(strain.dpsi[0, 0]) != bool(walked)`. For a norm-conserving insulator both entry points decide the route with the same `_streams(calculation, result.wavefunctions, False, whole_k=None)` call (strain.py:321-325; electrostriction.py:992-997 with _walk_lacks returning None at elastic.py:465-470; strain_route returns {} at elastic.py:483-486), so the test passes in every memory mode and the cached response is used. (5) The pairing is band by band: electrostriction.py:867-876 puts `response.dpsi[k, l]` as the tangent of `solver.psi` in `compiled_jvp(epsilon, (zero, psi, rho, b), (tangent, dpsi, drho, db))` and also feeds it to _position_response; elastic.py:299-303 writes the block into `zeros_like(psi).at[:, :, :nocc]`; the walked route does the same at electrostriction.py:1052-1056. StrainResponse (strain.py:164-200) records nothing about the states it was solved on. (6) The trigger is not refused: tests/data/qe/si-electrostriction.in is `nosym`, `noinv`, 2 2 2 0 0 0, so _require_closed_for_elastic passes under the default elastic=True, and ultrasoft and PAW do not reach this path. (7) The response_options of get_electrostriction reach strain_response only inside `if strain is None` (electrostriction.py:1041-1045), so a reused response keeps its own tr2 and alpha_mix. (8) No test covers it: every direct test solves the strain response on refined_states output first (tests/regression/test_electrostriction.py:118-133 and :143, tests/regression/test_dispersion.py:350-356 and :506-513), and get_electrostriction appears in tests only in two source-inspection tests (test_notebook_conventions.py:149, test_state_across_boundaries.py:391). Notebook 21 and docs/guide/piezo.md call get_electrostriction on a fresh calculator, so no published number is affected. The audit of 2026-09-18 (hole.8) looked only at the fresh-calculator case. What is argued and not read: how far the refined basis is from the SCF basis. I did not find where the Davidson pass opens with a Rayleigh-Ritz step, so I do not repeat that as a fact. The argument is that refined_states' own docstring says <psi_m|H[rho_out]|psi_n> is off-diagonal at 1.6e-7 Ry on the SCF states and 3.5e-15 after the pass, so the pass rotates; inside a pair split by less than that (5.3e-7 Ry at conv_thr=1e-9 on a nosym run, calculator.py docstring quoting P151) the rotation is not small. In addition davidson.py:381-382 and :754-756 say that a complex projected matrix gives each eigenvector an arbitrary phase, so on a complex k-point even a nondegenerate band may come back with a phase the cached dpsi does not carry; this would make the error larger than the finder claims, and it is also unmeasured.

Fix: Measure first: on si-electrostriction.in, one calculator with get_strain_response() then get_electrostriction(), against a fresh calculator calling get_electrostriction() alone, same cores, comparing dchi_dstrain and the elastic constants. Then either drop the setdefault at calculator.py:1497 (the cost is one more strain response), or let StrainResponse record which states it was solved on and have electrostriction() solve again when they are not its refined states, the same way it already does for the route; add a facade test of the sequence.

### `defuqumat/workflows/pdos.py:592`: The PDOS tetrahedra still take the rotations alone, from the group that ignores nosym, with time reversal forced on

Slice `hubbard-projwfc`, bug, severity `wrong-answer`, status **confirmed**, known: notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1529 names pdos.py:590 as a sibling to route, with the same two omissions. What is new: the fix recorded at AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:165 and PLAN.md P8 reached tetrahedra_for and the SCF only. git log -L shows _projection_tetrahedra untouched since 94bf0bfb (2026-08-21), and it is in neither OPEN.md nor PLAN.md as open. A second point not in the audit line: the group passed is calculation.symmetries, so under nosym the rotations are the whole crystal group and not the identity..

_projection_tetrahedra builds the PDOS tetrahedra with symmetries.rotation_array() and nothing else. So build_tetrahedra falls back to time_reversal=True and t_rev=None. The group it receives is calculation.symmetries, which is the whole detected group even under nosym (driver.py:2992 sets it from system.symmetry_group() with no nosym argument, and :3005 keeps nosym only in use_symmetry). The corners of the full grid are then folded onto representatives of a group the k-set was not reduced with. This is the same call shape that tetrahedra_for had before the P8 fix. The audit measured 32 of 64 corners sent to the wrong representative under nosym, and 55 of 64 under noinv on AlAs. Those numbers are the audit's and were not measured here. Every index is valid, so nothing raises. What moves is pdos, integrated and the total DOS of the ProjectedDOS. The Loewdin charges come from wg and are not affected. run_dos does it right: it passes system.grid_symmetry() to tetrahedra_for (dos.py:480 to 481). project_states does not.

Trigger: Any tetrahedron occupation (tetrahedra, tetrahedra_lin or tetrahedra_opt) together with nosym = .true., for example al-tetrahedra.in with nosym, then Calculator.get_pdos() at the default scheme. Also noinv = .true. on a cell without inversion (zincblende), and a magnetic noncollinear run whose grid has time reversal off and t_rev operations. The same holds on a denser grid (get_pdos(grid=...)), where denser_grid reduces with grid_symmetry() and the tetrahedra fold with the other group.

```
defuqumat/workflows/pdos.py:592-598
    return build_tetrahedra(
        kind,
        kpoints.grid,
        kpoints.shift or (0, 0, 0),
        symmetries.rotation_array(),
        np.asarray(cell.bg_2pi_alat),
        precision=kpoints.precision,
    )
called at pdos.py:640 as _projection_tetrahedra(scheme, system.kpoints, calculation.symmetries, system.cell)
driver.py:2992  self.symmetries = system.symmetry_group()
driver.py:3005  self.use_symmetry = bool(not system.nosym and self.symmetries.nsym > 1)
```

Skeptic: I read the path and found no guard. defuqumat/workflows/pdos.py:592-598 calls build_tetrahedra with symmetries.rotation_array() only, so defuqumat/scf/tetrahedra.py:226-227 keeps time_reversal=True and t_rev=None, and :250 calls grid_equivalence with that triple. The caller at pdos.py:640-642 passes calculation.symmetries, which defuqumat/scf/driver.py:2992 sets from system.symmetry_group() with no nosym argument; :3005 keeps nosym only in use_symmetry. The k-set is built with another triple: defuqumat/system/builder.py:951-955 (grid_symmetry) gives rotations None under nosym, time reversal off under noinv or for a magnetic noncollinear run, and t_rev. So under nosym the eigenvalues have the rows of the complete grid, and the corners are indices into the wedge list of the whole crystal group with time reversal. Nothing downstream tests the corners against nk: compute_pdos (pdos.py:505-545) passes the tetrahedra to the scheme and to compute_dos, and my grep for a range check on corners in pdos.py and scf/tetrahedra.py found none. The indices are valid, so the result is a wrong pdos, integrated and total, with no error. The sibling does it right: defuqumat/workflows/dos.py:482-484 passes system.grid_symmetry() to tetrahedra_for, and tetrahedra.py:308-313 maps None rotations to the identity with no time reversal. No upstream refusal: Calculator.get_pdos (calculator.py:1102 onward) delegates to run_pdos, and run_pdos to project_states, with no nosym, noinv or tetrahedron guard. The unit test tests/unit/test_projwfc.py:41-91 builds its tetrahedra on a symmetric grid only. One correction to the finder: the 32 of 64 and 55 of 64 numbers are the docstring of tetrahedra_for (tetrahedra.py:290-293) for the identity with time reversal and for noinv. With the whole group passed under nosym the mismatch is larger than that case (the wedge list is much shorter than the full grid). The unpolarized symmetric run and the collinear run without noinv are not affected, since there the triple equals the default. Recorded but not fixed: notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1529 names pdos.py:590 as a sibling, the fix row for workflows/dos.py:453 in the same note covers tetrahedra_for and the SCF only, and OPEN.md has no entry for it (grep on tetra with pdos or project).

Fix: Give _projection_tetrahedra the triple of system.grid_symmetry(), with None rotations read as the identity and time reversal off, exactly as tetrahedra_for does (or call tetrahedra_for with the kind already promoted to linear). Add a test that the PDOS summed over a complete set equals run_dos under nosym and under noinv on AlAs, and a guard that corners.max() < nk.

### `defuqumat/workflows/transport.py:823`: The incoherent vertical map rotates its multiplets at the 1e-6 constant, not at the resolved degeneracy_tol

Slice `transport-stm`, bug, severity `wrong-answer on a diagnostic (incoherent, interference, band_edge_weight); the coherent map T is not affected`, status **confirmed**, known: new. PLAN.md P149 (table at line 26084) records the transport/green.py site as resolved per branch. The missing tol= in the four incoherent calls is not recorded in OPEN.md or in the three audits..

P149 resolves degeneracy_tol per branch, to 1e-5 or more on the SCF's own states. _assemble passes that value to the band window (:744), the top-multiplet mask (:753) and _diagnose (:772). The incoherent sums do not get it. They call transmission and spin_transmission with coherent=False and no tol, so channel_basis runs at green.DEGENERACY_TOL = 1e-6. As a result, a pair that the solver split by between 1e-6 and 1e-5 Ry is one block for the window and the mask, but two blocks for the rotation. The incoherent map is then a diagonal taken in the eigensolver's own basis inside that pair, which is rule D4. VerticalTransport.incoherent and .interference depend on that basis, and so does notes['band_edge_weight'] = top_band / total_incoherent. The _top_multiplet_mask docstring says the mask and the rotation always agree about where the block is. That holds only when the tolerance is 1e-6. A degeneracy_tol= the caller passes is also ignored by the incoherent map, with no warning.

Trigger: run_vertical_transport(..., incoherent=True) on the SCF's own states (no grid=, so the resolved tol is at least the 1e-5 empty-band floor), with a degenerate multiplet in the energy window whose solver split lies between 1e-6 and 1e-5 Ry. P149 item 3 measured such a margin. Also any explicit degeneracy_tol other than 1e-6, on either branch, and the wedge route _completed_on_a_wedge, which goes through the same _assemble.

```
defuqumat/workflows/transport.py:823-828
    total_incoherent[ie, here] += scale * transmission(
        amplitudes[component], overlaps, weight_of_k,
        weights, coherent=False, eigenvalues=in_window)
    top_band[ie, here] += scale * transmission(
        amplitudes[component], overlaps, weight_of_k,
        weights * cut, coherent=False,
        eigenvalues=in_window)
and the same at :809-816 for spin_transmission. Compare windows = _band_window(..., tol=degeneracy_tol) at :743-744 and _top_multiplet_mask(eigenvalues[ispin], degeneracy_tol) at :753. green.py:286 has transmission(..., eigenvalues=None, tol: float = DEGENERACY_TOL).
```

Skeptic: I read the path and it stands. run_vertical_transport resolves the tolerance at workflows/transport.py:282-283 and hands it to _assemble (:290). Inside _assemble it reaches _band_window (:743-744), _top_multiplet_mask (:753) and _diagnose (:770-772). The four incoherent calls at :809-816 and :823-828 pass eigenvalues=in_window and no tol, and green.py:286-287 and :354-356 default tol to DEGENERACY_TOL = 1e-6 (green.py:211), so channel_basis (green.py:344, :443) groups at 1e-6. No guard upstream removes the case: incoherent=True is the default (:111), the SCF branch resolves to at least the 1e-5 empty-band floor (_resolved_degeneracy_tol :611-628, davidson.py:290-295), and _completed_on_a_wedge receives the same assemble dict (:297-299). The certain trigger is an explicit degeneracy_tol: degeneracy_tolerance returns it unchanged (davidson.py:311-327), so degeneracy_tol=1e-4 moves the window and the mask and leaves the rotation at 1e-6, with no warning. The default trigger needs a pair split between 1e-6 and 1e-5 on the SCF states; PLAN.md P149 only measured a split 3e-7 under 1e-6, so that half is unmeasured. One limit on the claim: at a multiplet that is irreducible under the operations that keep the exit plane, S restricted to it is a multiple of the identity (channel_basis docstring, green.py:231-236), so the missing rotation changes nothing there. It matters for pairs degenerate by an operation that does not keep the plane, for example the PT pairs of a spinor run on a centrosymmetric slab, which are degenerate at every k. The test tests/unit/test_degeneracy_tolerance.py:66-78 checks multiplets() at the resolved value only, not that _assemble forwards it, so it does not show the claim false. Not in OPEN.md or the audits (AUDIT-2026-09-20:1521 says no D4 diagonal survives in transport, written before P149).

Fix: Pass tol=degeneracy_tol in the four coherent=False calls of _assemble (transmission and spin_transmission, both total_incoherent and top_band), and add a test that calls _assemble with a pair split by 3e-6 and two different solver bases and asserts the incoherent map does not move.

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:1809`: default_nbnd rounds half to even and drops nelup/neldw outside nspin = 2, so it gives fewer bands than pw.x

Slice `driver-a`, bug, severity `crash (narrow, a clean ValueError and not a silent number) for a spinor at fixed occupations; minor for the smeared band count and for the sizing estimate`, status **confirmed**, known: new. notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1376 lists default_nbnd as checked and faithful to setup.f90, including the noncollinear doubling, which this contradicts. PLAN.md:6895 records the affected count (16 as a spinor) as if it were pw.x's. PLAN.md:529 records the same NINT-against-round trap for the Monkhorst-Pack fold, and occupations.py already has _nint for it..

The docstring says this is QE's default band count from setup.f90, and the 2026-09-20 audit lists it as faithful. Two things break it. First, Python round is half to even, where setup.f90:375 uses NINT, so round(4.5) gives 4 and NINT(4.5) gives 5. Second, setup.f90:367 always calls set_nelup_neldw and takes the MAX with NINT(nelup) and NINT(neldw), with nelup = INT(nelec+1)/2 when unconstrained. The two call sites (driver.py:4405 in band_count and :8369 in run_scf) pass (None, None) unless nspin == 2. Either fix alone is enough: _nint from occupations.py, or passing nelup and neldw in every regime. When nelec is 1, 5, 9, 13 and so on, nspin = 1 gets one band fewer than pw.x and a spinor gets two fewer. With smearing the run still works, but the band count differs from pw.x's. For a spinor with nelec = 9: here 2 x max(round(5.4)=5, round(4.5)+4=8) = 16, and pw.x gives 2 x max(NINT(5.4)=5, NINT(6.0)=6, NINT(4.8)=5, 5+4=9) = 18. For nelec = 5: 6 against 7 at nspin = 1, and 12 against 14 as a spinor. The crash case is a spinor with occupations = 'fixed' and nelec = 5, 9 or 13, with no nbnd given. The default is then 2 x max(round(nelec/2), 1) = nelec - 1 bands, and occupations.py:96-97 raises 'need 5 bands but only 4 were computed'. pw.x takes 2 x NINT(2.5) = 6 bands and runs. One hop is inferred, not read: that run_scf passes degeneracy 1 for a spinor to fixed_occupations. occupied_counts (occupations.py, nspin != 2 branch) says a spinor fills nelec bands, and that supports it. There is a third face. sizing.py:650-656 restates the rule with nelup/neldw only when tot_magnetization is set. At nspin = 2 with nelec = 9 and no tot_magnetization, the estimate therefore sizes 8 bands, and the run takes 9 through band_count, which does pass nelup = 5 and neldw = 4.

Trigger: Any run without an explicit nbnd where nelec is 1 mod 4 and below about 40. One verified case is the cobalt atom as a spinor (nelec = 9, smearing), which PLAN.md:6895 records at default_nbnd = 16, where pw.x takes 18. The crash case is a noncollinear single N, P or Sb atom, or an Ir or Co atom with 9 valence electrons, under occupations = 'fixed'. It gets nelec - 1 bands and raises at the first filling.

```
defuqumat/scf/driver.py:1809  occupied = int(round(nelec / 2.0))
defuqumat/scf/driver.py:1813-1814  if occupations == "fixed": return degeneracy * max(occupied, 1)
defuqumat/scf/driver.py:4405-4409  return nbnd or system.nbnd or default_nbnd(self.nelec, system.occupations, *((self.nelup, self.neldw) if system.nspin == 2 else (None, None)), noncolin=system.noncolin)
qe-7.5 PW/src/setup.f90:367  call set_nelup_neldw ( tot_magnetization, nelec, nelup, neldw )
qe-7.5 PW/src/setup.f90:375  nbnd = MAX ( NINT( nelec / degspin ), NINT(nelup), NINT(neldw) )
defuqumat/scf/occupations.py:96-97  if occupied > nbnd: raise ValueError(f"{nelec} electrons need {occupied} bands but only {nbnd} were computed")
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. The rule in pw.x is setup.f90:367-390: set_nelup_neldw is called in every regime, nbnd = MAX(NINT(nelec/degspin), NINT(nelup), NINT(neldw)), the metallic line adds NINT(1.2*nelup) and NINT(1.2*neldw), and the spinor doubling comes last. constants.f90:106 makes degspin a PARAMETER equal to 2, and electrons_base.f90:389-391 gives nelup = INT(nelec+1)/2 when tot_magnetization is not set. Here driver.py:1809 takes int(round(nelec/2.0)), which is half to even, and driver.py:4405-4409 and 8369-8374 pass (None, None) unless nspin == 2, so at nspin 1 and 4 the nelup term that would repair the half is absent. For nelec = 5, 9, 13 the count is one band short (two as a spinor). The crash path is read in full: default_nbnd returns 2*max(round(nelec/2), 1) = nelec - 1 at driver.py:1813-1814; Calculation.occupations sets degeneracy = 1 if self.noncolin else 2 (driver.py:6745, the hop the finder inferred) and calls fixed_occupations with counts=None at 6749; occupations.py:91-97 then has occupied = nelec > nbnd and raises. No upstream guard stops it: builder.py:635 and _check_occupations (builder.py:1822 onwards) refuse fixed occupations only for LSDA without an integer tot_magnetization, and tests/data/qe/h-chain-spiral-fixed.in shows that fixed with noncolin is an accepted regime (it gives nbnd = 4, so it never reaches the default). pw.x takes 2*NINT(2.5) = 6 bands and iweights.f90:105-117 fills NINT(nelec) of them, so it runs. The smeared face is also real: for nelec = 9 as a spinor, here 2*max(round(5.4)=5, 4+4) = 16 and pw.x 2*max(5, 6, 5, 5+4) = 18. The sizing face is real from sizing.py:648-656: at nspin = 2, nelec = 9 and no tot_magnetization it gives 8, and the run gives 9 because Calculation.nelup is always computed (driver.py:2585). Nothing records it: OPEN.md and tests have no entry, notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1376 lists default_nbnd as faithful, and PLAN.md:529 has the NINT trap only for the Monkhorst-Pack fold. Three corrections to the finder. (1) 'Either fix alone is enough' is false: _nint alone leaves the NINT(1.2*nelup) term missing, which gives 27 against 28 at nelec = 45 and 28 against 29 at nelec = 47 (nspin = 1, smearing), so the trigger is not only nelec = 1 mod 4 below 40 (see N1). And passing the counts alone as the code stands loses the spinor doubling, because the return at driver.py:1815-1821 has no degeneracy factor. (2) nelec = 1 is short only under smearing (4 against 5); at fixed occupations max(occupied, 1) saves it. (3) PLAN.md:6895 records what default_nbnd returns (9 collinear, 16 as a spinor) and does not say 16 is the count of pw.x; the collinear 9 matches pw.x and only the spinor leg is off.

Fix: Three changes together in default_nbnd and its callers: use _nint (occupations.py:107) in place of int(round(...)) everywhere, pass calculation.nelup and calculation.neldw in every regime (at nspin 1 and 4 they are already the unconstrained INT(nelec+1)/2 split), and multiply the return of the nelup branch (driver.py:1815-1821) by degeneracy. Then make sizing.py:648-656, ultracell/driver.py:776-781 and workflows/topology.py:349 use the same counts, and add a test on nelec = 5, 9, 45, 47 against the hand values of setup.f90.

### `defuqumat/magnetic/peierls.py:458`: On a left-handed cell the field is along -a3, and the plaquette guard then raises after every solve, blaming the Peierls sign

Slice `magnetic`, bug, severity `crash`, status **confirmed**, known: new (no 'left-handed' in OPEN.md, the audits, system/ or io/).

With a(r) = 2 pi n_phi (b1.r) b2, curl a = 2 pi n_phi b1 x b2 = 2 pi n_phi a3/det(at), where det(at) is signed, while Cell.volume and area use abs(det). The field is therefore along a1 x a2 always, which is -a3 when det(at) < 0. signed_area carries orientation = sign((a1 x a2).a3) = -1, so plaquette_check expects exp(+i 2 pi n_phi) for the loop 0, a1, a1+a2, a2. The hops give exp(-i 2 pi n_phi), because the line integral around that loop is 2 pi n_phi whatever the handedness. The check therefore raises with the message 'the sign of the Peierls phase is not the electron's'. run_landau_levels calls it at landau.py:226, after all the solves. Any Wannier set whose orbitals have self-hops at R = +-a1 and +-a2 reaches it, which is any set at cutoff = 0. Nothing in system/ or io/ refuses or reorders a left-handed cell, and pw.x accepts one. The same orientation means that route P's Chern numbers on such a cell are for the field along -a3. That second point was not verified by a run. Fix: refuse det(at) < 0 in MagneticCell.build, or define the field along a1 x a2 and drop the orientation factor in signed_area. Then say in the docs which one applies.

Trigger: A hexagonal cell written with a1 and a2 swapped, for example ibrav = 0 with a1 = (a/2, a sqrt(3)/2, 0), a2 = (a, 0, 0), a3 = (0, 0, c), then get_wannier and get_landau_levels(B=10). |product - expected| = 2 |sin(2 pi/q)| is about 1.6e-3 at q = 7900, far above tol = 1e-10.

```
conventions.py:129-133 `def a(self, r): ... return (TPI * self.flux * self.scale) * (r @ b[0])[..., None] * b[1]` with `b_chem = jnp.linalg.inv(self.cell.at).T`; conventions.py:208-209 `orientation = np.sign(np.dot(np.cross(lattice[0], lattice[1]), lattice[2]))` / `return float(0.5 * np.sum(...) * orientation)`; peierls.py:455-462 `expected = complex(np.exp(-1j * TPI * cell.flux * float(cell.scale) * area))` / `if abs(product - expected) > tol: raise ValueError(...)`; landau.py:226 `plaquette = _first_plaquette(cell, bonds) if bonds.nw else None`; system/cell.py:302-303 `def _volume(at): return jnp.abs(jnp.linalg.det(at))`
```

Skeptic: I followed the algebra in the code. MagneticCell.a is 2 pi n_phi (b1.r) b2 with b = inv(at).T, so curl a = 2 pi n_phi b1 x b2 = 2 pi n_phi a3/det(at) with the signed determinant: the field is along a1 x a2, not along a3. peierls_phase is -a(mid).(r_to - r_from), and around the loop 0, a1, a1+a2, a2 only the hop at x = 1 contributes, so the product is exp(-i 2 pi n_phi) for either handedness. signed_area (conventions.py:194-195) multiplies the shoelace area by sign((a1 x a2).a3), which is -1 on a left-handed cell, so plaquette_check expects exp(+i 2 pi n_phi) and raises with the message about the sign of the Peierls phase (peierls.py, the `abs(product - expected) > tol` branch). run_landau_levels calls _first_plaquette at workflows/landau.py:226, after every solve, whenever one orbital has self-hops at +-a1 and +-a2, which a graphene p_z set has at cutoff = 0. No upstream guard exists: Cell.from_vectors (system/cell.py:339-358) checks only the shape and zero lengths, _volume takes abs(det) (cell.py:303), and a grep for a handedness check in system/, io/, OPEN.md, PLAN.md and the audit found none. The only test of signed_area (tests/unit/test_magnetic_conventions.py:113-114) uses a right-handed cell. A natural trigger is a hexagonal cell written as a1 = (a, 0, 0), a2 = (a/2, -a sqrt(3)/2, 0), a3 = (0, 0, c). The error text of MagneticCell.build (conventions.py:82-83) makes it worse: it says that a field along -a3 is the cell with a3 reversed, but reversing a3 leaves b1 and b2 unchanged, so a(r) and the field in space do not change, and the reversed cell is the left-handed one that raises. This was read and not run. Whether route P's Chern sign follows the same orientation was not checked.

Fix: Refuse det(at) < 0 by name in MagneticCell.build, or drop the orientation factor in signed_area and document the field as along a1 x a2. Correct the 'a3 reversed' sentence, and run the plaquette check before the solves.

### `defuqumat/system/symmetry.py:153`: fft_factors takes a near-zero translation component of about 1e-6 as a denominator of 1,000,000, while the search accepted it as zero at 1e-5

Slice `system`, bug, severity `crash`, status **confirmed**, known: new. notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:164 and :578-584 moved _crystallographic_translation and _maps_structure to 1e-5. That entry also names a zero test at 1e-6 inside the filter, which was fixed. The zero test inside Symmetries.fft_factors stayed at _TOLERANCE, and the stored translation is not snapped. Neither is recorded in OPEN.md, PLAN.md or the three audits..

The search accepts a translation component as zero when its residue is below _POSITION_TOLERANCE = 1e-5 (_crystallographic_translation, _maps_structure). It stores the candidate as np.round(candidate, 10) % 1.0 (symmetry.py:363) without snapping that residue to zero. fft_factors then tests for zero against the ten-times-tighter _TOLERANCE = 1e-6. A residue of -1e-6 is stored as fl(0.999999), so min(v, 1 - v) = 1.0000000000268e-06, just above 1e-6. A residue of +1e-6 arrives as 1.000001 % 1.0 = 1.00000008e-06. In both cases n = round(1/v) = 1000000, and |1/v - n| is 2.7e-5 or 8.3e-5, inside the 1e-4 test that is meant to catch a non-fraction. So factors[axis] becomes lcm(..., 1000000), and a residue of 2e-6 would give 500000. QE's fft_fact (symm_base.f90:580-586) uses eps2 = 1e-5 for the same zero test and sets nfrac = 0, so pw.x adds no factor there. _fft_factors (basis/builder.py:149-155) and sizing.py:612 consume this without a guard. good_fft_order(2n+1, 1000000) then returns 1,000,000 points along that hexagonal axis, and building that box fails. The symmorphic property (line 132) has the same mismatch, so it reports such a group as non-symmorphic. The committed co-hcp test (tests/unit/test_symmetry.py:170-200) cannot see this, because its first atom is at the origin. There rotated[0] = 0 for every rotation, the candidates are exact positions and no near-zero residue appears. I derived this by hand from the code and the IEEE arithmetic. I did not run it, because the audit allowed no Python.

Trigger: An hcp metal (Mg, Ti, Zn, Co) or wurtzite in the standard Wyckoff 2c setting with six-decimal thirds, ibrav = 4, `ATOMIC_POSITIONS crystal` with `X 0.333333 0.666667 0.25` and `X 0.666667 0.333333 0.75`, through Calculator.from_file or from_keywords with these numbers. With c' = c M and M = [[0,-1,0],[1,1,0],[0,0,1]], the 6_3 screw sends atom 0 to (0.666667, 0.333334, 0.25). Its accepted candidate is (0, -1e-6, 0.5), because atom 1 lands within 2e-6 of atom 0, which is inside the 1e-5 match. It is stored as (0, 0.999999, 0.5), so fft_factors()[1] = 1000000. The opposite rotation gives (1e-6, 0, 0.5) and the same factor on axis 0. No upstream guard refuses the input: build_basis calls _fft_factors unless nosym is set.

```
symmetry.py:150-162: `value = min(abs(value), abs(1.0 - value))` / `if value <= _TOLERANCE: continue` / `n = int(round(1.0 / value))` / `if n and abs(1.0 / value - n) < 1.0e-4: factors[axis] = _lcm(factors[axis], n)`. symmetry.py:56 `_TOLERANCE = 1.0e-6` against symmetry.py:76 `_POSITION_TOLERANCE = 1.0e-5`. symmetry.py:1053-1054 `if abs(residue) < _POSITION_TOLERANCE: continue` (accepts a residue of 1e-6 as zero). symmetry.py:363 `translations.append(np.round(candidate, 10) % 1.0)` (keeps the residue). symm_base.f90:580-586 `IF ( ABS (ft_(i)) > eps2 ) THEN nfrac = NINT(1.0_dp/ABS(ft_(i))) ELSE nfrac = 0`.
```

Skeptic: The path is real at commit b5b88c5c, and the trigger is wider than the finder says. I ran nothing; every step below is read from the code or done by hand in integer arithmetic.

Code path. `_search` (symmetry.py:351-364) tries each candidate `positions[target] - rotated[0]` (`_candidate_translations`, :1063-1066). It accepts a component as zero when `abs(residue) < _POSITION_TOLERANCE` = 1e-5 (:1053-1054), matches atoms at 1e-5 (`_maps_structure`, :1069-1075), and stores `np.round(candidate, 10) % 1.0` (:363) without snapping the residue. Unlike QE it has no zero-translation first attempt (symm_base.f90:538-542 tries `ft_ = 0` first and keeps ft = 0 exactly). `Symmetries.fft_factors` (:147-161) then tests zero with `value <= _TOLERANCE` = 1e-6 (:56, :151) and takes any `n = round(1/value)` with `abs(1/value - n) < 1e-4` (:153-157), with no limit to the denominators 2, 3, 4, 6. QE's own factor loop (symm_base.f90:580-586) uses eps2 = 1e-5 and sets nfrac = 0.

The last bit, by hand. A residue of -1e-6 is stored as fl(0.999999). 0.999999 x 2^53 = 9007190247541737.259, which rounds down, so 1 - v = 9007199255 x 2^-53 = 1e-6 + 2.9e-17, and fl(1e-6) is within 1e-22 of 1e-6. So the `<=` test does not skip it, 1/v = 1e6 - 2.9e-5, and n = 1000000 passes the 1e-4 test. This half of the claim holds (the exact value is 1.0000000000288e-06, not the finder's ...268e-06).

What is wrong in the finder's account. A residue of +1e-6 does not trigger. As 1.000001 % 1.0 it is 4503599627 x 2^-52 = 1e-6 - 8.2e-17, below the tolerance, and a bare +1e-6 rounds to fl(1e-6), equal to the tolerance; both are skipped. The figure 1.00000008e-06 is not a possible value, and the sentence that the opposite sixfold rotation gives the same factor on axis 0 is false: that rotation gives (1.000001, 0, 0.5), which is skipped.

Axis 0 gets the factor from another operation, and the screw is not needed. With atom 0 at (0.333333, 0.666667, z), the threefold rotation M^2 = [[-1,-1,0],[1,0,0],[0,0,1]] gives rotated[0] = (0.333334, -0.333333, z). The target-0 candidate is (-1e-6, 1.0, 0), it passes the filter, the atom at (0.666667, 0.333333) lands 2e-6 from itself, and (0.999999, 0, 0) is stored. The sixfold case of the finder, (0, -1e-6, 0.5) stored as (0, 0.999999, 0.5), I traced too and it holds.
