So both hexagonal axes carry 1,000,000, for any hexagonal cell with a threefold axis whose first atom sits at six-decimal thirds: hcp in the 2c setting, wurtzite, and also graphene, hBN or a MoS2 monolayer written with atom 0 at (0.333333, 0.666667). Truncated thirds (0.333333, 0.666666) reach it as well. pw.x takes ft = 0 for these operations and adds no factor.

Guards. `_fft_factors` (basis/builder.py:149-154) has only the `nosym` guard, sizing.py:605-612 the same, and `good_fft_order` (fftgrid.py:50-55) has no upper bound, where QE's stops at nfftx. 1,000,000 = 2^6 5^6 is an allowed size, so the loop returns it. The failure point is `generate_gvectors` (basis/gvectors.py:178-180), which builds `np.meshgrid` over the whole box: with both axes at 1e6 that is 1e12 x nr3 integers, a MemoryError before any physics. `from_file` does not snap positions (the co-hcp docstring at symmetry.py:66-72 shows six-decimal values arrive as written).

Not recorded and not exercised. The only committed inputs with six-decimal thirds are tests/data/qe/co-hcp-anisotropy-{sr,soc}.in and tests/unit/test_symmetry.py:159, :310, and all have atom 0 at the origin, so rotated[0] = 0 and no residue appears; test_symmetry.py:198 asserts (3, 3, 2) only for that cell. OPEN.md has no entry, and PLAN.md and the three audits name `fft_factors` only for the lost factor of 3 (AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:583-584). Seven-decimal or longer thirds are safe (residue 1e-7 is under 1e-6; twelve decimals round to zero), which is why the bismuthene inputs never showed it.

The `symmorphic` property (:131-132) has the same mismatch, but no caller in defuqumat/ reads it, so that part is inert.

Fix: In `Symmetries.fft_factors`, test zero with `_POSITION_TOLERANCE` (QE's eps2, symm_base.f90:581) instead of `_TOLERANCE`, and accept only n in `_CRYSTALLOGRAPHIC_DENOMINATORS`; use the same tolerance in `symmorphic`. The QE-faithful companion is a zero-translation first attempt in `_search` before the candidates (symm_base.f90:538-542), so a symmorphic operation stores ft = 0 exactly; add a test with atom 0 at (0.333333, 0.666667, 0.25) asserting fft_factors == (1, 1, 2), and a bound in `good_fft_order` as QE's nfftx.

### `defuqumat/workflows/vc_relax.py:470`: vc-relax checks the detected group even under nosym, which run_relax was fixed not to do

Slice `forces`, bug, severity `crash (a valid nosym vc-relax stops with a RuntimeError that blames a bug)`, status **confirmed**, known: new for vc_relax. The same defect was fixed in run_relax only (PLAN.md about line 13266, "`run_relax` checks the group it applies, not the one the crystal has"), and vc_relax.py is not mentioned there..

`Calculation.symmetries` is the full detected group whatever `nosym` says (driver.py:2992, with `use_symmetry` as the switch). run_relax guards its `checkallsym` with `calculation.use_symmetry` because, as PLAN.md about line 13266 records, checking the unused group refused valid steps under nosym. vc_relax's `_check_geometry` has no such guard. It runs `check_lattice_symmetry` and `check_symmetry` against `previous.symmetries` on every step, on both the frozen-basis and the `treinit_gvectors` paths. Under nosym the forces and the stress are not symmetrised (forces/__init__.py, stress `_symmetrised`), so the step can leave the detected group. Then the run raises RuntimeError with a message that blames "a bug or a cell whose symmetry was mis-detected". pw.x under nosym has nsym=1 and runs on. The clearest case breaks the group by construction: `cell_dofree='z'` (or 'x', 'xy', 'fixc', 'epitaxial_*') on a cubic cell stretches one axis, which breaks the cubic group at the first cell step. In both codes you set nosym to allow that (with symmetry on, pw.x's checkallsym stops too), and here nosym does not help.

Trigger: `calculation='vc-relax'`, `nosym=.true.`, `cell_dofree='z'` on cubic silicon (any input not at its equilibrium c). Another trigger is PLAN's adsorbate-on-a-slab nosym case run as vc-relax (for example `cell_dofree='2Dxy'`), whose first displacement leaves the detected group.

```
defuqumat/workflows/vc_relax.py:470-486 `def _check_geometry(system, previous):` `if not check_lattice_symmetry(system.cell, previous.symmetries): raise RuntimeError(...)` / `if not check_symmetry(system.cell, system.structure, previous.symmetries): raise RuntimeError(...)`, called at :451 and :463 with no `use_symmetry` test. Compare defuqumat/workflows/relax.py:656 `if calculation.use_symmetry and not check_symmetry(`.
```

Skeptic: The path is as reported. scf/driver.py:2992 sets `self.symmetries = system.symmetry_group()` with no nosym argument, and system/builder.py:957-986 returns the full detected group in that case; `use_symmetry` at driver.py:3005 is the only switch. workflows/vc_relax.py:470-486 `_check_geometry` tests `check_lattice_symmetry(system.cell, previous.symmetries)` and `check_symmetry(..., previous.symmetries)` with no use_symmetry test, and it is called on both branches of `_advance` (:451 treinit_gvectors, :463 frozen basis). A grep of vc_relax.py for use_symmetry and nosym returns nothing, so there is no guard elsewhere in the file. run_relax has the guard (workflows/relax.py:656 `if calculation.use_symmetry and not check_symmetry(`) with a comment that says why. The trigger is not refused earlier: cell_dofree 'z', 'x', 'xy', '2Dxy', 'fix*' and 'epitaxial_*' are registered masks (relax/cell.py:95-116), the mask is applied to the cell rows of the step (relax/bfgs.py:231), and under nosym the stress is not symmetrised (stress/__init__.py:172 returns the raw tensor). On a cubic cell a step that changes only h_zz breaks R g R^T = g at the 1e-6 relative tolerance of system/symmetry.py:1591-1620 unless the zz cell force is exactly zero, so the first cell step raises. pw.x does not stop there: setup.f90:607-610 sets nsym = 1 under nosym, so checkallsym (move_ions.f90:419, the orthogonality loop at symm_base.f90:1040-1052) runs over the identity only. The PLAN record at 13266 names run_relax only, PLAN.md:5024 and :5126 describe check_lattice_symmetry without nosym, OPEN.md has no entry, and tests/unit/test_geometry_invalidation.py:242 (test_nosym_does_not_check_a_group_it_never_applies) covers run_relax only. The same path also stops an ionic displacement off a site in a nosym vc-relax (the adsorbate case), through the second half of _check_geometry.

Fix: Pass the calculation's switch into _check_geometry and return early when `previous.use_symmetry` is false, as relax.py:656 does, and add the vc-relax twin of test_nosym_does_not_check_a_group_it_never_applies (nosym, cell_dofree='z', one step, non-slow by stubbing the SCF as that test does). Reword the lattice message so that with symmetry on it names cell_dofree and nosym as the user's way out, since pw.x stops there too with 'not orthogonal operation'.

### `defuqumat/xc/functional.py:863`: grad_m exchange gives NaN where the charge is negative and |m| is exactly zero

Slice `xc-io`, bug, severity `crash`, status **confirmed**, known: new. OPEN.md Part XXI item 1 and PLAN.md P142 record grad_m and its relative step (PLAN.md:25405), but not the sign of n in the guard. The three audits do not mention _NONCOLLINEAR_RELATIVE_STEP or step_s..

The relative-step guard compares s = |m| with 1e-4 * n and not with 1e-4 * |n|. At a point with n < 0 and s = 0 exactly, step_s is 0 > (negative), which is True. So safe_s = s = 0 and h = (q(n) - q(n)) / 0 = 0/0 = NaN. q(n) is finite there, because gx is gated to zero, but the difference over a zero step is still 0/0. Then t = a . m = 0 and t * h = 0 * NaN = NaN. The NaN goes into the pointwise energy, the summed XC energy and, through jax.value_and_grad in the caller, into every component of the potential. No caller masks it: scf/potential.py:593 passes charge = rho[0] + rho_core unclipped, and paw/gradient.py:291 passes rho_rad[0]/r2 + core unclipped. 'grad_m' is the default wherever it applies (system/builder.py:163-168). The fix is a guard on |n| and on s > 0, for example step_s = (s > 0) & (s > step * jnp.abs(n)), or folding in the existing both = n > SMALL_SPIN_GGA mask. I reasoned this from the code and did not run it.

Trigger: A noncollinear magnetic PBE run (default noncollinear_gga = 'grad_m') where one grid point has a slightly negative plane-wave charge and a magnetization that is bit-exactly zero. The second condition is the one the safe_modulus docstring (functional.py:1066-1072) says occurs: sym_rho makes m exactly zero where the magnetic little group allows no axial vector. A concrete case is an even-layer antiferromagnetic slab with PT symmetry (for example a CrI3 or MnBi2Te4 bilayer, with spin-orbit coupling) on an even nr3 grid. The periodic image of the PT centre is the middle of the vacuum. There m is zero by symmetry, and the starting superposition or the mixed density often rings slightly negative. The same holds on a PAW sphere whose magnetic becsum is zero by symmetry while the pseudo-plus-compensation charge goes negative. How often a grid point meets both conditions depends on the cell, and I did not measure it.

```
functional.py:863-866: `step_s = s > _NONCOLLINEAR_RELATIVE_STEP * n` / `safe_s = jnp.where(step_s, s, 1.0)` / `h = jnp.where(step_s, (q(n + s) - q(n - s)) / safe_s, slope)`; and the return `even + t * h`. The caller is scf/potential.py:610, `functional.noncollinear_gradient_energy(charge, magnetization, grad_n, grad_m)` with `charge = rho[0] if rho_core is None else rho[0] + jnp.real(rho_core)`.
```

Skeptic: The arithmetic is as the finder says. functional.py:863 is `step_s = s > _NONCOLLINEAR_RELATIVE_STEP * n`, with no abs on n. At n < 0 and s = 0 this is 0 > (negative), so True, and :864 gives safe_s = s = 0. At n < 0 the gate `both = n > SMALL_SPIN_GGA` (:837) is False, so gx = 0 and q(n) = 0 on both branches of :859-861. Line :866 is then (0 - 0)/0 = NaN, and :872 returns even + t*h with t = a.m = 0, so 0*NaN = NaN in the energy and, through jax.value_and_grad (potential.py:613), in the whole potential. s is exactly 0 because safe_modulus returns 0.0 for a zero square (:1075-1077). No caller clips: potential.py:593 builds charge = rho[0] + Re(rho_core), paw/gradient.py:292 builds rho_rad[0]/r2 + core, and starting_charge in pseudo/potentials.py has no clip, abs or where. I found a simpler trigger than the finder's PT bilayer, and it makes m zero on every grid point instead of on a few. is_magnetic (system/builder.py:1058-1066) makes a noncollinear run magnetic from a B_field alone, with no seed, so nspin_mag = 4. _noncollinear_starting_density (scf/driver.py:6132-6143) then weights each species by magnitudes*directions = 0, so the three components are zeros in G and bit-exact zeros on the grid. resolve_noncollinear_gga (potential.py:695-709) picks 'grad_m' for nspin_mag = 4 with a gradient functional, PAW included. So the first XC evaluation of such a run gives NaN as soon as one grid point of the starting charge is negative. The existing test (tests/unit/test_noncollinear_gga_grad_m.py:155-169) zeroes m on a positive charge only, and the field-only test inputs (h-sheet-field.in) use an LDA dataset, so nothing in the suite reaches it. PLAN.md P142 and OPEN.md do not record it. What I did not measure, since no code was run: that the starting charge of a given cell has a negative point. A truncated atomic superposition rings through zero in a vacuum region, which is why the n > 1e-10 gate exists, so I take a molecule or slab as reaching it. In a dense bulk cell without vacuum it may never fire.

Fix: Guard on |n| and on s > 0: `step_s = (s > 0.0) & (s > _NONCOLLINEAR_RELATIVE_STEP * jnp.abs(n))`, so the n <= 0 points take the slope branch, which is 0 there. Add a test with m = 0 exactly on a charge that has negative points, and a field-only PBE start in a vacuum cell.

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:4892`: at_spiral_q and at_kpoints rebuild the projector core in double but keep the single-precision labels, floors and plane chunk

Slice `driver-b`, bug, severity `minor (perf and memory on the spiral scan, plus doc-drift; no wrong energy)`, status **confirmed**, known: new. The constructor comment at driver.py:2697-2700 and OPEN.md:8300 say at_kpoints rebuilds the core in the cell's precision, but no record says the copy keeps the single-precision band_precision label, ethr_floor, conv_thr_floor and plane_chunk..

The constructor casts the projector core to band_precision.complex (driver.py:2702), and the band side takes its dtype from that core (_band_real, :3125). at_strain(_moving=True) repeats the cast (:4097). at_spiral_q(rebuild_basis=True) (:4892) and at_kpoints (:4286) build a fresh core with _projector_core and do not cast it, so the moved copy computes its bands in double. copy.copy still carries band_precision (single), ethr_floor (SINGLE_ETHR_FACTOR*eps_single*ecutwfc, :3228-3229), conv_thr_floor (SINGLE_CONV_THR_FLOOR) and plane_chunk (sized for single box bytes). at_band_precision is the only mover that resets them (:4478-4489). The constructor comment at :2697-2700 calls the moved copy 'consistently double', but only the arrays are double. The result is three concrete effects. (1) A spiral E(q) scan point, or a band path from at_kpoints, pays double-precision time and memory while Davidson is clamped to the single-precision ethr floor, so it gets single accuracy at double cost. (2) run_scf refuses a conv_thr below the single floor (:8351) on a copy that actually runs in double. (3) SCFResult records band_precision='single' (:9950) for bands computed in double. The plane chunk, sized for 8-byte complex, is half the size it should be for 16-byte complex on the moved copy, which roughly doubles the per-chunk working set it was meant to bound. No silent wrong energy: the numbers come out more accurate than labelled, not less, and the refusals that read band_precision (forces, dE/dq, topology) still fire.

Trigger: Calculation(system_with_spiral_q, pseudos, band_precision='single') handed to run_spiral_scan(..., calculation=base) (workflows/spiral.py:305 then :323, base.at_spiral_q(q, widths=widths)). Every scan point's SCF then runs in double, is refused below the single conv_thr floor, and reports 'single'. The same holds for a band path or nscf on a single-precision Calculation through at_kpoints. One note outside this slice: run_spiral_scan builds its own base without band_precision (spiral.py:305-306), so a band_precision in scf_options is ignored there altogether.

```
driver.py:4892-4896 `moved.projector_core = _projector_core(self.pseudos, system.structure, cell, smooth, planewaves, moved.basis_kpoints, self.origin_tangent, chunked=self.memory_mode == "memory",)` with no `complex_dtype=self.band_precision.complex` replace, against driver.py:4097-4100 `if self.band_precision.complex != strained.projector_core.complex_dtype: strained.projector_core = dataclasses.replace(strained.projector_core, complex_dtype=self.band_precision.complex)`; same omission at driver.py:4286 (at_kpoints). driver.py:3125 `complex_dtype = self.projector_core.complex_dtype or self.system.cell.precision.complex`. driver.py:4489 `moved.ethr_floor, moved.conv_thr_floor = ETHR_MIN, 0.0` exists only in at_band_precision.
```

Skeptic: Confirmed, but narrowed to the spiral scan; the band-path half is almost all documented design.

The path. build_projector_core sets complex_dtype=cell.precision.complex (pseudo/projectors.py:447 and :526). at_spiral_q builds a fresh core at driver.py:4892-4896 and returns at :4905 with no cast; a grep of driver.py for complex_dtype finds the cast only at :2702-2704 (constructor), :4097-4100 (at_strain, _moving) and :4481 (at_band_precision), and no reject_single_band_side call anywhere in the driver. The band dtype is read off the core (_band_real, :3125; projectors.py:228, :265, :366 cast vkb to it), so the moved copy's H|psi> is complex128. copy.copy keeps band_precision, ethr_floor and conv_thr_floor, which are set only at :3228-3230 and reset only at :4489, and plane_chunk, sized at :2678-2680 and re-sized only at :4485-4487.

The input reaches it. A spiral in single is a supported regime (_require_band_precision_supported docstring at :3142, guide performance.md:351-352) and at_spiral_q refuses nothing about precision. The front door is Calculator(..., spiral_q=..., band_precision='single').get_spiral_scan(qs): calculator.py:724 builds the single Calculation, calculator.py:2607-2608 hands it to run_spiral_scan, workflows/spiral.py:323 calls base.at_spiral_q(q, widths=widths) for every point, then run_scf(calculation=...). With gradients=True the scan is refused at forces/spiral.py:603 before any SCF, so the trigger is gradients=False. Every scan point then runs double arrays with ethr floored at 4 eps32 ecutwfc (:9002, :9285, :9305 read ethr_floor), conv_thr below 1e-7 refused (:8351) and history labelled 'single' (:9950). The user asked for single to halve time and memory and gets single accuracy at double cost; no energy is wrong. The stale label also makes scf/continuation.py:703 use the looser single-source electron tolerance on a double density, which is harmless.

What dies. For at_kpoints (:4286) only the plane chunk survives. bands.py:318, :333 and nscf.py:241 take fixed_density_ethr and never read ethr_floor or conv_thr_floor, and :9950 is run_scf's history only, so a band path or nscf on the moved copy is fully double at its own threshold. That double run is the stated design: driver.py:2697-2701 and docs/guide/performance.md:381-383 ("A calculation moved to a new geometry or to a band path builds its projectors again in double precision"). The guide does not name a spiral scan and says nothing about the floors, the label or the chunk.

Two corrections to the finder. The plane chunk is sized with itemsize 8, so it holds twice the planes a double sizing gives, not half; the conclusion, a per-chunk working set twice the 384 kB budget, is right. It costs on a CPU only (batching.py:867 returns None on an accelerator), and batching.py:835-841 records that the spinor si16 cell falls off a cliff past 4 planes, and a spiral is a spinor run. Also 'mixed' heals itself: at_band_precision proceeds when switch_precision is not None (:4471-4472) and resets label, floors and chunk (:4478-4489) at the switch (:9301-9305), so only pure 'single' keeps the stale state for the whole scan.

No test covers it: tests/unit/test_band_precision.py has no at_spiral_q, at_kpoints, scan or plane_chunk. I could not check by reading alone that nothing else carried by copy.copy is still float32 and meets the double core; I found no such object.

Fix: Either cast the rebuilt core in at_spiral_q to self.band_precision.complex as at_strain does at driver.py:4097-4100, so a scan in single stays single (the measured regime); or declare a moved copy double and reset band_precision, the two floors and plane_chunk in at_spiral_q and at_kpoints as at_band_precision does at :4478-4489, and add the spiral scan to the guide's sentence at performance.md:381. At least re-size plane_chunk in at_kpoints for the dtype the copy runs in.

### `defuqumat/realtime/propagate.py:1455`: A real-time checkpoint never resumes for nspin = 2, or for any path that does not end in .npz

Slice `realtime`, bug, severity `perf`, status **confirmed**, known: new. notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1398 checked the .partial to .npz rename of the SCF's own checkpoint.py, not this one..

The resume test looks for the file under the exact name it was given. The write goes through np.savez, which adds '.npz' when the name does not already end in it (numpy/lib/_npyio_impl.py:776, `if not file.endswith('.npz'): file = file + '.npz'`). For a collinear nspin = 2 run, propagate() builds the per-channel name f"{checkpoint}.channel{channel}", which never ends in .npz. So the file is written as '<name>.channel0.npz' and the next run looks for '<name>.channel0'. It finds nothing and silently recomputes every chunk. The same happens on nspin = 1 when the user passes a name without the suffix. No number comes out wrong, but the documented claim does not hold: the propagate docstring says 'a run that finds a file with the same time grid and k-set resumes after the chunks it records'. A killed long run loses all its finished chunks, and each run leaves an orphan .npz behind. The only tests (tests/unit/test_realtime.py:561 and :585) use tmp_path / 'rt.npz' on nspin = 1, which is exactly the one case that works.

Trigger: run_realtime / Calculator.get_realtime (or propagate) with checkpoint='rt.npz' on any collinear nspin = 2 ground state: the second call recomputes every chunk. Also checkpoint='rt' (no suffix) on nspin = 1.

```
propagate.py:1399 `path = Path(f"{checkpoint}.channel{channel}")`; propagate.py:1455-1456 `if checkpoint is not None and Path(checkpoint).exists():\n        saved = np.load(checkpoint)`; propagate.py:1515 `np.savez(checkpoint, signature=np.asarray(signature), current=current, ...`
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. propagate.py:1397-1399 builds `Path(f"{checkpoint}.channel{channel}")` whenever `len(pairs) > 1`, and channel_arrays (propagate.py:1296) gives two pairs for every collinear nspin = 2 run. The resume test at propagate.py:1455 is `Path(checkpoint).exists()` on that exact name, and the write at propagate.py:1515 is `np.savez(checkpoint, ...)`. The installed numpy (python3.14 site-packages/numpy/lib/_npyio_impl.py:761-764) does `file = os.fspath(file)` and then `if not file.endswith('.npz'): file = file + '.npz'`, so the file on disk is `<name>.channel0.npz` and the next run looks for `<name>.channel0`. The same mismatch holds on nspin = 1 for any name without the suffix. No upstream guard stops it: the only checkpoint refusal is for potential != 'frozen' (propagate.py:1383), run_realtime (workflows/realtime.py:349, :403) and get_realtime_dielectric's route (:472, :491) forward the path unchanged, and nspin = 2 at a frozen potential is in scope (P140). The two tests (tests/unit/test_realtime.py:561, :585) use a `.npz` name on one channel, the one working case. I found no record in OPEN.md, PLAN.md, notes/ or docs/guide; notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1398 is about the SCF's checkpoint.py. No number is wrong, the cost is that a killed nspin = 2 run recomputes every chunk and leaves orphan files, against the docstring at propagate.py:1357-1359.

Fix: Normalise the name once in _propagate_channel (append '.npz' when the path does not end in it, and build the channel name as `<stem>.channel{c}.npz`), use the same name for exists, load and savez, and add a test on nspin = 2 and on a suffix-free name.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:604`: Calculator.estimate reads k_batch=None and band_batch=None as 'not given' and sizes the calculator's default chunk

Slice `calculator`, bug, severity `minor (a wrong size report, not a wrong physics number)`, status **confirmed**, known: new; the 2026-09-18 audit (line 269) covers estimate(projectors=...) only..

estimate_size documents None as a real value for both dials: k_batch None is 'the whole axis' and band_batch None is 'every band at once' (sizing.py:547-549, :566-569). The facade's estimate tests `options.get(...) is None` to decide whether the caller gave a value. So an explicit None is overwritten by the calculator's resolved dial, which on a CPU is 1 and 1. The report then sizes one k-point and one band box in flight for a run that holds all of them, and the k-live lines come out low by up to a factor of nk. The docstring says 'what is not given is taken from this calculator's own defaults', and None was given. The CLI's `size --k-batch` is `type=int` (cli.py:351), so the whole axis cannot be asked for there either, while `scf --k-batch all` exists (cli.py:102-105).

Trigger: `calc.estimate(k_batch=None)` or `calc.estimate(band_batch=None)` on any multi-k cell, asking what the run would cost in speed mode or with k_batch=None. tests/unit/test_k_budget.py:289 calls estimate_size(k_batch=None) directly, so only the facade route is affected.

```
calculator.py:595 `if options.get("band_batch") is None:` and calculator.py:604 `if options.get("k_batch") is None:` then `options["k_batch"] = resolve_k_batch_for(self.defaults.get("k_batch", "default"), ...)`
```

Skeptic: calculator.py:595 `if options.get("band_batch") is None:` and calculator.py:604 `if options.get("k_batch") is None:` cannot tell an explicit None from a missing key, and both overwrite it with the calculator's resolved dial. sizing.py:547-550 documents `k_batch=None` as the whole axis and sizing.py:566-570 documents `band_batch=None` as every band at once, and sizing.py:707 `k_live = nk if k_batch is None else min(k_batch, nk)` has no string spelling for it, so None is the only way to ask for the whole axis besides passing nk itself. The estimate docstring (calculator.py:563-568) says the options are estimate_size's and that only what is not given is taken from the calculator. So `calc.estimate(k_batch=None)` sizes the calculator's own chunk. No test or doc snippet calls the facade with an explicit None (grep over tests, docs and README found none), so nothing pins either reading. Two things limit it: `estimate(k_batch=nk)` works, and a calculator built with `k_batch=None` or `memory_mode='speed'` is resolved correctly through `self.defaults`. The CLI part is also right: cli.py:351 is `type=int` and cli.py:57-58 only forwards a non-None value, so `size` cannot ask for the whole axis.

Fix: Test `"k_batch" not in options` and `"band_batch" not in options` (or use a sentinel) instead of `is None`, and let `size --k-batch` take `all` as `scf --k-batch` does.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:832`: A setup-only option passed to get_scf on a cache hit is silently dropped, origin_tangent included

Slice `calculator`, bug, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**, known: new. The get_scf comment and tests/unit/test_projector_storage.py:134-175 cover the opposite direction (the key no longer carries the option), not the drop on a hit..

get_scf removes SETUP_ONLY_OPTIONS from the cache key and returns the cached state before it reaches `_adopt(options)`. The comment just above says _adopt 'already drops the cache when one changes', but _adopt only runs on a miss. So after a plain get_scf(), a call to get_scf(origin_tangent=False) returns the cache, does not record the option in defaults and does not rebuild `calculation`. Every later response (dielectric tensor, Born charges, phonons, piezo) then runs on the l = 1 tangent convention the caller asked to leave, which is the convention that moves a ph.x comparison on a Gamma-containing mesh (OPEN.md:5620, :6211). For projectors, memory_mode and band_batch the same drop only means the memory dial is not applied.

Trigger: `calc.get_scf(); calc.get_scf(origin_tangent=False); calc.get_born_charges()` on a mesh that holds Gamma. On a fresh calculator the same call is adopted correctly. Constructor use, Calculator(..., origin_tangent=False), is not affected.

```
calculator.py:828-839 `merged = {... if name not in SETUP_ONLY_OPTIONS}` / `if self._scf is not None and _same_options(merged, self._scf_options): return self._scf` / ... `self._adopt(options)`
```

Skeptic: calculator.py:829-831 strips SETUP_ONLY_OPTIONS from `merged`, calculator.py:832-833 returns the cache when `merged` matches, and `_adopt(options)` at calculator.py:839 is the only call site of `_adopt` in the file (grep) and sits after that return. So after a plain `get_scf()`, `get_scf(origin_tangent=False)` returns the cached state, leaves `self.defaults` unchanged and keeps `self._calculation`, which calculator.py:721 built with `origin_tangent=self.defaults.get("origin_tangent", True)`. The later response then runs on the default convention. The comment at calculator.py:827-828 and the test docstring at tests/unit/test_projector_storage.py:171-175 both say `_adopt` drops the cache when a setup option changes, which holds only on a miss; the three tests there (:134-175) all pass the option on the first call of a fresh calculator. The same drop applies to `projectors`, `memory_mode` and `band_batch`, where it costs only the dial. The options in SETUP_OPTIONS that are not setup-only (`k_batch`, `david`, `diagonalization`, `band_precision`) are in the key, so they miss and are adopted. Severity stays minor: the sequence needs a second `get_scf` with an unchanged key, the constructor route is correct, and the effect of `origin_tangent` is on a ph.x comparison convention (OPEN.md:5620, :6211), not on the ground state.

Fix: Call `_adopt(options)` before the cache test and have it return whether anything changed; on a change, rebuild the calculation and either keep the SCF (the state does not depend on these options) or treat it as a miss, and say which in the comment.

### `defuqumat/kpm/dos.py:163`: reconstructed() replaces energies, kernel and n_moments but leaves the old spin_dos beside them

Slice `kpm`, bug, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**.

KPMDensityOfStates.reconstructed rebuilds dos, integrated and dos_error from the stored samples and swaps energies, kernel and n_moments. spin_dos is not in the replace call, and the spin moments are not stored on the result, so they cannot be rebuilt. After reconstructed(energies=new) the result holds spin_dos of shape (3, nE_old) next to energies of length nE_new. After a kernel or moment cut, spin_dos keeps the old broadening while the labels claim the new one. Nothing raises.

Trigger: kpm = calc.get_kpm_dos(spin_resolved=True) on a spinor run, then kpm.reconstructed(kernel='lorentz') or kpm.reconstructed(energies=np.linspace(...)), then read .spin_dos

```
dos.py:163-165 `return replace(self, energies=energies, dos=dos, integrated=integrated, dos_error=error, kernel=kernel, n_moments=n_moments, broadening=broadening)`; spin_dos is set only at dos.py:361 from moments that are not kept
```

Skeptic: reconstructed (dos.py:143-165) ends with `return replace(self, energies=energies, dos=dos, integrated=integrated, dos_error=error, kernel=kernel, n_moments=n_moments, broadening=broadening)`. spin_dos is a field of the frozen dataclass (dos.py:95) and is not in the replace call, so the old array is copied. The dataclass (dos.py:75-95) stores samples of the scalar trace only; the spin moments are local to spin_resolved_dos (dos.py:438-443) and are lost, so they cannot be rebuilt. No guard on spin_dos is in the method. A new energy grid leaves a visible shape mismatch, (3, nE_old) beside nE_new. A new kernel or fewer moments leaves a silent stale array with the labels of the new broadening. No test covers it and Calculator.get_kpm_dos advertises .reconstructed() (calculator.py:1011-1012).

Fix: Keep the spin moments (3, N), or the samples (3, R, N), on the result and rebuild spin_dos in reconstructed(); until then set spin_dos=None or raise when it is present.

### `defuqumat/realtime/pulse.py:131`: One kick, three time tolerances: an on-grid kick at t >= 128 can switch on one step late, or be refused on US/PAW

Slice `realtime`, bug, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**, known: notes/incompat/I63.md:89 records the 'between two points' refusal itself. The mismatch between its 1e-12 acceptance and the 1e-14 switch-on is new..

Kick.kappa_at and the `on` selection in kick_jumps both switch the kick on at `t >= time - 1e-14`, an absolute slack. The on-grid test in kick_jumps accepts `1e-9*dt + 1e-12`, and conductivity_from_kick uses 1e-12. For t >= 128 the spacing of doubles is at least 2.8e-14, so the 1e-14 slack is zero there. If `start + n*dt` lands one ulp below a decimal kick time, `on` picks n+1. On an ultrasoft or PAW dataset, |times[n+1] - time| is then about dt, so the code gives the 'between two points of the time grid' error for a kick that is on the grid, which contradicts the error's own advice. On a norm-conserving dataset the dynamics are unchanged, because the midpoint rule still kicks step n. But kappa_t[n] is unkicked, so J(t_n) is the pre-kick current. conductivity_from_kick(kick_time=time) takes that sample as J(0+), because its own slack is 1e-12. That gives a quadrature error of order (dt/3) J(0+) on sigma. I did not compute a specific (dt, time) pair, since no Python was allowed. The ulp argument is from reading.

Trigger: propagate() on an ultrasoft or PAW calculation with a Kick(time=T) where T >= 128 a.u. is a decimal literal and start + n*dt rounds one ulp below it (a literal time rather than n*dt). The workflow route run_realtime_dielectric (kick at 0, start 0) is not affected.

```
pulse.py:131 `on = jnp.where(t >= self.time - 1.0e-14, 1.0, 0.0)`; propagate.py:1202 `on = np.flatnonzero(times >= time - 1.0e-14)`; propagate.py:1205 `if abs(float(times[n]) - time) > 1e-9 * dt + 1e-12:` (raises NotImplementedError 'between two points of the time grid'); spectra.py:107 `after = times >= kick_time - 1e-12`
```

Skeptic: The path is real and I derived a concrete input by hand, with no machine check. The grid is `times = start + dt * np.arange(nsteps + 1)` (propagate.py:289) and Kick.start is the class default 0.0 (pulse.py:88), so times[n] = fl(dt*n). Take dt = 0.3, Kick(time=129.3): double(0.3) = 5404319552844595 * 2^-54, which is 0.2 units below 0.3. In [128, 256) one ulp is 2^-45 = 512 of those units, and 129.3 sits at K + 0.6 ulp, so double(129.3) = K + 1. The exact product 431 * double(0.3) is K + 0.6 - 86.2/512 = K + 0.43 ulp and rounds to K, one ulp below the literal (the same arithmetic reproduces 0.3*3 = 0.8999999999999999). The slack 1e-14 is 0.35 ulp there, so `129.3 - 1e-14` is 129.3 again, and both pulse.py:131 and propagate.py:1202 read times[431] as before the kick. On an ultrasoft or PAW calculation `on[0]` is 432, `abs(times[432] - time)` is 0.3, and propagate.py:1205 raises the 'between two points of the time grid' error for a kick that is on the grid within the function's own 1e-9*dt + 1e-12 acceptance. The earlier guard (propagate.py:1199) only skips kicks at or before the start. On a norm-conserving dataset kick_jumps returns None (:1190) and kappa_t[431] is unkicked, so J(t_431) is the pre-kick current, and conductivity_from_kick(kick_time=129.3) keeps that sample through its 1e-12 slack (spectra.py:107) with Simpson weight dt/3. The workflow routes kick at t = 0 (workflows/realtime.py:440, :816) and are not affected; the reach is a hand-built delayed kick, for instance a pump plus a probe kick, which tests/unit/test_realtime.py:825 shows is a supported sum. The tests only use kick times of 0.5, 1.0, 1.01 and 5.0. notes/incompat/I63.md:71 and :89 record the comparison and the refusal, not the mismatch of the three tolerances.

Fix: Use one relative tolerance for the three sites, for instance a shared helper that takes the grid index as `round((time - start)/dt)` when it is within 1e-9*dt and switches kappa on from that index, so Kick.kappa_at, kick_jumps and conductivity_from_kick agree.

### `defuqumat/pseudo/upf.py:408`: The beta-chi overlap in _renormalize_orbitals stops at each projector's own cutoff, while QE integrates to kkbeta, and on PAW files beta is not zero in between

Slice `pseudo-paw`, bug, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**, known: notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1416 saw this range difference and dropped it because 'beta is zero by construction'. What is new: that premise fails on all four committed PAW files I checked, where beta is of order 1e-4 between the projector cutoff and kkbeta..

QE's upf_check_atwfc_norm (upflib/upf_auxtools.f90:22) computes <beta_i|chi> as simpson(kkbeta, beta*chi, rab), over the dataset-wide kkbeta. The code stops at projector.cutoff_index and uses a slice of the full-mesh Simpson weights. For a PAW dataset kkbeta is widened to the augmentation index, so the code drops the points between the two indices. An earlier audit dismissed this because it assumed beta is zero there. That is false on the committed PAW files. In Si.pbe-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1.UPF the cutoff_radius_index is 841 and cutoff_r_index is 849. At 1-based indices 842 to 849, PP_BETA.1 reads -4.95e-4, -4.57e-4, -4.17e-4, -3.75e-4, -3.30e-4, -2.83e-4, -2.34e-4 and -1.83e-4, and PP_CHI.1 is about -0.67 there. With rab of about 0.025, the dropped part of <beta_1|chi_1> is about 2.8e-3 * 0.67 * 0.025, roughly 5e-5. This is arithmetic from the printed rows, not a measurement. The q_ij term of the norm then moves by about 2 q_11 <beta|chi> 5e-5, so chi is rescaled differently from pw.x in roughly the fifth decimal. The threshold abs(norm - 1) > 1e-6 can also flip on the same margin. formfactors.projector_form_factors already integrates to kkbeta for the reason its docstring gives: 'Integrating over the shorter range would drop a contribution QE keeps'. The orbital norm did not get the same change.

Trigger: read_upf on any PAW file that has PP_PSWFC, for example tests/data/pseudo/Si.pbe-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1.UPF or Ni.rel-pbe-spn-kjpaw_psl.1.0.0.UPF. No guard comes before the read. The rescaled chi reaches a reported number only through consumers of the atomic orbitals: the DFT+U occupation matrix with atomic projectors (ns scales as 1/norm^2), ortho-atomic projectors, and the projected DOS. Otherwise it only changes the starting wavefunctions. I did not check whether DFT+U on a PAW dataset is admitted upstream.

```
defuqumat/pseudo/upf.py:408-411
                cut = projector.cutoff_index
                overlaps[i] = float(
                    np.sum(projector.beta[:cut] * chi[:cut] * weights[:cut])
                )
with weights = np.asarray(simpson_weights(rab)) over the full mesh (upf.py:383). QE: gi(1:kkbeta)= upf%beta(1:kkbeta,ibeta) * upf%chi(1:kkbeta,iwfc); call simpson (kkbeta, gi, upf%rab, work(ibeta)) in upf_check_atwfc_norm, upflib/upf_auxtools.f90:22. Cutoffs on the committed files: Si.pbe-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1 projectors 841, augmentation 849; Ni.rel-pbe-spn-kjpaw_psl.1.0.0 866 against 874; Pt.pbe-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1 984/989/993 against 1001; O.pbe-kjpaw 761/773 against 781.
```

Skeptic: I tried to refute it on three grounds and none held. (1) The code path is as quoted: defuqumat/pseudo/upf.py:383 builds weights = simpson_weights(rab) on the full mesh and upf.py:408-411 sums beta*chi*weights over [:projector.cutoff_index], the projector's own cutoff_radius_index (upf.py:542-547). QE's upf_check_atwfc_norm (upflib/upf_auxtools.f90:54, 84-86) uses kkbeta = upf%kkbeta and calls simpson(kkbeta, gi, upf%rab, work(ibeta)); kkbeta is MAXVAL(kbeta) widened to paw%iraug for PAW (upflib/read_upf_new.f90:533-535), and read_upf_new never zeroes beta past kbeta (the only kbeta lines are 356, 367, 393, 533, 535). The routine runs at read time, after kkbeta is set (Modules/read_pseudo.f90:198). (2) The premise 'beta is zero in between' is false on the files. In tests/data/pseudo/Si.pbe-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1.UPF (cutoff_radius_index 841, cutoff_r_index 849) the rows at lines 1453-1455 give PP_BETA.1 at points 842 to 849 as -4.95e-4, -4.57e-4, -4.17e-4, -3.75e-4, -3.30e-4, -2.83e-4, -2.34e-4, -1.83e-4, exactly the finder's numbers, and PP_CHI.1 there is -0.636 to -0.575 (lines 6385-6387; the finder's -0.67 is slightly high). With Simpson coefficients 4/3, 2/3, ..., 1/3 the dropped sum is about 1.7e-3 times rab, so about 4e-5 if rab is about 0.025 (I did not read PP_RAB; this is hand arithmetic, not a run). In Pt.pbe-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1.UPF the 5D projector has cutoff 984 against cutoff_r_index 1001 and PP_BETA.5 is about 5.4e-5 on points 985 to 1001 (lines 2921-2925), chi(5D) about 0.3 to 0.4, so the dropped part is about 1e-5 there. (3) A valid input reaches it and nothing refuses it: read_upf calls _renormalize_orbitals unconditionally (upf.py:314), defuqumat/hubbard/__init__.py:5-8 admits DFT+U on PAW with atomic, ortho-atomic and norm-atomic projectors, and the committed tests/data/qe/pt-yukawa.in is exactly this case (Pt.pbe-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1.UPF, HUBBARD {ortho-atomic}, U Pt-5d 4.0). The rescaled chi reaches the Hubbard projectors through formfactors.py:606 and the Yukawa Slater integrals through hubbard/yukawa.py:154 (density chi**2). The same module already integrates the projectors to kkbeta for this reason (formfactors.py:421-438). Records: OPEN.md has no entry (grep for kkbeta, atwfc, renormali), and notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1416 dismissed it on the false premise. What limits it: the size is not measured. The norm moves by 2 q_ij <beta_j|chi> times the dropped overlap, and I did not read q_ij or the overlaps, so 'fifth decimal of chi' is an upper estimate; for ortho-atomic the effect on ns is weaker than for atomic, since only the relative scale between orbitals matters. The ultrasoft nickel numbers of P20 are not touched: in Ni.pz-nd-rrkjus.UPF beta.1 is exactly zero after its cutoff 949 (line 1476) and the Simpson weight at 949 is the interior 2/3 in both codes since kkbeta = 951 is odd. So this is PAW only, plus any ultrasoft file whose projectors have unequal cutoffs with a nonzero tail.

Fix: In _renormalize_orbitals take the dataset-wide range as formfactors.projector_form_factors does: cut = kkbeta (max projector cutoff, widened to paw.cutoff_index, so paw must be passed in), weights = simpson_weights(rab[:cut]) built on the truncated mesh so that an even kkbeta gets QE's closure, and overlaps[i] = sum(beta[:cut] * chi[:cut] * weights). Then compare the list of rescaled labels with pw.x's 'wavefunction(s) ... renormalized' line on a PAW file.

### `defuqumat/io/keywords.py:407`: from_keywords crashes when only some atoms carry a starting moment

Slice `xc-io`, bug, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**, known: new. There is nothing on _moment or STARTING_MOMENTS rows in OPEN.md, PLAN.md P159 or the three audits..

A two-entry atom stores None as its moment (line 235). As soon as any atom has a moment, the STARTING_MOMENTS loop calls _moment on every atom, None included (lines 313-316). For spin='collinear', np.ndim(None) is 0, so line 407 runs float(None) and raises a bare TypeError with no word about the input. For spin='noncollinear', the same input raises the wrong message, 'moment None: a noncollinear moment is a cartesian vector'. The user guide (docs/guide/own-crystal.md:94-97) presents the moment as an optional third entry and gives ('Fe', (0, 0, 0), 2.5) as the example. It does not say that a nonmagnetic atom in a magnetic crystal must be written with an explicit 0.0. The fix is to map None to a zero moment, (0, 0, 0), in the card row, or to refuse a partial list by name.

Trigger: Calculator.from_keywords(lattice='fcc', a=4.17, atoms=[('Ni', (0, 0, 0), 2.0), ('O', (0.5, 0.5, 0.5))], spin='collinear', ...), which is NiO or any oxide with a nonmagnetic anion written the natural way. from_keywords (calculator.py:684) hands atoms to pw_input_text unchanged.

```
keywords.py:235 `moments.append(entry[2] if len(entry) == 3 else None)`; :313-316 `if given: cards.append("STARTING_MOMENTS"); for moment in moments: cards.append(... _moment(moment, spin))`; :403-407 `if np.ndim(moment) != 0: raise ...` / `return (0.0, 0.0, float(moment))`.
```

Skeptic: keywords.py:235 stores None for a two-entry atom. Line :304 builds `given` from the non-None moments, and :313-316 then calls _moment on every entry of `moments`, None included. For spin='collinear', np.ndim(None) is 0, so the check at :403 passes and :407 runs float(None), a bare TypeError. For spin='noncollinear', np.asarray(None, dtype=float) has shape (), so :409-410 raises 'moment None: a noncollinear moment is a cartesian vector'. Calculator.from_keywords (calculator.py:684 onward) hands atoms to pw_input_text unchanged, so there is no earlier guard. The docstring (:125-128) and docs/guide/own-crystal.md:94-97 present the moment as an optional third entry and do not say that every atom needs one. A grep of tests/unit/test_keywords.py for a None or mixed moment found nothing. So NiO written as [('Ni', ..., 2.0), ('O', ...)] fails. I lower the severity: the run stops before any calculation, so there is no wrong number, only an error that does not name the cause.

Fix: In the STARTING_MOMENTS loop write a None as a zero row, 0.0 for collinear and (0, 0, 0) for noncollinear, and say so in the docstring and the guide. Check scf/fields.py:355-366 first, which refuses a zero row of the card under a constrained magnetization, so that route keeps a clear message.

## Confirmed by the skeptic: holes (35)

### `defuqumat/response/phononq.py:1733`: The phonon at q != 0 (and elph) runs on a time-reversal-halved k-set when the crystal's group is trivial

Slice `phonon`, hole, severity `wrong-answer (size not measured; narrow regime: a crystal with nsym = 1 and nosym left at its default)`, status **confirmed**, known: new for this consumer. The mechanism (a P1 cell reduced by time reversal alone) is AUDIT-2026-09-18 / PLAN.md P37, closed there for conductivity, chi0, spinchi0, transport, stm and qpi through is_reduced. The phonon at q was never one of those consumers and still tests use_symmetry only..

The q route refuses a wedge only through `calculation.use_symmetry`, which is `not nosym and nsym > 1` (scf/driver.py:3005). A P1 crystal with nosym left at .false. has nsym = 1, so use_symmetry is False. But the builder still passes rotations=[E] with time_reversal = not noinv to KPoints.automatic, which takes the irreducible_wedge branch and sets KPoints.reduced = True (system/kpoints.py:519-529, builder.py:1525-1528). AUDIT-2026-09-18 measured this mechanism: '{E} with time reversal keeps 32 of 64 points'. At q != 0 the -k partner of k contributes conj(drho_{-q,k}), not drho_{q,k}: its states sit on the k - q sphere. So the half-zone sum at weight 2 gives the wrong drho_q and the wrong D(q). The hermitisation 0.5 (M + M^H) then hides part of the asymmetry. No consumer in response/ calls is_reduced or reads kpoints.reduced. The docstring at phononq.py:1019 says dynamical_matrix_at_q 'refuses a reduced one by name', and for this wedge it does not. electron_phonon_at_q inherits the hole through dynamical_matrix_at_q.

Trigger: Any norm-conserving LDA crystal whose point group is only the identity (a P1 cell), nosym = .false. (the default), noinv = .false., K_POINTS automatic 4 4 4 0 0 0, then Calculator.get_phonons at q = (1/4, 0, 0) through the q route, or electron_phonon_at_q on the same cell with smearing. The k-set is about 36 of 64 points and nothing refuses it.

```
phononq.py:1733 `if calculation.use_symmetry:` (the only wedge test in require_a_two_sphere_regime); driver.py:3005 `self.use_symmetry = bool(not system.nosym and self.symmetries.nsym > 1)`; kpoints.py:519 `reduced = bool(rotations is not None and len(rotations))` and :529 `reduced = len(points) < int(np.prod(grid))`; phononq.py:1019 'dynamical_matrix_at_q refuses a reduced one by name'
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. The only wedge test on the q route is defuqumat/response/phononq.py:1733 `if calculation.use_symmetry:`, and defuqumat/scf/driver.py:3005 sets `self.use_symmetry = bool(not system.nosym and self.symmetries.nsym > 1)`, so a P1 crystal passes. The k-set of that crystal is still reduced: defuqumat/system/builder.py:1524-1528 gives `rotations = None if nosym else symmetries.rotation_array()` and `time_reversal=not noinv and not magnetic`, and defuqumat/system/kpoints.py:519-529 takes the irreducible_wedge branch for any non-empty rotation list and sets `reduced = len(points) < int(np.prod(grid))`. PLAN.md:6604-6614 records the measurement, {E} with time reversal keeps 32 or 36 of 64 points, "even a P1 cell reduces by time reversal". Nothing downstream stops it: a grep of phononq.py, chunked_phonon.py and elph.py finds no is_reduced and no read of kpoints.reduced, elph.py has no guard of its own, and the states at k+q come from a non-self-consistent solve on the shifted points (phononq.py:112-139), so a half set does not fail by a missing partner. I read the whole guard (phononq.py:1663-1787) and the entry (phononq.py:1876-1935); no other line refuses this input. The physics holds by the module's own text: phononq.py:1008-1012 says the +q and -q responses "are complex conjugates rather than equal", and 1015-1019 says "nothing is symmetrised ... this needs the whole k-grid". QE agrees: PW/src/irrek.f90:197-199 treats k and -k as equivalent only `IF (minus_q)`, so ph.x keeps the whole grid at a general q with one operation. At 2q = G ph.x uses the half zone but averages drho with its conjugate, and this code does not, so I expect the defect there too, but I did not settle that case by reading. No test and no entry of OPEN.md or PLAN.md covers the q phonon with this wedge (greps empty); P37 closed it for six other consumers only. Two corrections to the finder: the builder is defuqumat/system/builder.py, not defuqumat/builder.py, and the entry is Calculator.get_phonons_at_q (calculator.py:1325), not get_phonons. The guide repeats the false claim: docs/guide/phonons.md:407 says that without nosym the phonons at q give an error. The size of the error in D(q) is not measured, since this was a reading audit.

Fix: In require_a_two_sphere_regime, refuse when `calculation.use_symmetry or is_reduced(<the calculation's k-set>)` (system/kpoints.py:576), with the same message, and add a test that the guard fires on a P1 cell with nosym = .false. The guide's sentence then becomes true as written.

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:9309`: band_precision='mixed' under k-point pools crashes at the switch to double, because the PoolStore is cast with jnp.asarray

Slice `driver-c`, hole, severity `crash`, status **confirmed**, known: new (OPEN.md, PLAN.md and the three audits have no entry for band_precision 'mixed' under pools).

Under pools the store is a PoolStore at every iteration. park_wavefunctions passes it through unchanged and stream_diagonalize updates it in place. Its __array__ raises TypeError on purpose. The switch to double precision tests only isinstance(np.ndarray) and sends everything else to jnp.asarray, so every pool raises at the first iteration that meets the switch condition. Before that, single-precision iterations have already been paid for. The rotate_moments step at driver.py:10386 has a PoolStore branch for the same store, but the precision switch has none. Nothing refuses this pair upstream. _refuse_under_pools does not look at band_precision, and neither does Calculation._require_band_precision_supported. The pools Limits note in docs/guide/performance.md lists only the get_* methods and the Newton-Krylov Jacobians. A plain skip of the cast would be worse than the crash: the double-precision Davidson would then write into a complex64 pool store.

Trigger: DEFUQUMAT_POOLS=2 with Calculator(..., band_precision='mixed').get_scf(), or DEFUQUMAT_BAND_PRECISION=mixed, on any cell with at least 2 k-points. It fires at the first iteration > 1 where 0.1*accuracy/nelec < ethr_floor or accuracy < 10*conv_thr.

```
driver.py:9306-9309
            if wavefunctions is not None:
                wavefunctions = (np.asarray(wavefunctions, dtype=switch_to.complex)
                                 if isinstance(wavefunctions, np.ndarray)
                                 else jnp.asarray(wavefunctions, dtype=switch_to.complex))
parallel.py:225 PoolStore.__array__ raises TypeError("this wavefunction store holds k-points ... cannot be read as the whole set")
```

Skeptic: I followed the store through the pooled loop. Under pools run_scf forces wfc_store='stream' (driver.py:9058), and stream_start returns a PoolStore (streaming.py:202, called at driver.py:9349). park_wavefunctions returns a PoolStore unchanged (batching.py:1225-1228), and stream_diagonalize writes into it in place (streaming.py:244 on). So at every iteration > 1 `wavefunctions` is a PoolStore. The switch at driver.py:9306-9309 tests only isinstance(np.ndarray), so the PoolStore goes to jnp.asarray(..., dtype=switch_to.complex). PoolStore is not an array and its __array__ raises TypeError on purpose (parallel.py:225-231). I did not run it, so the exact text is unverified: it is either that TypeError or JAX's own error for an input type it does not know. Both stop the run, on every rank, since the accuracy that decides the switch is broadcast. The trigger is wider than the finder wrote: driver.py:9812-9815 sets converged=False while switch_to is set, so every pooled 'mixed' run that would converge reaches the cast, not only the ones that meet the threshold condition first. No guard upstream: _refuse_under_pools (driver.py:7832-7886) lists pool count, the jvp backend and the residual occupations only; _require_band_precision_supported (driver.py:3142-3233) refuses fields, constraints and unmeasured regime pairs and has no pools or platform test; calculator.py only forwards band_precision (lines 109, 724, 731, 745). The pools Limits note (docs/guide/performance.md:293-305) and the band_precision Limits note (same file, 351-372) do not mention the pair. tests/unit/test_parallel.py has no band_precision or 'mixed' case, and OPEN.md and PLAN.md have no entry (grep). band_precision='single' alone under pools has no such cast and is not touched by this finding. The rotate_moments step shows the right pattern for the same store (driver.py:10386-10387).

Fix: Add a PoolStore branch at driver.py:9306 that casts `.array` to switch_to.complex and rewraps it with the same rows and nk, as driver.py:10386 does, and add a 2-pool 'mixed' case to test_parallel.py. Until that is measured, refuse band_precision='mixed' in _refuse_under_pools by name and say so in the pools Limits note.

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:7265`: A Newton-Krylov warm-up under k-point pools runs the whole k-set on rank 0 alone, then crashes when it hands a wavefunction array to the pooled residual

Slice `driver-c`, hole, severity `crash`, status **confirmed**, known: new (PLAN.md:26663 records only the refusal of the tetrahedron LDOS warm-up; no record covers warm-up under pools).

Under pools, _solve_residual_body wraps the residual in PooledScfResidual. Ranks other than 0 go into serve() and wait at a broadcast. On rank 0 the warm-up loop calls residual.step_states, which PooledScfResidual does not override. So rank 0 runs the serial ScfResidual.step_states: a whole-set calculation.diagonalize, becsum and density, on one process, while the other pools sit idle. The point of pools is that this set may not fit or run in time on one process. The warm-up returns a whole-set array as `wavefunctions`, and this goes to newton_krylov as psi0. The first evaluate then reaches PooledScfResidual.step, whose _command does float(warm) on that array, giving a TypeError. The wrapper then aborts every pool. The pooled protocol expects an integer token (or None) as the warm start. Nothing refuses warm-up under pools: _refuse_under_pools refuses only jvp other than 'finite-difference', and refuse_residual_occupations refuses only tetrahedra and from_input. The ground-state guide recommends warmup 6 for Newton-Krylov, and the performance guide says Newton-Krylov with its default Jacobian runs under pools.

Trigger: DEFUQUMAT_POOLS=2 with get_scf(scf_solver='newton-krylov', scf_solver_options={'warmup': 6}), which is the guide's own recommended setting (docs/guide/ground-state.md:305). Any warmup_mixing triggers it, the default 'kerker' included.

```
driver.py:7264-7265
        for _ in range(warmup):
            fx, wavefunctions, eigenvalues = residual.step_states(x0, wavefunctions)
residual.py:636 (PooledScfResidual._command)
        vector[1] = -1.0 if warm is None else float(warm)
solvers.py newton_krylov: psi = psi0 ... r, psi_new = residual.residual(y, warm)
```

Skeptic: The warm-up loop calls residual.step_states (driver.py:7264-7265). Under pools `residual` is a PooledScfResidual on rank 0 (driver.py:7124-7131), and that class overrides step, jvp and sternheimer_jacobian but not step_states (residual.py:530-699; a grep for step_states finds only residual.py:308, 311 and driver.py:7265). So rank 0 runs the serial ScfResidual.step_states (residual.py:311-350): calculation.diagonalize, becsum and density on the whole set, with no collective, while the other ranks wait in serve() at the broadcast (residual.py:677-683). I did not check whether a pooled Calculation holds projectors for its own rows only (projector_rows, driver.py:2715-2722), so this serial pass may already fail; if it does not, the crash comes at the latest in the first solver evaluation: newton_krylov takes psi = psi0 (solvers.py:198) and calls residual.residual(y, warm) (solvers.py:208, 211), which reaches PooledScfResidual.step and _command, where float(warm) on a whole-set array is a TypeError (residual.py:636, 645). The wrapper then aborts the pools (driver.py:7059-7063). No guard: _refuse_under_pools (driver.py:7832-7886) refuses only a jvp other than 'finite-difference' and the residual occupations; the only warm-up refusal is LDOS with tetrahedra (driver.py:7217-7229). The apparent counter-evidence is stale. notes/incompat/I53.md:10 measured 'warmup': 3 under pools, and tests/unit/test_parallel.py:432-435 (test_newton_krylov_under_pools_reproduces_one_pool, slow) runs it at 2 and 3 pools. At the I53 merge 950943f2 (2026-10-16 20:32) the loop read `fx, wavefunctions = residual.step(x0, wavefunctions)` (driver.py:6977 of that commit), which is the pooled collective and returns a token. I75 (afbe10a9a, merged 8d9a3212 at 21:24 the same day) changed the call to step_states on a parallel branch (git blame of driver.py:7265); neither commit is an ancestor of the other, the merge was textually clean, and the test that would catch it is slow. So the claim is a regression of a path that worked, and the slow test should fail at HEAD. The guide advertises the pair: docs/guide/ground-state.md:305 recommends 'warmup': 6 and docs/guide/performance.md:298-299 says newton-krylov runs under pools.

Fix: Give PooledScfResidual a step_states that runs the collective evaluation and returns the gathered eigenvalues with the token, or under pools send a warm-up that is not LDOS through residual.step. Refuse warmup_mixing='ldos' under pools by name until its LDOS pass is pooled, since driver.py:7272-7275 calls calculation.density on the whole-set states.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1630`: get_landau_levels(route='ultracell') swallows zeeman=True, nlevels and kappa, so the documented zeeman refusal cannot fire

Slice `calculator`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **confirmed**, known: new; PLAN.md:28702 states the refusal as holding on both routes..

zeeman, nlevels, kappa, dirac, geometry and width are named parameters of the facade method, so they never reach **options. Only the peierls branch passes them on, to run_landau_levels, which is where zeeman=True is refused (landau.py:151-153). The ultracell branch calls run_magnetic_ultracell with flux, nbnd, N1 and **options only. So zeeman=True on that route returns levels without a Zeeman term and no error, against the docstring ('Refused by name: zeeman=True') and PLAN.md:28702 ('zeeman=True is refused by the workflow by name'). nlevels is also a parameter of run_magnetic_ultracell (landau_ultracell.py:301), and a caller's nlevels=20 is silently dropped there: the result keeps all `rank` levels. The facade also forwards none of the calculator's shared options on this route (no _defaults_for), so a constructor k_batch does not reach the workflow's own k_batch. That one changes memory, not the numbers.

Trigger: `calc.get_landau_levels(route='ultracell', flux=(1, 10), nbnd=8, zeeman=True)` on the nspin = 1 norm-conserving graphene or silicon cell, which refuse_regime admits.

```
calculator.py:1628-1634 `if route == "ultracell": ... return run_magnetic_ultracell(self.system, self.pseudos, self._ground_state("Landau levels"), flux=flux, nbnd=nbnd, N1=N1, local_only=False, **options)`; signature at 1595-1597 `nlevels=40, kappa=((0.0, 0.0),), dirac=True, zeeman=False, nbnd=None, N1=None, **options`
```

Skeptic: calculator.py:1595-1597 names `nlevels`, `kappa`, `dirac`, `zeeman`, `geometry` and `width` in the signature, so they never enter `**options`. The ultracell branch at calculator.py:1628-1634 returns `run_magnetic_ultracell(self.system, self.pseudos, self._ground_state(...), flux=flux, nbnd=nbnd, N1=N1, local_only=False, **options)` and passes none of them. `run_magnetic_ultracell` (landau_ultracell.py:290-321) has no `zeeman` parameter at all and has its own `nlevels` at :301 (used at :578 and :585), which therefore stays None and returns all `rank` levels. The only zeeman refusal is in `run_landau_levels` (landau.py:151-153), which this branch never reaches, and `refuse_regime` at calculator.py:1627 sees the system only. The docstring (calculator.py:1622-1623), PLAN.md:28701-28702 and docs/guide/magnetic-field.md:303 all state the refusal without a route. The only test of it is tests/unit/test_magnetic_peierls.py:210, on `run_landau_levels` directly. One correction to the trigger: the default ultracell route with `nbnd` refuses for its own reason (the docstring at calculator.py:1616-1618, the hybrid basis is not Hermitian), so graphene or silicon with `nbnd=8` most likely stops on that error first. The silent case is the route that does return levels, `trials=` and `N=` (P176 step 3) or `strict=False`, with `zeeman=True` or a caller's `nlevels`. I did not read which cell passes that gate, so the exact cell is not settled, but the swallow itself is unconditional. The part about shared options not being forwarded on this route is true (no `_defaults_for`) and changes memory only.

Fix: In the ultracell branch raise the same NotImplementedError on `zeeman=True`, forward `nlevels` (default None in the signature, resolved to 40 on the peierls route), and refuse non-default `geometry`, `width`, `kappa` and `dirac` by name.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1634`: zeeman=True is silently ignored on route='ultracell' (with nlevels, kappa, geometry, width, dirac)

Slice `magnetic`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **confirmed**, known: new (PLAN.md:28702 states the facade's zeeman=True refusal for both routes).

get_landau_levels takes zeeman as a named parameter and only the Peierls branch forwards it to run_landau_levels, which raises. The ultracell branch calls run_magnetic_ultracell without it, and that function has no zeeman argument. So with route='ultracell', trials= and N= (the projected basis, which runs), zeeman=True returns levels with no Zeeman term and no error. The Limits note of docs/guide/magnetic-field.md says that the Zeeman term is not in and that zeeman=True gives an error. The named nlevels, kappa, geometry, width and dirac are dropped on this branch in the same way. Fix: raise on zeeman before the route branch, and refuse the Peierls-only names when route='ultracell'. This is in calculator.py, outside the slice's file list, so the Calculator slice can deduplicate it.

Trigger: calc.get_landau_levels(route='ultracell', flux=(1, 10), trials=[...], N=..., zeeman=True) on any norm-conserving unpolarized cell that refuse_regime admits

```
calculator.py:1597 `dirac=True, zeeman=False, nbnd=None, N1=None, **options):`; :1629-1634 `if route == "ultracell": ... return run_magnetic_ultracell(self.system, self.pseudos, self._ground_state("Landau levels"), flux=flux, nbnd=nbnd, N1=N1, local_only=False, **options)` (no zeeman); landau_ultracell.py:290-322 signature has no zeeman; landau.py:162-164 the only zeeman refusal; guide magnetic-field.md:303 `The Zeeman term is not in: zeeman=True gives an error.`
```

Skeptic: calculator.py:1596-1597 takes zeeman, nlevels, kappa, geometry, width and dirac as named parameters, so they never enter **options. The ultracell branch at calculator.py:1627-1634 forwards only flux, nbnd, N1, local_only=False and **options. run_magnetic_ultracell (landau_ultracell.py:290-322) has no zeeman argument and no **kwargs, and the only Zeeman refusal is run_landau_levels (workflows/landau.py:152-154), which this branch does not call. The guard at calculator.py:1630 admits flux=(1, 10) with N in options, and trials= and N= is the form that runs (P176 step 3). So zeeman=True there returns levels with no error. The facade docstring ("Refused by name: zeeman=True") and the guide Limits note (magnetic-field.md:303) both promise an error. The test tests/unit/test_magnetic_facade.py:97-98 checks the refusal only on the Peierls route, so nothing shows the claim false. The facade's nlevels=40 is dropped in the same way although run_magnetic_ultracell has its own nlevels, so nlevels cannot be set on this route through the Calculator.

Fix: Raise on zeeman=True before the route branch. On route='ultracell' forward nlevels by name and refuse geometry, width, kappa and dirac when they differ from their defaults.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1639`: get_landau_levels drops every extra keyword once wannier= is given, so run_landau_levels's own options cannot be reached

Slice `magnetic`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **confirmed**.

On the Peierls route, **options are passed only to get_wannier, and only when wannier is None. With wannier= given (the guide's own form) they are never read, and nothing raises. run_landau_levels's energy, cutoff, qmax, degeneracy_tol, vectors and bonds are not forwarded on either branch. So calc.get_landau_levels(B=10, wannier=pz, energy=e) returns the window centred on E_D (or on the edge), not on e. qmax=500 is ignored, so B is rounded with qmax = 20000 and the flux returned is not the one asked for. cutoff=1e-4 is ignored and the full bond list is used. The named nbnd and N1 are also dropped on this route. Each result looks valid. Fix: forward run_landau_levels's parameters by name, as the facade rule asks, and refuse leftover options when wannier is given. This is in calculator.py, outside the slice's file list.

Trigger: graphene.get_landau_levels(B=10.0, wannier=pz, nlevels=44, energy=0.2) or (..., qmax=500) or (..., cutoff=1e-4), where pz is the guide's 2-function p_z set

```
calculator.py:1639 `wannier = self.get_wannier(**options) if wannier is None else wannier`; :1642-1644 `return run_landau_levels(wannier, B=B, flux=flux, geometry=geometry, width=width, nlevels=nlevels, kappa=kappa, dirac=dirac, zeeman=zeeman, fermi_energy=level)` (no options, no energy, cutoff, qmax, degeneracy_tol); landau.py:106-123 run_landau_levels signature with `qmax`, `energy`, `cutoff`, `degeneracy_tol`, `vectors`, `bonds`
```

Skeptic: calculator.py:1639 reads **options only in `self.get_wannier(**options) if wannier is None else wannier`, and calculator.py:1642-1644 calls run_landau_levels with a fixed list of names (B, flux, geometry, width, nlevels, kappa, dirac, zeeman, fermi_energy). run_landau_levels has qmax, energy, cutoff, degeneracy_tol, vectors and bonds (workflows/landau.py:106-123), and none of them can be reached from the facade. With wannier= given, which is the guide's own form (magnetic-field.md:90), any extra keyword is accepted and never read, including a misspelled one. nbnd and N1 are also dropped on this route. tests/unit/test_magnetic_facade.py:65-72 passes only flux, kappa, nlevels, dirac and B, so no test contradicts it. I lower the severity from wrong-answer because the result carries .energy and .flux, so the window and the flux that were used are on the result. It is still a silent drop, against the facade rule of forwarding by name (.claude/rules/calculator.md).

Fix: Forward energy, qmax, cutoff, degeneracy_tol and vectors by name to run_landau_levels, and raise TypeError on leftover options when wannier is given.

### `defuqumat/wannier/disentangle.py:59`: An outer window with no eigenvalue inside it at some k takes every band below dis_win_min as the outer set

Slice `wannier`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **confirmed**.

dis_windows refuses only a window that lies wholly above or below the band range (line 56). If at some k no eigenvalue lies in [dis_win_min, dis_win_max] but e[0] <= dis_win_max and e[-1] >= dis_win_min, then in_win is all False. np.argmax then returns 0, so imin = 0, while imax is the last band at or below dis_win_max. The outer set becomes bands 0..imax, and all of them lie below dis_win_min. The refusal at line 62 ('fewer states than num_wann') can only fire if fewer than num_wann bands lie below the window. With enough bands below, the run goes on with an outer set made of states the user excluded, and nothing warns. With symmetry, slim_d_matrix_band may then refuse a star whose members differ, but on a star that is uniform, or with symmetry off, the wrong set passes. Swapped bounds (dis_win_min > dis_win_max) reach the same branch, because nothing checks the order. I found this by reading and did not run it. Repair: refuse when in_win has no True at a k-point (and refuse min > max), naming the k.

Trigger: get_wannier with given projections, num_bands > num_wann and a dis_win that, at one or more mesh points, falls entirely inside a gap of the num_bands bands while at least num_wann bands lie below dis_win_min there; or dis_win given with its two ends swapped.

```
defuqumat/wannier/disentangle.py:56 `if e[0] > dis_win_max or e[-1] < dis_win_min:` ; :58 `in_win = (e >= dis_win_min) & (e <= dis_win_max)` ; :59 `imin = int(np.argmax(in_win))  # first True (assumes ascending eigenvalues, matches Fortran)` ; :60 `imax = int(np.nonzero(e <= dis_win_max)[0][-1])` ; :61 `outer[imin:imax + 1, k] = True`
```

Skeptic: The path is as quoted. defuqumat/wannier/disentangle.py:56 refuses only a window wholly above or below the bands at that k. With in_win all False, np.argmax gives imin = 0 (:59), imax is the last band at or below dis_win_max (:60), and outer[0:imax+1] is set (:61); the only later check is the count against num_wann (:62) and windows_from_masks checks only the count again and frozen inside outer (:92-101). So the outer set is made of bands below dis_win_min. dis_main calls dis_windows whenever no mask is given (disentangle.py:414-415), which is every run with given projections or SCDM and num_bands > num_wann; nothing in run_wannier or run_engine checks the window against the eigenvalues or the order of its ends (workflows/wannier.py:583-586, engine.py:126-133). A concrete input that reaches it: graphene, get_wannier(2, ['C:pz'], num_bands=16, dis_win=(ef - 1, ef + 1), symmetry=False), the cell of docs/guide/magnetic-field.md with a narrow outer window around the Dirac point. At K the two Dirac states are inside, but at Gamma and at M no band lies within 1 eV of ef (sigma near ef - 3 and pi near ef - 2.3 below, the next states above ef + 1.7), so there the outer set becomes the four valence bands, 4 >= num_wann = 2, and the run goes on. The band energies are the known ones of graphene, I did not compute them here. The comment at :59 ('matches Fortran') is not exact for this case: wannier90-3.1.0/src/disentangle.F90:710-722 leaves imin = 0 and imax = num_bands, so ndimwin = num_bands + 1, which is also not a refusal. The swapped-ends half is real but narrower than claimed: it passes silently only if at least num_wann bands lie at or below dis_win_max at every k; otherwise it stops with the misleading 'fewer states than num_wann'. Wannier90 refuses swapped ends before that, parameters.F90:1676-1677. I did not read slim_d_matrix_band, so the remark about symmetry is not checked. No test covers the two refusal messages and the Limits note of docs/guide/wannier.md does not mention it.

Fix: In dis_windows raise when `not in_win.any()` at a k-point, naming the k and the window, and refuse dis_win_min > dis_win_max in run_engine as param_read does. Add a test that each guard fires.

### `defuqumat/workflows/anisotropy.py:1045`: The torque's nosym refusal fires only when the texture turns, so a torque at the system's own orientation is taken on its magnetic wedge

Slice `magnetism-response`, hole, severity `missing-refusal (the wrong-number face is argued from symmetry, not measured; the trigger as the finder wrote it is a null)`, status **confirmed**, known: Partly known. notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:168 and :653-662 record and fix the turned branch (run_torque and frozen_expectation turning the axis without nosym). The fix put the check inside the turning branch only. The early-return branch, where the direction is the system's own and the default rotation is the identity, is new..

The docstring of _turned_on_its_own_wedge (anisotropy.py:1072-1074) says the torque and the orientation torque need nosym, because they differentiate a wedge sum in the direction and dH/dtheta is an axial vector that the direction's group does not leave alone. The refusal sits in _require_nosym_to_turn, and both helpers return before it when nothing turns: _with_rotation at :1045-1046 for the identity, and _with_quantization_axis at :1026-1029 when the direction is the system's own axis. run_orientation_torque defaults to rotation=None, which _checked_rotation (:1482) maps to the identity. So the default call on a noncollinear spin-orbit run without nosym takes -dF/dw for the three generators on the k-set reduced with the magnetic group of the moment, and nothing symmetrises the result (forces/torque.py has no reduced, wedge or nosym check). The wedge sum of a per-k gradient equals the zone sum only for the components that every operation of the group leaves fixed. A moment along a C4 or C2 axis (tetragonal Co along z, its easy axis) has a true torque of exactly zero perpendicular to it, and the wedge returns a residue there instead. run_torque at angle=0, or any angle where the direction equals the system's angle1/angle2, takes the same branch. A second face: relax_orientation from the identity computes step one on the wedge, then is refused at step two by _with_rotation or Calculation.with_texture, after one diagonalisation is already paid for. I did not measure the size of the residue. The only number on record is the turned branch at 45 degrees, where the tangent is invariant and the result was a null. frozen_expectation is not part of this claim, because its quantity is a scalar on its own wedge.

Trigger: co-tetragonal-anisotropy-soc.in with nosym = .true. deleted (moment along z, so the k-set is the wedge of its magnetic group), then Calculator.get_orientation_torque(spinor) at the default rotation, or get_torque(spinor, angle=0). The same applies to get_relaxed_orientation from the identity, which is refused at step two.

```
anisotropy.py:1045-1048
    if np.max(np.abs(rotation - np.eye(3))) <= DIRECTION_TOL:
        return system
    own = np.asarray(_reference_axis(system), dtype=float)
    _require_nosym_to_turn(system, own)
anisotropy.py:1026-1030
    if np.sum(np.abs(own - wanted)) <= DIRECTION_TOL:
        ...
        return system
    _require_nosym_to_turn(system, own)
anisotropy.py:1072-1074 "The torque and the frozen expectation value still need ``nosym`` (:func:`_require_nosym_to_turn`): they differentiate a wedge sum in the direction"
anisotropy.py:1670  turned = _with_rotation(system, rotation)
```

Skeptic: The guard gap is real as the code stands. _with_rotation returns at anisotropy.py:1045-1046 for the identity and _with_quantization_axis at :1026-1029 for the system's own axis, both before _require_nosym_to_turn (:1030, :1048). Nothing upstream asks for nosym: _refuse_system (:396-475) has no nosym clause, _checked_rotation (:1482-1483) maps None to the identity, and Calculator.get_torque, get_orientation_torque and get_relaxed_orientation (calculator.py:2483-2545) only forward. The kernel is a bare wedge sum: forces/torque.py:_value_and_slopes (:262-271) is sum_k of occupation[ik] * <psi|H(p)|psi> under one jvp, and orientation_torque (:420-425) returns -slopes with no group projection; the grep of that file for nosym, reduced, wedge and symmetr found nothing. The reduced k-set does reach it: builder.py:880-887 builds KPoints.automatic with the magnetic group's rotations and t_rev when nosym is off, and tests/regression/test_anisotropy.py:630-651 runs the same cell without nosym at (0,0,1) on 6 points against 18. So a valid input reaches the path with no refusal.

The finder's trigger does not show the defect, and this must be corrected. co-tetragonal-anisotropy-soc.in is ibrav = 6 with K_POINTS 3 3 2 0 0 0 (lines 5 and 18), so every k-point has kz = 0 or 1/2. For a moment along z the mirror z -> -z is an unprimed operation, it maps each of these k-points onto itself modulo G, and it reverses the two transverse components of an axial vector. Each k-point then gives zero transverse torque on its own, and the wedge sum has no residue. This is the same kind of null as the 45 degree one in notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:168. Two inputs that do reach a residue by the same symmetry argument: (a) the same cell without nosym on a grid with a kz layer the mirror does not fix, for example K_POINTS 3 3 3 0 0 0, with get_orientation_torque at the default rotation or get_torque(angle=0); there the transverse per-k torque transforms like kx kz and the 1/16 wedge keeps one sign of it. (b) the committed grid with the system's own moment at angle1 = 45 in the xz plane and no nosym (the 4-operation, 8-point set that test_anisotropy.py:1610 names): C2y T maps k to (kx, -ky, kz) and reverses the x and z components of the torque, the wedge holds ky = 1/3 with weight two, so get_orientation_torque returns x and z components where the whole grid gives zero, while the y component (the one run_torque reads) is invariant and correct. I did not run either, by the rule of this audit, so the size is unknown.

The record does not have it. notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:168 and :653-662, and the test docstring at test_anisotropy.py:310-319, cover the turned branch only, and the docstring says there is no cell here that would show it. notes/incompat/I78.md is about rotate_moments in the SCF and with_turning_moments, not about these one-shot entry points. docs/guide/anisotropy.md:164-169 gives no nosym limit for a torque at the system's own orientation. The relax_orientation face is real but mild: step one runs on the wedge (anisotropy.py:1957), and a later step is refused by _with_rotation with the nosym message, so the cost is one diagonalisation and an error that already names the cure.

Fix: Move the check to where the wedge sum is differentiated, not where the axis turns: in run_torque and _orientation_torque, before fixed_density_states, refuse when the k-set is reduced (KPoints.reduced) unless the group is the {E, I} of with_turning_moments, since inversion leaves an axial vector alone. The alternative is to complete the three components with the group, as a time-reversal-even axial vector (the average of det(R) R t over the operations), which gives the whole-grid sum; run_torque would then read its number from the three components. Add a test on a 3 3 3 grid against the nosym run.

### `defuqumat/response/shg.py:797`: SHG at nspin=2 does not refuse a fixed filling that stops inside a degenerate shell; the shift current does

Slice `optics`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **confirmed**, known: notes/incompat/I6.md:57-62 records it ('get_shg and get_optical_conductivity at nspin = 2 do not have this refusal ... that is a gap in those two'). It is not in OPEN.md, PLAN.md or the audits, and it is still open at b5b88c5c. The conductivity half is partly covered by the fixed-regime warning of _report_dropped_pairs; SHG has nothing..

With a collinear nspin=2 run under tot_magnetization, fixed occupations can fill a channel to a depth that stops inside a degenerate multiplet. Which member is occupied is then the eigensolver's choice, and every f_n - f_m depends on the rotation inside the shell. The shift current refuses this by name through _require_a_gap_at_the_filling. second_harmonic, require_an_shg_regime and run_shg never call that check. require_an_shg_regime delegates to require_a_velocity_sum_regime, which does not contain it.
