The only cut diagnostic SHG has is _warn_about_the_cut on band_cut_gap, and run_shg builds that from cut_gaps(eigenvalues, (nbnd,)*nspin): the gap at the top of the diagonalised band set, not at the occupation cut. So the gauge-dependent chi^(2) comes back silently. The optical conductivity has the same gap, but there _report_dropped_pairs at least warns in the fixed regime. The rule in scope.md (P147) says the response is refused there.

Trigger: run_shg / Calculator.get_shg on a collinear nspin=2 insulator with occupations='fixed' and a tot_magnetization that leaves one channel holding part of a degenerate shell. This is the I6 example: three electrons in a channel whose valence top is a triplet at Gamma, on a k-set containing Gamma.

```
response/photocurrent.py:589 `_require_a_gap_at_the_filling(calculation, eigenvalues)` (shift_current only); :844-882 the check, `if int(eigenvalues.shape[0]) != 2: return ... raise NotImplementedError("the shift current of a filling that cuts a degenerate multiplet is not defined ...")`; response/shg.py:615-624 `require_an_shg_regime(calculation) ... _warn_about_the_cut(band_cut_gap, ...)` with no filling check; workflows/shg.py `band_cut_gap = float(np.min(cut_gaps(eigenvalues, (nbnd,) * eigenvalues.shape[0])))`
```

Skeptic: I could not refute it. require_an_shg_regime (response/shg.py:797-826) only wraps require_a_velocity_sum_regime, whose four refusals (photocurrent.py:759-799: spiral, wedge, unmeasured Hubbard regimes, a scheme other than 'fixed') do not include the filling check. _require_a_gap_at_the_filling is called once in the package, at photocurrent.py:600 inside the shift current. second_harmonic (shg.py:615-624) calls the regime check, the grid warning and _warn_about_the_cut on band_cut_gap, and workflows/shg.py:134 builds that gap at the top of the band set, cut_gaps(eigenvalues, (nbnd,)*nspin), not at the occupation cut. The occupations then come from calculation.occupations(eigenvalues) (shg.py:656), which at fixed nspin=2 fills each channel to its count, so a count that stops inside a shell occupies whichever member the eigensolver returned. The input is valid and reaches it: scope.md (P147) says such a filling is warned about in the SCF and never refused, and SHG requires occupations = 'fixed', so this is the one occupation scheme SHG admits. Two things make it worse than the finder said. The SCF warning text (scf/driver.py:1887-1895) tells the user "every response on this state do depend on it (the response refuses by name)", which is false for get_shg. And that warning is silent when nbnd equals the channel's count (driver.py:1833-1835), so a run with no band above the cut gets no message at all. The guide has the sentence for the shift current (docs/guide/nonlinear.md:485-487) and not in the SHG paragraph (:489 onward). Recorded only in notes/incompat/I6.md:58-61 ("get_shg and get_optical_conductivity at nspin = 2 do not have this refusal ... that is a gap in those two"); nothing in OPEN.md or PLAN.md. I did not read conductivity.py:780 (_report_dropped_pairs), so I do not confirm the finder's remark about the conductivity's warning.

Fix: Call _require_a_gap_at_the_filling(calculation, eigenvalues) in second_harmonic after the channel axis is restored, with the message's quantity renamed as require_an_shg_regime already does, add the two unit tests the shift current has, and add the sentence to the SHG paragraph of the Limits note. Decide the same for optical_conductivity (I6.md names both).

### `defuqumat/projwfc/projections.py:501`: The atomic projection of a nosym spin spiral pairs 2 nk rows of orbitals with nk rows of states, and the refusal tells the user to take that route

Slice `hubbard-projwfc`, hole, severity `missing-refusal (the symptom is a shape error from the batching layer; read, not executed)`, status **confirmed**, known: new. notes/INCOMPATIBILITIES.md lists no spiral x PDOS item. I50 (b225d9f4) added only the symmetrised-spiral refusal. 48a7b4b0 added the two-sphere splice to angular_momentum.py and not to projections.py. The three AUDIT notes were not grepped for this symbol..

For a spiral, Calculation.basis_kpoints is the doubled list: k + q/2 in rows 0 to nk-1 and k - q/2 in rows nk to 2nk-1 (driver.py:2622 to 2625). atomic_orbitals builds on calc.basis_kpoints, so it returns 2 nk rows. atomic_projections then maps those orbitals against the nk rows of states with map_k, and there is no splice of rows ik and nk + ik. The site angular momenta (angular_momentum.py:481 onward) and the Hubbard projectors (driver.py:3443 onward) both do that splice. map_axis takes the leading length from the first leaf, which is 2 nk, so lax.map or vmap should stop on the mismatch. This was read, not executed. The refusal at :435 fires only for a symmetrised spiral and tells the user to run with nosym = .true. or symmetrize=False, and that is exactly the input that reaches the mismatch. The test docstring at tests/unit/test_spiral_symmetry.py:257 says the atomic projections of a norm-conserving spiral run under nosym, and the code does not back it. A splice alone would also need each component projected on the orbitals of its own sphere, as angular_momentum.py does. The KPM PDOS calls the same atomic_orbitals (kpm/traces) and was not checked.

Trigger: A norm-conserving spin spiral with nosym = .true. (the ZIGZAG cell of tests/unit/test_spiral_symmetry.py with _nosym, or nii-chain-spiral.in), then Calculator.get_pdos() or atomic_projections(calculation, result.wavefunctions). The same happens with use_symmetry on and symmetrize=False, the second escape the refusal names. Neither pdos.py nor calculator.py has a spiral guard (same-line grep only).

```
defuqumat/projwfc/projections.py:352-355 (atomic_orbitals)
    return build_atomic_projectors(
        calc.pseudos, system.structure, system.cell,
        calc.basis.smooth, calc.basis.planewaves, calc.basis_kpoints, ...
projections.py:485-491
    if calculation.memory_mode == "memory" and not calculation.spiral:
        ...
    else:
        blocks = [(np.arange(nk), nk)]
projections.py:501-509
        projectors = build(calculation if whole else calculation.at_rows(rows))
        for spin, states in enumerate(wavefunctions):
            chunk = np.asarray(compiled(
                lambda xs: map_k(one_kpoint, xs, batch=calculation.k_batch),
                (projectors, upload(states, rows, whole))))
driver.py:2622-2625
        self.basis_kpoints = (
            spiral_kpoints(system.kpoints, system.spiral_q, system.cell)
            if self.spiral else system.kpoints
        )
```

Skeptic: The row mismatch is in the code as it stands. atomic_orbitals (defuqumat/projwfc/projections.py:352-374) builds on calc.basis.planewaves and calc.basis_kpoints, and for a spiral driver.py:2622-2625 makes basis_kpoints the doubled list. build_atomic_projectors returns one row per entry of that list (defuqumat/hubbard/projectors.py:336 and :378, jnp.stack over range(nk) of atomic.shape[0]), and for a norm-conserving spinor _spinor_overlap returns psi untouched (driver.py:3411-3412), so the build itself does not stop. The comment at defuqumat/projwfc/angular_momentum.py:481-483 confirms the 2 nk rows for the same builder and the same list, and :512-513 slices conjugate[:nk] and conjugate[nk:]. atomic_projections has no such splice: projections.py:485 sends a spiral to the single whole block, :501 builds 2 nk rows, and :506-508 maps (projectors, states) with map_k, where the states have nk rows (nspin, nk = wavefunctions.shape[:2] at :484). map_axis (defuqumat/batching.py:657-672) takes n from the first leaf, 2 nk, and then calls lax.map, jax.vmap or lax.map with batch_size on a pytree with leading sizes 2 nk and nk; each of the three stops on unequal leading axes. On a CPU the k dial is one, so the branch is lax.map at batching.py:669. Even if it did not stop, each orbital column has both spinor components on the sphere of k + q/2 (or both on k - q/2), which is not the two-sphere projection. Upstream guards checked: the only spiral refusal in projections.py is :435-447, for symmetrize and use_symmetry together, and its message sends the user to nosym or symmetrize=False; grep of spiral in defuqumat/calculator.py shows no refusal near get_pdos (:1102) and none in workflows/pdos.py; unfold_gamma_states returns untouched for a run that is not gamma_only. The records say this route runs: .claude/rules/scope.md lists only the symmetrised projections as refused for a spiral, notes/INCOMPATIBILITIES.md:671 (I50) says projections keep nosym, and the docstring at tests/unit/test_spiral_symmetry.py:257-259 says the atomic projections of a norm-conserving spiral run. No test executes it: test_the_same_consumers_are_not_refused_on_a_nosym_spiral (:286-296) only calls the angular momenta guard, and the spinor-spiral case of tests/unit/test_projector_storage.py:476-490 is about becsum on a lazy projector set. The KPM PDOS, which the finder left open, is safe: kpm/traces.py:169 goes through kpm_setup, and kpm/dos.py:228-232 (require_a_kpm_regime, called at dos.py:400 and :402) refuses a spiral before atomic_orbitals is reached at traces.py:202.

Fix: Short term: refuse a spiral in atomic_projections by name whatever symmetrize is, and correct the message at projections.py:443-448, scope.md and the test docstring. To implement it: build the orbitals for the up component from rows 0 to nk-1 and for the down component from rows nk to 2nk-1 (each component on its own sphere, as angular_momentum.py:481-515 does), splice them into one (nk, 2 npwx, natomwfc) set, and validate against the commensurate supercell as I24 did for the site moments.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1631`: On the ultracell branch, zeeman=True is silently dropped instead of refused

Slice `docs-holes`, hole, severity `missing-refusal (low: the route is experimental, but the drop is silent, not a wrong error message)`, status **confirmed**, known: new (not in OPEN.md or the three audits; the P175 and P176 records list zeeman as refused, without saying which route).

So the documented refusal is never raised on this branch. Either the user gets another error, or, on a projected run that completes, levels without Zeeman and no warning. If it never completes, this is only a wrong error message.

The only refusal is run_landau_levels at defuqumat/workflows/landau.py:151-153, on the Peierls branch. The finder's open question is settled by reading: a facade call on this branch does complete. With `free=True` in **options the facade's `local_only=False` changes nothing (landau_ultracell.py:202, 207, 439-442), and the two remaining raises in the body are the projectability guard (:496-503, passed by the gate's stripe trials at 4e-10 to 5.1e-8, PLAN.md:28823-28830) and the rank check that applies only to `not projected` (:579). A second completing input is graphene with `projectability_tol=None` (Hermiticity 1e-10 under the strict 1e-8, PLAN.md:28848). So `zeeman=True` returns levels with no Zeeman term and no warning, against the unconditional docstring line calculator.py:1622 ("Refused by name: zeeman=True") and the guide's Limits line 303. PLAN.md:28702 says "zeeman=True is refused by the workflow by name", which is true of the Peierls workflow only. The facade test checks it on Peierls only (tests/unit/test_magnetic_facade.py:97-98). The same capture happens to `nlevels` (facade default 40 at :1596, never forwarded, while the workflow has its own `nlevels` at landau_ultracell.py:301 and :585), so `nlevels=10` on this branch returns every level; `kappa`, `dirac`, `geometry`, `width` and `wannier` are dropped the same way, so `geometry="ribbon"` with `route="ultracell"` runs the torus.

Fix: On the ultracell branch refuse `zeeman=True` before the delegation (or forward `zeeman=zeeman` and raise in run_magnetic_ultracell next to refuse_regime at :379), and forward `nlevels` only when the caller gave it (default None in the facade, 40 resolved on the Peierls branch). Add the ultracell case to test_get_landau_levels_refuses_the_regime_of_the_calculator. The body is at the 20-line delegation budget, so the refusal fits best in the workflow.

### `defuqumat/workflows/orbital_magnetization.py:140`: The orbital magnetization of a smeared metal is not refused; only the direct gap at the mesh points is checked

Slice `topology`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **confirmed**, known: new (P64 states the refusal; nothing in OPEN.md or the audits records that it is only the direct-gap check).

The guide (docs/guide/spin-orbit.md:177, "A metal gives an error") and PLAN.md P64 ("Refused by name: ... a metal or any manifold not separated from the band above it") say a metal is refused. The workflow refuses nspin = 2, no spinors, a spiral, US/PAW and the potential-only meta-GGA, and nothing about the occupations. The only guard left is DFTSource._check_gap, which compares e_(nocc+1) - e_nocc at each mesh point with gap_tol = 1e-4 Ry. That is a direct-gap test at a finite set of points, not a test of the filling. In a spinor magnet the crossings of bands N and N+1 are isolated Weyl points or protected lines, so the direct gap at generic mesh points is finite and usually above 1.4 meV. A smeared run then passes and returns M of the lowest nelec bands, which is not the occupied manifold of the SCF state. The -2 mu term is then also not quantized. The repository already has the right test in two places: response/geometry.py:require_an_insulator judges max f(1-f) on the fillings, and workflows/geometry.py:187 refuses when min e_nocc+1 <= max e_nocc over the mesh. Either one would back the documented refusal here.

Trigger: The P64 iodine cell (docs/figures/spin-orbit_bismuthene/iodine.in) with occupations = 'smearing', degauss = 0.005 Ry. P64 records that this puts 0.2 electron in band 8 and that 'the manifold stops being isolated', but the gap above band 7 is 0.16 eV, about 0.0118 Ry, which is far above gap_tol. So get_orbital_magnetization returns a number. The same holds for smeared Ni or Fe with lspinorb on a mesh that does not land within 1e-4 Ry of a node.

```
workflows/orbital_magnetization.py:126-140: `if any(p.is_ultrasoft or p.is_paw for p in pseudos): raise ...` then `source = _source(...)`, `_refuse_potential_only_meta(source, ...)`, `source.gap_tol = gap_tol`, no check of system.occupations; workflows/topology.py:440-444: `gaps = eigenvalues[:, self.nocc] - eigenvalues[:, self.nocc - 1]` ... `if gaps[worst] < self.gap_tol: raise`
```

Skeptic: I read run_orbital_magnetization (defuqumat/workflows/orbital_magnetization.py:99-151). Its refusals are nspin = 2, no spinors, a spiral, US/PAW and the potential-only meta-GGA. Nothing reads system.occupations or the fillings. Calculator.get_orbital_magnetization (calculator.py:2308-2326) and _ground_state (calculator.py:932-947) add only require_converged. The only guard left is DFTSource._check_gap (workflows/topology.py:429-453), which compares e[nocc] - e[nocc-1] with gap_tol at each mesh point. It does see band nocc+1, because nbnd = max(nocc + 2, default_nbnd) at topology.py:347-353, so the check is not skipped, but it is a direct-gap test only. The sister workflow already says this is not enough: workflows/polarization.py:238-258 (_refuse_ungapped) refuses any occupations outside fixed and from_input, and its docstring says the per-k gap check does not cover a smeared occupation. The orbital magnetization never calls it (grep of _refuse_ungapped, require_an_insulator and occupations in the workflow finds nothing). The iodine trigger holds by reading: nelec = 7 gives nocc = 7 (topology.py:522-533), and PLAN.md:12658-12662 records a gap of 0.16 eV (0.0118 Ry, above gap_tol = 1e-4) with 0.2 electron in band 8 at degauss = 0.005. So the call returns a number, while docs/guide/spin-orbit.md:177 says a metal gives an error and PLAN.md:12738 lists a metal as refused. No test asserts the refusal: the only pytest.raises in tests/unit/test_orbital_magnetization.py are two divisions (line 122) and the spiral (line 263). I did not verify the Ni/Fe trigger or the clause about the -2 mu term; an indirect-gap semimetal is the cleaner second case, since its direct gap is finite at every mesh point.

Fix: Add a filling test at the workflow entry, before _source: refuse when max f(1-f) of the SCF occupations is above a small threshold (response/geometry.py:require_an_insulator), or when min e[nocc] <= max e[nocc-1] over the mesh as workflows/geometry.py does. A blanket refusal of smearing would also refuse a smeared insulator with gap much larger than degauss, which is a valid input. Add a test that the guard fires on the smeared iodine cell.

### `defuqumat/topology/links.py:36`: SINGULAR is never used, so the documented raise on a singular link cannot fire

Slice `topology`, hole, severity `doc-drift (missing-refusal only plausible)`, status **confirmed**, known: new (grep of SINGULAR, 'fail loudly' and link_phase over OPEN.md, PLAN.md and the three audits finds nothing).

The module docstring says link_phase and unitarize 'both fail loudly on a singular M', and SINGULAR is documented as the value below which 'a singular value or a determinant is treated as zero and raises'. Nothing reads SINGULAR anywhere in the package. link_phase returns the slogdet sign with no test of logabs, and unitarize returns U V^H with no test of the singular values. For an exactly singular link, slogdet gives sign 0, so string_phase multiplies its product by 0 and returns angle(0) = 0, a silent zero phase. A Wilson loop takes an arbitrary unitary, and an FHS plaquette divides by 0 and gives NaN. For a near-singular link the phase is kept with no warning. DFTSource._check_gap covers a gap that closes at a mesh point. It does not cover a mesh that is too coarse to follow a narrow anticrossing, which is the case the docstring names, and ModelSource has no guard at all. The orbital magnetization reports smallest_determinant, but the polarization, the Wilson loop and FHS report no such number.

Trigger: A ModelSource Chern number, Z2 or polarization string on a model whose occupied state turns by about pi between two neighbouring mesh points (an anticrossing narrower than the mesh step, as in the elkpy graphene case at nloop = 24 quoted in wilson.py). Or any DFT string or loop on a mesh coarse enough that |det M| falls below 1e-8 while the direct gap at each point stays above gap_tol.

```
links.py:22-26 'Both fail loudly on a singular M ... Clipping it would return a number rather than an error'; links.py:36 `SINGULAR = 1.0e-8`; links.py:48-49 `sign, logabs = jnp.linalg.slogdet(matrix)` / `return sign`; links.py:54-55 `u, _, vh = jnp.linalg.svd(...)` / `return u @ vh`; topology/polarization.py:108-109 `total *= np.complex128(link_phase(matrix))` / `return float(np.angle(total))`
```

Skeptic: The dead constant is settled by grep: SINGULAR appears once in the package and the tests, at defuqumat/topology/links.py:36. link_phase (links.py:39-49) returns the slogdet sign and drops logabs, and unitarize (links.py:52-55) drops the singular values, so neither raises, against the docstring at links.py:22-26 and the comment at :35. The five consumers add no guard: topology/polarization.py:108, berry.py:206, invariants.py:206 and :208, wilson.py:168. Only orbital_magnetization.py:259 keeps the log-determinant as a diagnostic. git log -S finds no commit that removed a use, and PLAN.md, OPEN.md and notes/AUDIT*.md do not mention SINGULAR or 'fail loudly'. So the documented raise does not exist. As a missing refusal I downgrade it. The finder's named input (an anticrossing narrower than the mesh step) gives a small determinant, not one below 1e-8, so a raise at SINGULAR would not fire there either. Also a threshold of 1e-8 on the determinant would be wrong for a large manifold: link_phase's own docstring says a healthy 30-band determinant is 1e-10 or smaller. The silent zero phase needs exact orthogonality (slogdet sign 0, then np.angle(0) = 0 in string_phase), which is reachable only on a very coarse symmetric mesh, for example a two-point string of a two-band model where d(k) and d(k+b) are antiparallel. I did not read the FHS plaquette, so the NaN claim is not verified.

Fix: Either delete SINGULAR and the 'fail loudly' paragraph, or enforce it on the smallest singular value of M, not on the determinant, and report the smallest value on the result as the orbital magnetization does with smallest_determinant.

### `defuqumat/workflows/qpi.py:207`: run_qpi without energies= on a fixed-occupation run returns NaN maps instead of a refusal or the midgap fallback

Slice `transport-stm`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **confirmed**, known: new. PLAN.md P157 lists no such limit, and OPEN.md and the audits have no QPI entry..

With energies=None the energy axis is np.asarray(levels['fermi_energy'], dtype=float). A run with occupations='fixed' has fermi_energy None, and NumPy turns None into nan. So the axis is [nan], every denominator is nan, and every map, transform, T-matrix and wraparound is nan, with no error. QPI.fermi_energy comes back None. _refuse_what_has_no_fermi_level does not test for a Fermi level, despite its name (stm.py:866-941). QPIBands keeps only fermi_energy and no homo or lumo, so the midgap fallback that run_stm (_tip_energy) and the transport (_energies) take cannot be taken here. Those two either fall back to midgap with a warning or raise a ValueError. I read this path and did not execute it.

```
defuqumat/workflows/qpi.py:206-208
    energies_axis = np.atleast_1d(
        np.asarray(levels["fermi_energy"] if energies is None else energies,
                   dtype=float))
with levels = {"fermi_energy": states.fermi_energy} at :172, and QPIBands(..., fermi_energy=levels.get("fermi_energy")) at the end of qpi_bands.
```

Skeptic: I followed the level from the occupations to the axis. For occupations='fixed' at nspin = 1 Calculation.occupations returns {'homo', 'lumo', 'cut_gap'} and no 'fermi_energy' (scf/driver.py:6761-6763), and SCFResult.fermi_energy is levels.get('fermi_energy'), None (driver.py:10678). qpi_bands keeps only that key on both branches: levels = {'fermi_energy': result.fermi_energy} (workflows/qpi.py:380) or the occupations' levels (:389), then fermi_energy=levels.get('fermi_energy') (:404). run_qpi builds the axis with np.asarray(levels['fermi_energy'] if energies is None else energies, dtype=float) (:206-208), and NumPy makes None a NaN there. No guard catches it: _refuse_what_has_no_fermi_level (workflows/stm.py:866-941) tests a spiral, fermi_energy_up and a field, not a missing level; the run_qpi door (about :140-165) checks readers, axis, spinors, augmentation and broadening; stm/qpi.py raises only at :141, :230 and :232, none on a non-finite energy. The transport and the STM both fall back to midgap with a warning or raise (workflows/transport.py:1361-1377, stm.py:582-603 and :794-824). The guide says only 'If you do not give them, it is the Fermi level' (docs/guide/stm.md:551-552) and has no Limits line for it. The nspin = 2 fixed run is refused through fermi_energy_up, as the finder says, so the reach is an nspin = 1 fixed-occupation slab; occupations='from_input' returns {} (driver.py:6781-6786) and reaches it too. I did not run it, so the NaN propagation through impurity_maps is read and not measured.

Fix: In run_qpi, when energies is None and the level is None, raise a ValueError that asks for energies= in Ry (a midgap default has no states to scatter); do it before qpi_bands on the SCF branch, and keep homo and lumo on QPIBands if a midgap warning is preferred.

### `defuqumat/io/pwin.py:279`: A comma-separated namelist value list is silently cut to its first value

Slice `xc-io`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **confirmed**, known: new. notes/AUDIT-2026-09-18.md (line 253) closed the bare logical spellings in the same parser, which is a neighbouring defect. The value-list form is not recorded in OPEN.md, PLAN.md or the audits..

Fortran list-directed namelist input lets an array be assigned from a value list, for example `starting_magnetization = 0.5, -0.5`. The bare-token alternative of _ENTRY is the class [^,\n!#], so the value stops at the first comma. _parse_namelist_body walks _ENTRY.finditer(body) and skips any text that does not start with `key =`, so ', -0.5' is thrown away with no error. PwInput.indexed then puts the scalar into slot 1 (pwin.py:133-134) and leaves every other species at its default. The run then starts from a different magnetic state than the one written, and it can converge to it. The blank-separated form, `celldm = 10.2 1.0 1.6`, is kept whole as a string and fails loudly at pwin.py:134 `values[0] = float(raw)`. So only the comma form is silent. The fix is to refuse any unmatched non-blank text left in a namelist body, or to parse the list into indices 1..n.

Trigger: A pw.x file with `starting_magnetization = 0.5, -0.5` (two species, antiferromagnetic start) or `london_c6 = 20.0, 30.0`. pw.x reads both through the Fortran runtime. Here species 2 silently gets 0. I found no use of this form in QE 7.5's own test-suite and example inputs, so it is rare in practice, but it is valid input.

```
pwin.py:279-286 `for match in _ENTRY.finditer(body): ... entries[key] = value` (unmatched text is never inspected); the _ENTRY bare-token branch `(?: (?!...) [^,\n!#] )+`; pwin.py:133-134 `elif raw is not None: values[0] = float(raw)`.
```

Skeptic: The bare-token branch of _ENTRY (pwin.py:77-80) is built from the class [^,\n!#], so the value of `starting_magnetization = 0.5, -0.5` ends at the comma. _parse_namelist_body (:277-287) only walks _ENTRY.finditer(body) and never looks at the text between two matches, so ', -0.5' is dropped without an error. parse_pw_input (:152-170) only strips comments before it, so nothing upstream sees the leftover. PwInput.indexed (:133-134) then puts the scalar in slot 1 and leaves the other species at the default. The indexed spelling `starting_magnetization(1) = 0.5, -0.5`, also valid Fortran, loses the second value the same way. tests/unit/test_pwin.py:101-117 covers blank-separated assignments and :133-138 a comma inside a quoted string, and no test has a value list. AUDIT-2026-09-18.md:253 closed only the bare logical spellings. The same loop also drops any other unmatched text, for example a line that lacks its '='. The form is rare in practice, but pw.x reads it and here the run starts from another magnetic state in silence, so the severity stands.

Fix: In _parse_namelist_body keep the end of the previous match and raise a ValueError that quotes any text between matches that is not blanks or commas. Or parse a comma list into indices 1..n, as the Fortran runtime does.

### `defuqumat/workflows/magnetic_bands.py:268`: The dense fallback of route P is outside the memory estimate that is checked before the run

Slice `magnetic`, hole, severity `memory`, status **confirmed**, known: PLAN.md:29477 and :29686 record the rule ('dense fallback below ndim 20000, stop above') but not its memory, nor that the refusal misses it; new.

run_magnetic_bands refuses by number only the Davidson working set (estimate_bytes, line 234). When Davidson leaves a band unsettled and ndim <= DENSE_LIMIT = 20000, it builds the whole operator densely (dense_matrix: a list of column blocks plus their concatenation, 2 ndim^2 complex), then forms 0.5 (M + M^H) (two more ndim^2 temporaries beside M), then np.linalg.eigh (zheevd: its own copy plus workspace of about 2 ndim^2 x 16 B). That is about 5 ndim^2 x 16 B at the peak. This count comes from reading the code and was not measured. At ndim = 18432 it is 5.4 GB per copy and roughly 27 GB in total, above MEMORY_LIMIT (26 GB) and close to D22's 31 GB. memory_limit never sees it. The fallback also takes a full O(ndim^3) solve on one core where only nbnd values are wanted. The guide says that the memory of one k-point is estimated before the run and that a run above the limit gives an error, so the code does not back that claim on this path. Fix: put the fallback's ndim^2 term into the estimate whenever unconverged='fallback' and ndim <= DENSE_LIMIT, or lower DENSE_LIMIT to what the limit allows. Use scipy.linalg.eigh(subset_by_index=[0, nbnd-1]) on the matrix that is already Hermitian, rather than symmetrising a copy.

Trigger: Calculator.get_magnetic_bands on graphene at 50 Ry (box (12, 12, 64), 9216 points per chemical cell) at flux (1, 2), so ndim = 18432, with any k-point where Davidson ends with unsettled > 0 after max_iterations = 200 (the default unconverged='fallback'). Any cell and q with roughly 8000 < ndim <= 20000 reaches the same path.

```
magnetic_bands.py:234 `refuse_memory(estimate, memory_limit, f"route P at p/q = {p}/{q}, nbnd {nbnd}, ndim {ndim}")` (estimate from estimate_bytes, no ndim^2 term); :268-270 `elif ndim <= DENSE_LIMIT:` / `matrix = dense_matrix(hamiltonian, ik)` / `w, v = np.linalg.eigh(0.5 * (matrix + matrix.conj().T))`; blochgrid.py:115 `DENSE_LIMIT = 20000`; blochgrid.py:922-932 dense_matrix concatenates (ndim, ndim) column blocks
```

Skeptic: I read magnetic_bands.py:233-234: the only refusal is refuse_memory on estimate_bytes, and blochgrid.py:875-882 has only terms linear in ndim (Davidson, FFT buffers, kept states), no ndim^2 term. The fallback at magnetic_bands.py:268-270 calls dense_matrix (blochgrid.py:922-932), which holds the list of column blocks (16 ndim^2 B) and its np.concatenate copy (another 16 ndim^2 B) at the same time. Then 0.5 * (matrix + matrix.conj().T) makes two or three more ndim^2 arrays while matrix stays alive as a local, and np.linalg.eigh adds its work copy, the eigenvector output and the zheevd workspace. My count from the code is 5 to 6 copies of 16 ndim^2 B during eigh, so 27 to 33 GB at ndim = 18432 and more at 20000, above MEMORY_LIMIT = 26e9 (blochgrid.py:112). This was not measured, as the finder says. The path is reachable with valid input: tests/unit/test_magnetic_bloch.py:266 asserts run.solver == ("dense",), and Calculator.get_magnetic_bands forwards **options (calculator.py:1673-1674), so max_iterations or a stalled Davidson reaches it. PLAN.md:29477 records that Davidson did not converge in 200 steps with the first preconditioners, so the stall is not hypothetical. The guide (magnetic-field.md:316) and the facade docstring (calculator.py:1659-1660) both say the memory is estimated before the run and refused above the limit, which this path does not honour. Below ndim of about 8000 the fallback is under 5 GB and harmless, so the defect is the upper half of the allowed range.

Fix: Add the fallback's ndim^2 term to the estimate when unconverged='fallback' and ndim <= DENSE_LIMIT, or refuse the fallback by number when 6 x 16 ndim^2 exceeds memory_limit. Fill one preallocated (ndim, ndim) array in dense_matrix and call scipy.linalg.eigh(subset_by_index=[0, nbnd-1], overwrite_a=True) without the symmetrised copy.

### `defuqumat/ultracell/driver.py:2129`: A meta-GGA ultracell's convergence test leaves out the tau residual that run_scf converges on

Slice `ultracell`, hole, severity `doc-drift (minor: a looser stopping test, not a wrong fixed point)`, status **confirmed**.

The module docstring says conv_thr means the same thing in an ultracell run as in every other run in this package. For a potential-only meta-GGA, run_scf adds QE's tauk_ddot to its accuracy (scf/potential.py:tau_accuracy, the rho_ddot:828 term that scope.md says tau is converged on). In the ultracell, dr2 is _accuracy(new - density) plus, for DFT+U only, ns_accuracy. new_tau - tau_state never enters it. So a TB09 ultracell can stop with the density converged and tau still moving. This is not a silent wrong state like F1, because tau is mixed in the same packed vector, but the stated equivalence of conv_thr does not hold for the meta-GGA case. Either add tau's residual on the box (the |G+Q| analogue of tau_accuracy), or narrow the docstring.

Trigger: Any TB09 or BJ06 ultracell (norm-conserving, nspin=1, nosym) under a modulation, at the default conv_thr=1e-8.

```
defuqumat/ultracell/driver.py:2129 `accuracy, charge_dr2, magnetic_dr2 = _accuracy(new - density, ultracell, cell, g2_inverse, keep)`; :2139 `accuracy += sum(... calculation.ns_accuracy(ns_out[c] - ns_state[c]) ...)` (ns only); docstring :40 `it means ``conv_thr`` means the same thing in an ultracell run as in every other run in this package`.
```

Skeptic: The ultracell's accuracy is built at defuqumat/ultracell/driver.py:2129-2131 from `_accuracy(new - density, ...)` (the charge and magnetic halves, :957-997) and gains only the ns term at :2139-2141; `new_tau - tau_state` enters nothing between :2062 (new_tau built) and :2200 (`converged = accuracy < conv_thr`). run_scf adds the term on purpose, defuqumat/scf/driver.py:9692-9704: `tau_accuracy_term = float(_tau_accuracy(tau_out - tau_state, ...)); accuracy += tau_accuracy_term`, with the comment that tau is an ingredient of the potential that is not a function of the density, so a stopping test built from the density residual alone does not bound it. The module docstring (driver.py:39-41) and OPEN.md:3107-3108 both say conv_thr means the same thing in an ultracell run as in every other run, which is false for this regime. The input is valid and tested: require_an_ultracell_functional (ultracell/potential.py:189-204) lets tb09 and bj06 through at nspin_mag = 1 on a norm-conserving dataset, docs/guide/ultracell.md:458-461 says it runs, and tests/regression/test_ultracell_regimes.py:539 runs it. Nothing records the omission: notes/incompat/I33.md and the PLAN.md P155 I33 row (PLAN.md:26617) speak of the supercell bound only, and the guide's Limits note does not mention the stopping test. The effect is bounded, because tau is mixed in the same packed vector as the density (:2247-2258) and the converged branch takes the output tau (:2280-2281), so the returned state is one iteration's output, stopped at a looser test than the unit cell's. I could not size it by reading; the I33 number (8.144e-3 at 48 bands) is a basis error far above it. PAW is not a second case of this: QE comments paw_ddot out and run_scf does too (scf/driver.py:9648), so the ultracell agrees there.

Fix: Add the box analogue of tau_accuracy (tauk_ddot of new_tau - tau_state over the kept G+Q set, per cell) to accuracy when tau_state is not None, beside the ns term at :2139; or narrow the docstring and OPEN.md Y1's sentence to say the meta-GGA stop is on the density residual alone.

### `defuqumat/wannier/engine.py:17`: engine.py says defuqumat passes bohr and gets spreads in bohr^2, but the workflow passes angstrom

Slice `wannier`, hole, severity `doc-drift (minor)`, status **confirmed**.

The module docstring of engine.py says the engine runs in the unit of the reciprocal lattice it is given, 'defuqumat passes bohr, so spreads come out in bohr^2'. run_wannier passes bg / BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM and at * BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM, so the engine runs in angstrom and 1/angstrom, as the workflow docstring says ('The engine runs in Wannier90's own units, angstrom and eV'). The workflow then divides the spreads by a2 and the centres by BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM. The code is right and the engine docstring is wrong. A reader who calls run_engine directly and trusts it would mis-scale conv_tol and kmesh_tol by 0.28 or 3.6.

Trigger: Reading or calling defuqumat.wannier.engine.run_engine directly with a kmesh built in bohr, as its docstring suggests.

```
defuqumat/wannier/engine.py:17 `was given in (defuqumat passes bohr, so spreads come out in bohr^2) and the` ; defuqumat/workflows/wannier.py `kmesh = kmesh_get(points.T, bg / BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM)` and `real_lattice=at * BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM` ; `spreads=result.spreads / a2`
```

Skeptic: engine.py:16-17 says 'defuqumat passes bohr, so spreads come out in bohr^2'. The only caller in the package passes 1/angstrom and angstrom: workflows/wannier.py:450-451 (`# The engine's reciprocal lattice in 1/A` and `kmesh_get(points.T, bg / BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM)`), :592 and :596 (`site_cart ... * BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM`, `real_lattice=at * BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM`), and the result is converted back at :618-625 (`spreads=result.spreads / a2`, `centres = result.centres / BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM`). The workflow docstring (workflows/wannier.py:13-17) and PLAN.md:27033-27034 ('run in Wannier90's units (A, eV; fable's decision)') both say angstrom, and the unit tests build the kmesh in angstrom too (tests/unit/test_wannier.py:167). So the code and the record agree and only the engine docstring is stale. Nothing computed is wrong; the risk is a direct caller of run_engine who scales conv_tol or kmesh_tol for bohr.

Fix: Replace the parenthesis at engine.py:17 by 'defuqumat passes angstrom, as Wannier90, so spreads come out in A^2 and run_wannier converts them to bohr^2'.

### `defuqumat/workflows/magnons.py:398`: _locate's crossing is said to be free of broadening, but it reads X_0 at omega + i eta

Slice `magnetism-response`, hole, severity `doc-drift`, status **confirmed**, known: new. PLAN.md:12409 repeats the claim and does not record the drift..

_locate's docstring (magnons.py:456) and the comment on the method field (:95) say the magnon is found as the root of lambda_max(omega) = 1 and that no broadening enters it. PLAN.md:12409 says the same. But run_spin_susceptibility gives every non-static frequency the imaginary part `broadening` (default 0.002 Ry), and _locate takes its crossing from x0[nstatic:], so the eigenvalues it interpolates are those of X_0(omega + i eta) F. Below the Stoner onset that is no longer Hermitian, and the real part of lambda_max moves at second order in eta, with the curvature of lambda(omega). This is largest near the onset, where the dispersion is read. Only the static points are unbroadened. I did not measure the size. The fix is either a second, unbroadened set of frequencies for the crossing, or a docstring that states the O(eta^2) shift.

Trigger: Any run_spin_susceptibility or run_magnon_dispersion with interacting=True, the default broadening=0.002 Ry, and a mode below the Stoner continuum, which is the 'crossing' route.

```
magnons.py:398  imaginary = np.concatenate([np.zeros(nstatic), np.full(len(frequencies), broadening)])
magnons.py:~427  energy, method = _locate(frequencies, x0[nstatic:], kernel, x[nstatic:, 0, 0], enhancement, crossing_points)
magnons.py:456  "one-dimensional root on a handful of frequencies -- no broadening enters it"
```

Skeptic: The code evaluates the crossing at omega + i eta. magnons.py:398 gives every user frequency the imaginary part broadening (default 0.002 Ry, :239), :400-403 passes grid + 1j * imaginary to transverse_response with broadening = 0.0 (spinchi0.py:629 takes complex frequencies as they are), and :427-430 hands x0[nstatic:] to _locate, whose samples are eigvals(x0[i] @ kernel).real.max() (:480-482). Only the static rows (index 0, and the Larmor point) are at eta = 0. Three texts say otherwise: the method field comment at :94-95 ("exact and needs no broadening"), the broadening argument at :280-281 ("the width of the spectral function's peak and nothing else; the pole is found without it"), and _locate's docstring at :453-457. PLAN.md:12408-12409 says X_0 is Hermitian at eta = 0 and that no broadening enters the root, which is true of the method and not of what the code evaluates. run_magnon_dispersion forwards **kwargs to run_spin_susceptibility (magnons.py:570-597), so the default applies there too. No test checks that the crossing is independent of eta: the tests in test_magnons.py and test_magnon_regimes.py that pass broadening use 0.0 or 1e-3 for other purposes. The guide page magnons.md and OPEN.md do not record it. For an eigenvalue that is analytic in omega, the real part moves at second order in eta, so the shift of the root is small far below the onset and grows toward it; in a one-pole model it scales as eta^2 over the distance to the pole. I put no number on it, since nothing was run. It should also be weighed against the linear interpolation between the crossing_points samples (:484-488), which already makes "exact" too strong. The severity stays doc-drift.

Fix: Either correct the three docstrings and PLAN.md to say the crossing is read at omega + i eta, with a shift of second order in eta that grows near the Stoner onset, or append the crossing_points sample frequencies to the unbroadened static block and read lambda_max there. The second costs crossing_points more frequencies in the X_0 sum, and on a finite k-mesh the unbroadened X_0 has its poles on the real axis, so it is safe only below the lowest spin-flip pair.

### `defuqumat/response/piezo.py:431`: Polar-crystal guard says PAW is refused earlier, but that refusal is a no-op since I89

Slice `strain`, hole, severity `minor (doc-drift in one docstring sentence; the refusal fires and the user guide is right)`, status **confirmed**, known: new (I89 and I90 in PLAN.md record the lift and the polar NC case, not this docstring).

require_a_measured_polar_crystal's docstring says that PAW is refused earlier by require_a_measured_dataset. That function has done nothing since 2026-10-19 (I89 lifted the PAW refusal). A polar PAW crystal is therefore refused only by this guard, because is_ultrasoft is is_augmented and is true for PAW. The message then names 'an ultrasoft dataset' and gives the S-in-the-Berry-overlaps reason, and no number was ever measured for PAW in a polar class. The refusal still fires, so no wrong number comes out. What drifted is the record of which guard does the refusing for PAW, plus a message that names the wrong dataset.

Trigger: Calculator.get_piezoelectric_tensor() on a polar crystal (for example the mm2 AlAs of I90, As at a(1/4,1/4,0.29)) with the PAW datasets of tests/data/qe/alas-piezo-paw.in

```
piezo.py ~431 `symmetry. PAW is refused earlier, by :func:`require_a_measured_dataset`.`; piezo.py:474-498 `def require_a_measured_dataset(...)` whose body is comments only ('nothing is left to refuse'); piezo.py ~433-447 `if not calculation.is_ultrasoft: return` / `raise NotImplementedError("the piezoelectric tensor of a polar crystal with an ultrasoft dataset is not implemented ...")`
```

Skeptic: The stale sentence is piezo.py:430, `PAW is refused earlier, by :func:`require_a_measured_dataset``. That function's docstring opens `Nothing since 2026-10-19: the PAW half was lifted by a measurement` (piezo.py:474-476) and I found no `raise` in its body (lines 500-545). The call order at piezo.py:755-757 is require_a_measured_dataset then require_a_measured_polar_crystal, so a polar PAW crystal is stopped only by the second one: `_Regime.is_ultrasoft` is `is_augmented(pseudos, system.structure)` (piezo.py:694), which is `any(pseudos[t].is_ultrasoft ...)` (augmentation.py:1188), and the project treats PAW as is_ultrasoft everywhere (elastic.py:465-467). So no wrong number comes out. The claim that the message names the wrong dataset is only partly right: it opens with `with an ultrasoft dataset` (piezo.py:438-439) but gives the reason `for an augmented dataset the Berry overlaps carry S` (piezo.py:443-444). The module docstring at piezo.py:88-92 says `An augmented dataset on a polar crystal is still refused`, and docs/guide/piezo.md:298 says `A polar crystal with an ultrasoft or PAW dataset gives an error`, both correct. The guard is tested with the ultrasoft message in tests/unit/test_piezo_machinery.py:125-149; I did not check whether one of those cases is a PAW dataset.

Fix: Replace the sentence at piezo.py:430 by one saying that PAW is refused here as well since I89 emptied require_a_measured_dataset, and write `an ultrasoft or PAW dataset` in the first line of the message (the test at test_piezo_machinery.py:125 matches on that string and moves with it).

### `docs/guide/magnetic-field.md:313`: The Limits note says the ultracell route needs nbnd, but the code takes trials= and N= in place of nbnd

Slice `docs-holes`, hole, severity `minor (doc-drift, recorded as deliberate)`, status **confirmed**, known: PLAN.md P176, section "Route U step 3, the projected trial basis (u3, 2026-11-08)", records that the facade forwards trials and N. New: the user guide page and its Limits note were not updated for it..

The Limits bullet says route="ultracell" "needs `nbnd`", and the guide never names `trials` or `N`. Since 0d535fdb (2026-11-08) the facade guard accepts `N` in place of `nbnd`, and run_magnetic_ultracell has a projected-trial branch (trials= with N=, P176 step 3). The facade docstring advertises that branch, but the user guide does not. The last commit to the guide (8b23f971, 2026-11-07) is older than the facade change. So a reader of the guide cannot find the step 3 route, and the bullet states a requirement the code no longer has. I did not verify that the sentence "gives an error on every case we ran" is now false through the facade. The free-problem gate of P176 was run through the workflow, and the facade hard-codes local_only=False on this call.

Trigger: calc.get_landau_levels(route="ultracell", flux=(1, 10), trials=..., N=64) passes the facade guard, which the guide says needs nbnd. The guide has no section that names trials or N.

```
docs/guide/magnetic-field.md:313-315: "The magnetic ultracell (`route=\"ultracell\"`) gives an error on every case we ran, ... It takes only `flux`, $q \le 100$, and needs `nbnd`."  defuqumat/calculator.py:1630: `if B is not None or not flux or flux[1] > 100 or nbnd is None and "N" not in options:`  calculator.py docstring of get_landau_levels: "With ``trials=`` and ``N=`` (forwarded by name) in place of ``nbnd`` it runs on the projected trial basis (P176, step 3)."  defuqumat/workflows/landau_ultracell.py:317-318: `trials=None,` / `N: int | None = None,`
```

Skeptic: The drift is real. docs/guide/magnetic-field.md:313-315 says the ultracell route "needs `nbnd`" and "gives an error on every case we ran", and the page never names `trials` or `N` (grep of the page: only lines 56-61 and 313 mention the route). The facade guard at defuqumat/calculator.py:1630 accepts `N` in place of `nbnd`, the docstring at calculator.py:1617-1620 advertises `trials=` and `N=`, and for a projected run the workflow refuses `nbnd` (defuqumat/workflows/landau_ultracell.py:385-387), which is the opposite of the Limits bullet. The sentence "gives an error on every case we ran" is also no longer exact: the free Landau gate of step 3 completes (PLAN.md:28823-28830, 1 - p at most 5.1e-8 under the 1e-4 guard at landau_ultracell.py:496, Hermiticity 3e-12), and by reading the same call passes the facade, since `free=True` goes through **options and wins over the facade's `local_only=False` at landau_ultracell.py:202, 207 and 439-442, and a given state with `free=True` is not refused (:428-433). What lowers it: plans/orbital-field/u3.md:281 records the choice, "the guide and the README are unchanged, since acceptance 2 is not passed", and on a real crystal with the defaults the route still refuses (graphene, 1 - p of p_z 4.1e-3, PLAN.md:28831-28836). So the finder's "known: new" is wrong for the omission, but the facade docstring and the guide now disagree and the bullet states a requirement the code inverts.

Fix: Change the Limits bullet to say that the hybrid basis needs `nbnd` and gives an error, and that `trials=` with `N=` (no `nbnd`) runs the projected basis, which passes the free Landau problem and is refused on graphene at the projectability guard. One sentence in the route list at lines 56-61 with the measured 1 - p.

### `README.md:845`: The README row for the Z2 invariants in 3D names only get_z2, which computes one plane

Slice `docs-holes`, hole, severity `minor (doc-drift, table row only)`, status **confirmed**, known: new (notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:675 names get_z2_3d, but only for the conv_thr override, which is unrelated).

The feature-table row claims the Z2 invariants "in 2D and 3D" with the single call `calc.get_z2()`. get_z2 is the 2D invariant of one plane. The four 3D indices come from get_z2_3d, which the table does not name. The README prose at :661-662 names both calls, so the defect is the table row only. A reader who follows the table for a 3D crystal gets one plane's nu.

Trigger: A reader computing the strong and weak indices of a 3D topological insulator from the feature table.

```

```

Skeptic: README.md:845 reads "| **Z2 invariants** in 2D and 3D | `calc.get_z2()` | | |", while get_z2 is "The 2D Z2 invariant of one plane" (calculator.py:2581-2582) and the four 3D indices are get_z2_3d (calculator.py:2590-2591). The prose at README.md:661-662 and the guide (docs/guide/topology.md:448-452) name both calls, so only the table row is short. Other rows of the same table list every call of a feature (README.md:827, 841), so naming one call here is not the table's convention. A set-difference audit of get_* names against the README does not catch it, since get_z2_3d appears in the prose. No guard is involved, and nothing in OPEN.md or the audits records it (notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:675 is about conv_thr).

Fix: Write the call cell of README.md:845 as `calc.get_z2()`, `calc.get_z2_3d()`.

### `defuqumat/solvers/davidson.py:617`: The subspace tangent width eta becomes ethr, not the 1e-5 empty-band unit, when a per-band ethr vector has no empty band

Slice `hamiltonian`, hole, severity `doc-drift (latent hole, no wrong number reachable at b5b88c5c)`, status **confirmed**, known: new; PLAN.md P148 (around line 25911) records the rule as 'empty_band_threshold(ethr) for a scalar ethr and the per-band vector's largest entry otherwise' and '1e-4 Ry at the defaults', without noting that the vector's largest entry can be the bare ethr.

P148 regularises the mixing kernel of Davidson's subspace eigh at eta and says eta is the solver's own unit, max(5 ethr, 1e-5). The code gets that value only for a scalar ethr. For a vector it takes max(ethr), and the comment says the largest entry 'is that unit already'. That is true only when at least one band is btype 0. band_thresholds gives every band the bare scheduled ethr in three cases: on the first SCF iteration (wg=None), with diago_full_acc=True, and on an insulator run at nbnd = nocc, where no band is below the 0.01 occupation. In those cases eta equals ethr, as low as 1e-13. The kernel bound 1/(2 eta) is then about 5e12 instead of about 5e4, so the protection is in practice off: a multiplet split only by solver noise gets the 1/noise rotation tangent that P148 measured as NaN at ethr = 1e-13. The primal is not affected, because eta enters only the custom_jvp. Today no differentiated consumer takes the vector route: ScfResidual stores a scalar ethr (scf/residual.py:163) and fixed_density_ethr is scalar. So this is a latent hole, and the comment and PLAN P148's '1e-4 Ry at the defaults' claim more than the code backs. The fix is one line: use jnp.maximum(jnp.max(ethr), EMPTY_ETHR_FLOOR), or pass eta explicitly from the scalar the schedule started from.

Trigger: Any Davidson call that gets band_thresholds' (nspin, nk, nbnd) vector with every band occupied: NC silicon at nbnd=4 (nocc) in run_scf, any cell on SCF iteration 1, or diago_full_acc=True. Here eta = ethr (1e-2 down to 1e-13) instead of 1e-5. It becomes a wrong derivative only if a jvp or grad is taken through such a call, and no current consumer does that (the Newton residual uses a scalar ethr).

```
defuqumat/solvers/davidson.py:612-620: "# the solver's own unit, ``empty_ethr``, ... and the per-band vector's largest entry is that unit already (``cegterg.f90:129``)."  eta = ( jnp.maximum(5.0 * jnp.asarray(ethr), EMPTY_ETHR_FLOOR) if jnp.ndim(ethr) == 0 else jnp.max(jnp.asarray(ethr)) ); defuqumat/scf/driver.py:356-357: "``wg = None`` -- the **first** SCF iteration ... gives every band ``ethr``"; driver.py:374-376 diago_full_acc=True "leaves btype all ones, so every band is held to ethr".
```

Skeptic: Confirmed as code and comment, not as a wrong derivative. The line is as quoted: defuqumat/solvers/davidson.py:617-620 takes eta = maximum(5 ethr, EMPTY_ETHR_FLOOR) for a scalar and jnp.max(ethr) for a vector, and the comment at 612-616 says the vector's largest entry 'is that unit already'. band_thresholds (defuqumat/scf/driver.py:407-409) returns the flat ethr when wg is None or diago_full_acc is True, and at 418-420 only bands with occupancy below 0.01 get empty_band_threshold. The vector is sliced per k-point (davidson.py:1169, row = ethr[ik]), so the trigger is any k-point whose row has no empty band: an insulator at nbnd = nocc, a metal with a tight nbnd at that k, or diago_full_acc=True. There eta equals the bare ethr, down to ETHR_MIN = 1e-13 (davidson.py:201), and the kernel bound 1/(2 eta) of subspace.py:66 is 5e12 instead of 5e4. So the comment is false on valid inputs. I correct two points of the finder. First, the wg=None trigger is harmless: a fresh run starts at ETHR_INIT = 1e-2 (driver.py:309), which is above the 1e-5 floor, and a resume passes wg (driver.py:9191-9200), so only diago_full_acc and an all-occupied row late in the SCF reach a small eta. Second, there are two more vector callers the finder did not list, both with a largest entry that is not the empty-band unit: workflows/magnetic_bands.py:251-259 (max(ethr, buffer_ethr)) and magnetic/fold.py:226 with thresholds from fold.py:298-299 and 389. What is not reachable is the wrong derivative. I listed every call of diagonalize, stream_diagonalize and davidson_eigensolver_all under defuqumat/. The only rank-3 or rank-1 ethr comes from the run_scf loop (driver.py:9412, passed at 9437-9477) and from the two magnetic host loops, and all three are Python loops that read results on the host (np.zeros buffers, int(left[0])), so no jvp or grad goes through them. Every differentiated or response consumer passes a scalar: scf/residual.py:337, 427 and 615 use self.ethr (declared float at residual.py:163), response/electrostriction.py:362-366, response/phononq.py:174, workflows/nscf.py:292, workflows/topology.py:403, workflows/landau_ultracell.py:444, magnetic/blochgrid.py:1006. eta enters only the custom_jvp of _eigh_regularised (subspace.py:79-87), so the primal of every run is unchanged. PLAN.md P148 (lines 25912-25916) describes the rule as the code has it, and its '1e-4 Ry at the defaults' is right for the scalar route, the only one that is differentiated; it does not record that the vector's largest entry can be the bare ethr. OPEN.md and notes/DEGENERACY-NEXT.md do not record it. tests/unit/test_subspace_eigh_tangent.py passes eta by hand (lines 51, 68, 110, 127), so nothing tests the vector branch.

Fix: Replace the vector branch by jnp.maximum(jnp.max(jnp.asarray(ethr)), EMPTY_ETHR_FLOOR) at davidson.py:619 and correct the comment at 612-616 (the largest entry is the empty-band unit only when the row has a btype 0 band). Add one unit test that the vector route with a flat 1e-13 row gives a tangent bounded by 1/EMPTY_ETHR_FLOOR.

### `defuqumat/topology/polarization.py:256`: Polarization.total_phase is documented as reduced onto [-quantum/2, quantum/2) but is not reduced

Slice `topology`, hole, severity `doc-drift (minor)`, status **confirmed**, known: new (the only total_phase hit in the audits, notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1488, is about the 2 pi of the magnetoelectric contraction).

The field docstring says the total is reduced onto [-quantum/2, quantum/2). run_polarization deliberately does not reduce it (to match the branch pw.x prints), so electronic plus ionic can lie anywhere in [-2, 2) at nspin = 1 with quantum 2, and in [-1, 1) with quantum 1. A user who trusts the docstring and compares total_phase, polarization or polarization_si between two geometries is not on one fixed branch, and can be off by a whole quantum.

Trigger: Any run_polarization or get_polarization call where the folded electronic phase plus the folded ionic phase leaves [-q/2, q/2). AlAs is an example: ionic phase 0.25 and an electronic phase near -0.25 stay inside, but a shifted atom or another species can move the sum outside.

```
topology/polarization.py:256-257 `#: Their sum, reduced onto ``[-quantum/2, quantum/2)``.` / `total_phase: float`; workflows/polarization.py:210-215 `# Not reduced again. ... Folding here would put a value on a different branch from the one pw.x prints` / `total = float(strings.total + ionic)`
```

Skeptic: The field comment at defuqumat/topology/polarization.py:256 says the total is reduced onto [-quantum/2, quantum/2). The code says the opposite in so many words: workflows/polarization.py:210-215 'Not reduced again' and total = float(strings.total + ionic). The range follows from reading: at nspin = 1 the channel is folded on 1.0 and then doubled (topology/polarization.py:176-181), so the electronic phase alone spans [-1, 1), and the ionic phase is folded on its own quantum (:207-213). For an odd-valence cell the quantum is 1 and the total can reach [-1.5, 1.5); any such cell with a channel phase beyond 1/4 leaves the documented interval. Even the committed silicon test sits on the excluded edge: tests/regression/test_polarization.py:139-140 asserts |ionic| = 1 and |total| = 1 at quantum 2, and _fold(1.0, 2.0) returns +1.0 because rint rounds the half to even. The tests pin the unreduced value against pw.x (0.99030 mod 2 for SiC at :234, 0.24074 for AlAs at :262), so the code is the intended behaviour and the docstring is what is wrong. The harm is small: the class docstring already says every phase is defined modulo its quantum and that only a difference is physical. It does matter for F3, where a consumer subtracts two totals without reducing.

Fix: Change the comment at topology/polarization.py:256 to say the sum is not reduced, is the branch pw.x prints, and that a difference must be reduced modulo quantum by the caller. Do not fold in the code.

### `defuqumat/transport/momentum.py:195`: The incoherent column of the momentum transport decides a block at 1e-6 on SCF states, while PLAN says it decides none

Slice `transport-stm`, hole, severity `minor (diagnostic column) plus doc-drift in PLAN.md P149`, status **confirmed**, known: new. PLAN.md:26077 says the opposite ('run_momentum_transport decides no block and does not take it'). AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1511 mentions channel_basis in momentum_weights only as a cost item..

_assemble_momentum calls momentum_weights with eigenvalues= (workflows/transport.py:1119-1121) and no tol. momentum_weights then builds channel_basis at DEGENERACY_TOL = 1e-6 to diagonalise S^exit inside each multiplet for the incoherent column. On the SCF's own states the empty bands are converged only to the 1e-5 floor. This is the same margin P149 fixed for the vertical map. So the incoherent column, and the interference read from weight minus incoherent, can depend on the eigensolver's basis inside a solver-split pair. PLAN.md P149 says run_momentum_transport decides no block and does not take degeneracy_tol. That statement is false for this column.

Trigger: run_momentum_transport (Calculator.get_momentum_transport) on the SCF's own states (no grid=), with a multiplet near the tip energy split by the solver by more than 1e-6 Ry. The MomentumTransport.incoherent column is the number affected. The run_momentum_transport signature has no degeneracy_tol, so a caller cannot fix it.

```
defuqumat/transport/momentum.py:195-196
    u = channel_basis(exit_overlaps, np.asarray(eigenvalues),
                      DEGENERACY_TOL if tol is None else float(tol))
defuqumat/workflows/transport.py:1119-1121
    here = momentum_weights(exit_gram, tip_gram, kweights,
                            np.real(weights),
                            eigenvalues=eigenvalues[ispin])
```

Skeptic: The path is real. _assemble_momentum calls momentum_weights(exit_gram, tip_gram, kweights, np.real(weights), eigenvalues=eigenvalues[ispin]) at workflows/transport.py:1119-1121 with no tol, and momentum.py:195-196 then takes DEGENERACY_TOL. The signature has tol=None (momentum.py:107-108), so the knob exists and is not connected. run_momentum_transport has no degeneracy_tol (signature at :336 onward, and grep finds the keyword only at :118 and :282-290), and its SCF branch is reachable: resolve = kpoints is not None (:457), and without grid= the run's own eigenvalues and wavefunctions are used (:460-468). So the incoherent column is decided at 1e-6 on states whose empty bands are promised only to 1e-5, and PLAN.md's sentence 'run_momentum_transport decides no block and does not take it' (P149, about line 26077) is false for this column. What I could not settle by reading is how often a pair in the window is split by more than 1e-6: P149 measured 3e-7 under 1e-6. The usual route for W(k) is grid=, where the default conv_thr resolves to 1e-6 and nothing changes; a loose conv_thr on that route raises 5 ethr above 1e-6 and reaches the same gap. weight, tersoff_hamann and bare are not affected. The same Schur limit as in F1 applies. AUDIT-2026-09-20:1511 mentions this channel_basis only as a cost item.

Fix: Give run_momentum_transport the shared degeneracy_tol, resolve it with _resolved_degeneracy_tol as the vertical map does, and pass it as tol= to momentum_weights; correct the P149 sentence in PLAN.md.

### `tests/unit/test_magnetic_ultracell.py:524`: P176 step 3: the projected-trial levels are only bounded from below, so an upward error passes

Slice `tests`, hole, severity `test-gap`, status **confirmed**, known: new; the gate number is in PLAN.md P176 "Route U step 3" and notes/ORBITAL-FIELD-NEXT.md:431, without a test that holds it. I did not find a step 3 test list or a "what is left" line after PLAN.md:28850 that records this..

`test_the_projected_levels_fall_monotonically_in_J_and_stay_above_the_ladder` prints `levels/B` but asserts only that the J = 2 levels are at or below the J = 1 levels, that the lowest one moves by more than 1e-6, and that every level is above the exact free ladder. Any defect that adds a positive semidefinite term to the projected H (a doubled diamagnetic piece, a wrong weight in the overlap of the projected hybrid states) keeps Hermiticity, the nesting in J and the lower bound, so all three assertions pass. The slow windowed test (test_magnetic_ultracell_slow.py:61-62) is one-sided in the same way. The number that PLAN P176 calls the passing gate, the n = 0 error of 3.24e-2, 4.35e-3 and 1.8e-7 B_ph at J = 1, 2, 3, is asserted by no test, fast or slow. The field kernels have their own two-sided check on an analytic state (line 227), so the hole is the projected-basis assembly (`projected_hybrid`, the window, S), not the kernels.

Trigger: `run_magnetic_ultracell(..., flux=(1, 2), free=True, trials=StripeTrials(J=2, sigma=0.5), N=48, kmesh=(1, 1))` on the test's small graphene cell (the `_free_projected` helper), where a two-sided bound on the n = 0 level against `free_landau_levels` would be the check.

```
tests/unit/test_magnetic_ultracell.py:541 `assert np.all(r2.levels[:, :n] <= r1.levels + 1e-10)`; :542 `assert np.any(r2.levels[:, 0] < r1.levels[:, 0] - 1e-6)`; :548 `assert np.min(r.levels[:, :m] - ref[None, :m]) > -1e-8`; PLAN.md:28822-28823 "the n = 0 error is 3.24e-2, 4.35e-3, 1.8e-7 B_ph at J = 1, 2, 3".
```

Skeptic: The three assertions are as quoted: tests/unit/test_magnetic_ultracell.py:541 (`r2.levels[:, :n] <= r1.levels + 1e-10`), :542 (the J = 2 level moves by more than 1e-6) and :548 (`np.min(r.levels[:, :m] - ref[None, :m]) > -1e-8`), all one-sided from below, and the levels are only printed at :535-537. The slow file has no projected run at all (a grep for `trials=` in tests/ hits only the unit file), and its windowed test on bare hybrid states is also one-sided (tests/regression/test_magnetic_ultracell_slow.py:61). No test holds PLAN.md:28822-28823 (3.24e-2, 4.35e-3, 1.8e-7 B_ph). The hole is narrower than the finder wrote it, because two two-sided checks exist: the field kernels on an analytic state (:227, B_ph and 3 B_ph to round-off) and the projected assembly at zero flux with J = N (:580-598, `worst < 1e-10` against the bands, with S and both `h0`). So the two example defects of the claim (a doubled diamagnetic piece, a wrong weight in S) are caught there unless they are specific to the window or to p != 0. What no test bounds from above is the composition: window (3, 4) on, field on, projected trials, through `run_magnetic_ultracell`. The valid input is the test's own `_free_projected` helper (:514-521, flux (1, 2), N = 48), which passes the projectability guard (:564). `plans/orbital-field/u3.md` "What is left" (:270-281) does not list the missing assertion. Priority is low: P176 is open as a measured negative result, and on graphene the route is refused by the guard.

Fix: In the same test, read the n = 0 error of J = 1 and J = 2 at 1/2 once (it is already printed) and assert an upper bound on `(levels[:, 0] - ref[0]) / b_ph` with a margin; this adds no run. The 1/10 gate itself (N = 64, kmesh (2, 2), 120 to 215 s a run by u3.md) belongs in the slow file with PLAN's three numbers.

### `defuqumat/kpm/dos.py:428`: spin_resolved with trace='exact' draws random vectors anyway, while the result says exact with n_random = 0

Slice `kpm`, hole, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**.

spin_resolved_dos takes no trace argument. spin_moments always draws n_random random phases. With trace='exact' the scalar DOS is the exact trace, but spin_dos is a stochastic estimate with its full noise. The result records trace='exact' and n_random=0, so it reads as noiseless. The control-variate path refuses the exact trace by name, and this one is neither refused nor labelled.

Trigger: run_kpm_dos(..., trace='exact', spin_resolved=True) on a small spinor cell

```
dos.py:358 `spin_resolved_dos(hamiltonians, weights, centre, half, n_moments, n_random, seed, kernel, energies, batch)` (no trace); moments.py:185 `start = random_phases(jax.random.fold_in(key, ik), live, n_random, dtype)`; dos.py:365 `n_random=n_random if trace == "stochastic" else 0`
```

Skeptic: run_kpm_dos calls `spin_resolved_dos(hamiltonians, weights, centre, half, n_moments, n_random, seed, kernel, energies, batch)` (dos.py:358-359) with no trace argument. spin_moments always draws random phases (moments.py:185, `random_phases(jax.random.fold_in(key, ik), live, n_random, dtype)`) and has no kind argument. With trace='exact' the scalar DOS comes from unit vectors (dos.py:206-213), while spin_dos is still an estimate from n_random vectors. The result then records `n_random=n_random if trace == "stochastic" else 0` (dos.py:365) and trace='exact', so nothing on the object says the spin DOS is noisy. There is also no error array for spin_dos in any mode. The docstring (dos.py:322-323) and the guide (dos.md:445-447) say 'the same random vectors', which is not true for the exact trace. No refusal exists; the control variate has one for the same combination (dos.py:452-454).

Fix: Give spin_moments the exact start block (unit_vectors) when trace='exact', or refuse spin_resolved with trace='exact' by name. Add a spin_dos_error from the per-vector samples.

### `defuqumat/kpm/traces.py:399`: run_kpm_unfolded has no even check on n_moments, so an odd value fails as a shape error

Slice `kpm`, hole, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**.

kpm_moments (dos.py) and _common (traces.py:159) refuse an odd n_moments by name, because the doubling trick gives moments two at a time. run_kpm_unfolded calls interval_and_moments and chebyshev_moments directly with n_steps = n_moments // 2 - 1. An odd N then gives N - 1 moments, while _evaluate builds N kernel factors, so `moments[..., :n_moments] * factors` fails to broadcast. The user gets a broadcasting error and not the named refusal.

Trigger: calc.get_kpm_unfolded(primitive_lattice, kpoints, n_moments=401)

```
traces.py:399-400 `chebyshev_moments(hamiltonian, centre, half, key, n_moments // 2 - 1, starts.shape[1], kind="given", ...)` with no parity check in run_kpm_unfolded; compare traces.py:159 `if n_moments is not None and n_moments % 2: raise ValueError(...)`
```

Skeptic: run_kpm_unfolded (traces.py:340-412) has no parity check and does not go through _common, where the check lives (traces.py:159-160). interval_and_moments returns a given n_moments unchanged (dos.py:418-419). chebyshev_moments is called with n_moments // 2 - 1 steps (traces.py:399) and recursion returns 2 (n_steps + 1) moments (moments.py:60, 75), so an odd N gives N - 1. _evaluate builds N factors (dos.py:169; the kernels use np.arange(n_moments), kernels.py:54, 80, 106) and chebyshev_profile multiplies them, profile.py:52: `coefficients = jnp.asarray(moments) * jnp.asarray(factors)`. Shapes (..., N-1) and (N,) do not broadcast for N >= 3. The error comes after the whole recursion has been paid for, and check_moments passes first. Calculator.get_kpm_unfolded forwards n_moments (calculator.py:1070-1073). The default route is safe, since moment_count returns an even number (kernels.py:145). I call it a missing named refusal, not a crash of a valid input: an odd count is documented as invalid for the DOS (dos.py:300).

Fix: Add the check of traces.py:159-160 at the top of run_kpm_unfolded, or move it into interval_and_moments so that every caller inherits it.

### `defuqumat/magnetic/landau.py:96`: dirac=True is the facade default but dirac_point assumes a hexagonal zone corner on any lattice

Slice `magnetic`, hole, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**.

get_landau_levels defaults to dirac=True. dirac_point always takes K from the two crystal points (1/3, 1/3, 0) and (1/3, 2/3, 0), whatever the lattice. On a square, rectangular or 3D cell, and on a gapped hexagonal crystal such as h-BN, there is usually no pair within 1e-3 Ry there. The default call then raises 'no degenerate pair at K: not a Dirac cone at the zone corner' after the Wannier run, and the message does not say to pass dirac=False. If an accidental pair within 13.6 meV does exist at that point, the levels window is silently re-centred on it (energy = E_D) instead of on the edge or the Fermi level. Fix: default dirac to False, or to True only for a hexagonal a1, a2 at 60 or 120 degrees, and name dirac=False in the error.

Trigger: calc.get_landau_levels(B=10) with the defaults on any non-graphene crystal, for example h-BN (gap at K) or a tetragonal cell

```
calculator.py:1597 `dirac=True, zeeman=False, ...`; landau.py (magnetic) :94-96 `candidates = [np.array([1 / 3, 1 / 3, 0.0]), np.array([1 / 3, 2 / 3, 0.0])]` / `K = max(candidates, key=...)`; :109-110 `if not pairs: raise ValueError("no degenerate pair at K: not a Dirac cone at the zone corner")`; workflows/landau.py:194-197 `point = dirac_point(...)` / `energy = point["E_D"] if point is not None else float(start)`
```

Skeptic: calculator.py:1597 defaults dirac=True and forwards it (calculator.py:1642-1644), while run_landau_levels itself defaults dirac=False. workflows/landau.py:193-194 then calls dirac_point, which takes K from the fixed crystal points (1/3, 1/3, 0) and (1/3, 2/3, 0) whatever the lattice (magnetic/landau.py:94-95) and raises 'no degenerate pair at K: not a Dirac cone at the zone corner' when no pair is within 1e-3 Ry. So the default call on a gapped hexagonal crystal such as h-BN stops after the Wannier run with a message that does not name dirac=False. The guide documents dirac=True as the default (magnetic-field.md:75) and its Limits note says nothing about crystals without a cone. I lower the severity from crash: it is a ValueError with a physical message, raised before the solves, so nothing wrong is returned in that case. The silent half is real but narrower: when a pair within 1e-3 Ry does exist at that point, energy is set to its mean (workflows/landau.py:195-196) and the window moves away from the edge. On an fcc cell (1/3, 1/3, 0) lies on the Gamma to X line, where a twofold band is forced by symmetry, so silicon would take this path. I did not run it.

Fix: Name dirac=False in the error text, and make the facade default depend on the lattice (hexagonal a1, a2 at 60 or 120 degrees) or set it to False and update the guide.

### `tests/unit/test_magnetic_cross.py:47`: P177 crosscheck: two of the three fast tests read committed .npz files and run no defuqumat code

Slice `tests`, hole, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**, known: new; the docstring of tests/unit/test_magnetic_cross.py says it, PLAN.md P177 counts the file as three fast tests..

`test_route_p_full_mesh_is_q_copies_of_the_representatives` (line 47) and `test_an_open_set_of_representatives_is_refused_by_the_comparison` (line 56) load `plans/orbital-field/route-p-q*.npz` and compare arrays inside those files. They can fail only if the files are recommitted, never from a change under `defuqumat/`, yet they are counted in P177's fast tally ("test_magnetic_cross.py (3)"). ```
tests/unit/test_magnetic_cross.py:47-53: `for path in _route_p_files(): z = np.load(path) ... d = np.max(np.abs(z["sorted_levels"] - _copies(z["eigenvalues"], z["kpoints"], q)))`
```

Skeptic: tests/unit/test_magnetic_cross.py:47-53 loads each `plans/orbital-field/route-p-q*.npz` and compares `z["sorted_levels"]` with copies of `z["eigenvalues"]` from the same file; :56-61 does the same on route-p-q3-full.npz with the wrong members.
