The module docstring (:9, "from the committed files and the Peierls model only") says what they are, and the live form of the theorem is tests/unit/test_magnetic_bloch.py:151, so no check is missing. This is a counting and labelling issue only.

Fix: Say in PLAN.md P177's tally that two of the three tests are checks of the committed level sets, or mark them with a comment as data checks so a reader does not count them as coverage of `defuqumat/magnetic/`.

### `defuqumat/response/sternheimer.py:589`: Metal branch of the projector solves the bands past nbnd_occ with a nonzero right-hand side and an operator the level shift does not make positive definite

Slice `sternheimer`, hole, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**, known: new. PLAN.md:3792-3797 and the SternheimerSolver.__init__ comment (sternheimer.py:375-382) state the opposite ('multiplied by an occupation of zero, which is the same answer at a static shape'). notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1455 checked _smeared_projection term by term and the alpha_pv cut on j, but not the missing cut on i..

In the smearing branch the solver keeps every band (line 389) and `project` multiplies the right-hand side by wg1_i (line 666). It never masks the rows i outside `projector_mask`. The sharp branch does mask them (lines 612-613), and its comment explains that the reason is that `H - eps_n S` is not positive definite for an empty n. QE never solves those rows: `cgsolve_all` and `incdrhoscf` run ibnd only up to nbnd_occ(ikk). The code and PLAN.md say that the bands past the cutoff 'carry an occupation of zero', but they do not. At the cutoff ef + xmax*degauss, wgauss is erfc(3)/2 = 1.1e-5 for Gaussian smearing and about 7e-5 for Fermi-Dirac, so those rows get a right-hand side of order 1e-5 |dV psi|. For such a band i, the operator `H - eps_i S + alpha_pv S P_mask S` is indefinite. psi_i itself is an exact null vector because it lies outside the mask. Computed bands j between the cutoff and eps_i have eigenvalue eps_j - eps_i < 0. Even the lowest band gets cutoff - eps_i < 0, because alpha_pv = cutoff - emin. These rows are then run through a preconditioned CG to an absolute threshold, and the `lax.while_loop` keeps the whole block iterating until every row converges. A row that stagnates on the indefinite operator therefore costs up to MAX_ITERATIONS = 400 steps for the whole k-point. Its dpsi_i also enters drho with weight wk, as a term ph.x does not have, of order wg1_i. In exact arithmetic that term is small, about 1e-5 relative. I have not measured whether the CG stagnates; it needs a measurement.

Trigger: Any smeared metal solved through SternheimerSolver whose nbnd holds a band above ef + xmax*degauss at some k-point. For example the Gamma phonon of al2-metal (make_sternheimer with metals=True, Calculator.get_phonons), the metallic strain response, or elph, at pw.x's default nbnd for a smearing run (1.2 nelec/2 or nelec/2 + 4). The bands just past the cutoff (wg1 from 1e-5 to 1e-8) are the live rows. Bands far above have wg1 that underflows to 0 and exit at iteration 0. The measurement that settles it is the per-row CG count of one metal solve, or the solve's `iterations` with and without `rhs = jnp.where(self.projector_mask[spin][ik][:, None], rhs, 0.0)` applied to the smeared branch's output rows. With the mask, the rows exit at iteration 0 and the solve reproduces QE's nbnd_occ truncation exactly.

```
sternheimer.py:389 `keep = psi.shape[2] if smearing is not None else max(counts)`; :414 `self.projector_mask = self.eigenvalues < smearing.cutoff`; :589-595 `if self.smearing is not None: ... rhs, overlaps = self._smeared_projection(rhs, overlaps, ik, spin); return -(rhs - jnp.einsum("mn,mg->ng", overlaps, s_occupied))` (no row mask); :666 `return occupation[:, None] * rhs, weights * overlaps`; compare the sharp branch :612-613 `keep = self.projector_mask[spin][ik][:, None]; rhs = jnp.where(keep, rhs, 0.0)`, and :564-567 where the level shift's overlaps are masked to `projector_mask`, so a row outside the mask is not lifted.
```

Skeptic: The code path is as the finder says, and a default metal run reaches it. What I could not confirm is the stall, so the severity goes down from perf to minor. The lines that settled it: (1) sternheimer.py:389 keeps every band when smearing is set, and the smeared branch of `project` at :589-595 returns without any row mask, while :666 scales the right-hand side by wg1_i, which past the cutoff is small but not zero. The sharp branch masks the rows at :609-610 and its own comment at :601-604 gives the reason (the operator is not positive definite for an empty n). (2) QE stops at nbnd_occ(ikk) in all three places: orthogonalize.f90:98 (`DO ibnd = 1, nbnd_occ (ikk)`), response_kernels.f90:260-261 (cgsolve_all called with nbnd_occ(ikk)), incdrhoscf.f90:102. (3) The operator is indefinite for a row past the cutoff: _alpha_pv at :1500-1502 is cutoff - emin, and the level shift at :567 is masked to projector_mask, so on a masked band j the eigenvalue is eps_j - emin + cutoff - eps_i, negative for the lowest bands when eps_i > cutoff, it is eps_j - eps_i < 0 for an unmasked j below i, and psi_i itself (and any degenerate partner outside the mask) is a null vector. (4) The repository already treats this as wrong in the sibling class: TwoSphereSolver.project masks the rows before the smeared projection (phononq.py:307-309) and cuts the block at metallic_rows (phononq.py:239-246, comment 'nothing past the largest such count over the grid has a right-hand side'). (5) Reach: get_phonons on a metal at Gamma passes require_a_sternheimer_regime(metals=True) at phonon.py:411 and builds the base SternheimerSolver with smearing_of(...) at phonon.py:507-513, on both the streamed and the whole-k route; strain.py:309 and :341 do the same. al2-metal.in (tests/data/qe, marzari-vanderbilt, degauss=0.05, default nbnd) is such an input; PERFORMANCE.md:2243 gives 8 bands against a per-k nbnd_occ of 1 to 3 on al-metal. What dies or is weaker than claimed: (a) the wrong-answer part is already bounded by a test. test_phonons_at_q.py:231-236 and :283-299 compare the Gamma route (unmasked rows) with the two-sphere route at q=0 (masked rows) on al2-metal and measure 5.7e-7 Ry/bohr^2 on an onsite term of 0.048, and the Gamma route is 3.2e-3 cm^-1 from ph.x against 2.9e-3 for the masked one. So the extra term is at most about 1e-5 relative, and it is not a wrong answer at any tolerance the project claims. (b) The stall to MAX_ITERATIONS is not established and I think it is unlikely to set the count: a row past the cutoff starts with a residual of order wg1 |dV psi| (5e-7 at the cutoff for marzari-vanderbilt, 1.1e-5 for a Gaussian) and has to reach the absolute threshold 1e-11 (:230) or the scheduled one, which is 4 to 6 orders, where an occupied row needs about 11. CG on a Hermitian operator with a few negative eigenvalues can break down (c = d.A.d near 0 at :768-769) but usually converges. No CG count for a metal at Gamma is recorded in PLAN.md or PERFORMANCE.md, and the phonon result does not surface a capped row (phonon.py:835-837 only sums iterations, Phonons.converged is the outer loop's flag), so reading cannot settle it. (c) The trigger list must drop elph: it goes through TwoSphereSolver, which masks the rows, and a metal at q=0 is refused there. (d) 'known' is not fully new: PERFORMANCE.md:2238-2248 records the whole-block cost as backlog. What is new is that its sentence 'The bands past the cutoff carry an occupation of zero, so the answer is exact' is false, and the same sentence at PLAN.md:3792-3795 and sternheimer.py:373-377.

Fix: In the smeared branch of SternheimerSolver.project, zero the rows outside the mask before the projection, as phononq.py:307-309 does: `rhs = jnp.where(self.projector_mask[spin][ik][:, None], rhs, 0.0)`; optionally cut the block at metallic_rows as TwoSphereSolver does, which also takes the nbnd/nbnd_occ cost in PERFORMANCE.md's backlog. Then correct the 'occupation of zero, exact' sentence in sternheimer.py:373-377, PLAN.md:3792-3795 and PERFORMANCE.md:2241-2242. The measurement to take first is the per-row CG count of one al2-metal Gamma solve with and without the mask.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1668`: get_magnetic_bands and get_magnetizability run the SCF before the regime refusal

Slice `docs-holes`, hole, severity `minor (a cost, not a missing refusal)`, status **confirmed**, known: Not checked against the "named gaps" list of PLAN.md P177, which I did not read. Not in OPEN.md or the three audits..

get_landau_levels calls refuse_regime first, at :1627, before any SCF. get_magnetic_bands (:1668) and get_magnetizability (:1694) call self._ground_state() first, and the regime check only fires afterwards, inside run_magnetic_bands (magnetic_bands.py:198) or run_magnetic_scf (fold.py:267). The error the guide promises does come, but only after a full zero-field SCF has been paid for. On a spinor PAW cell this can be a long SCF.

Trigger: A Calculator with nspin=2, a PAW dataset or noncolin, then calc.get_magnetic_bands(flux=(1, 1), nbnd=8) or calc.get_magnetizability(nbnd=52): the SCF converges, then NotImplementedError.

```
defuqumat/calculator.py:1668: `state = None if options.get("free") else self._ground_state("magnetic Bloch bands")`; calculator.py:1694: `self._ground_state("a magnetizability"), kmesh=kmesh,`; against calculator.py:1627: `refuse_regime(self.system, self.pseudos, "Landau levels")`. Guide magnetic-field.md:305-307: "A spinor run ..., `nspin = 2`, ultrasoft and PAW datasets, DFT+U, a spin spiral, a meta-GGA and `K_POINTS gamma` give an error, in all three routes."
```

Skeptic: The order is as claimed. get_magnetic_bands calls self._ground_state at calculator.py:1668 and get_magnetizability at :1694, and _ground_state runs get_scf() when nothing is cached (calculator.py:932-947). The regime check fires only afterwards, at defuqumat/workflows/magnetic_bands.py:198 and defuqumat/magnetic/fold.py:267 (run_magnetizability reaches it through run_magnetic_scf, fold.py:453). A Calculator at nspin = 2, or with a PAW dataset, that calls get_magnetic_bands(flux=(1, 1), nbnd=8) therefore converges the SCF and then gets the NotImplementedError of conventions.py:229. The promised error does come, and nothing wrong is returned. Two things lower it: SCF first and then the workflow's refusal is the general pattern of the facade ("the refusals pass through untouched", .claude/rules/calculator.md), and the converged state stays in the one-slot cache. get_landau_levels checks first for a recorded and different reason: the Peierls workflow sees only the Wannier set (PLAN.md:28700). A grep of P177 from PLAN.md:29456 finds no named gap about this order, and OPEN.md has no entry, so it is not recorded.

Fix: One line in each of the two methods, `refuse_regime(self.system, self.pseudos, ...)` before `_ground_state`, as get_landau_levels does at calculator.py:1627. It costs nothing and the workflow keeps its own check.

### `defuqumat/workflows/qpi.py:212`: Three QPI input checks fire only after the whole-grid band solve

Slice `transport-stm`, hole, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**.

Three checks run only after qpi_bands, which re-solves the bands on the whole grid when grid= is given. These are the exchange refusal at nspin = 1, the sigma > 0 check (in gaussian_transform, inside the per-k loop) and an out-of-range atom index for impurity=. The QPIBands docstring says this solve is nearly all of the cost of run_qpi. None of the three checks needs the states, so a typo costs a full fixed-density solve. This is the 'refuse at the door' rule of OPEN.md Part VI item 1, which _check_bias_axis follows in the transport.

Trigger: get_qpi(potential=0.1, height=0.7, grid=(24, 24, 1), exchange=0.02) on an nspin = 1 surface, or sigma=0, or impurity=99 on a smaller cell: each raises only after the 24x24 band solve.

```
defuqumat/workflows/qpi.py:212
    if nspin == 1 and exchange != 0.0:
        raise ValueError("a magnetic scatterer (exchange) needs an nspin = 2 run")
runs after states = qpi_bands(...) at :167-170. stm/qpi.py gaussian_transform raises on sigma <= 0 only when impurity_overlaps is first called in the per-k loop.
```

Skeptic: The order is as claimed. qpi_bands is called at workflows/qpi.py:167-170 (the fixed-density solve of the whole grid at :383-388). After it come: the impurity index, positions[int(impurity)] (about :184), which raises a bare IndexError for an index past the last atom; the exchange refusal at nspin = 1 (:212-213); and the sigma check, which lives in gaussian_transform (stm/qpi.py:140-142) and is first reached through impurity_overlaps inside the per-k loop (:220-222). None of the three reads the states: nspin is on the system, sigma is a number, and the atom count is on system.structure. The door already checks broadening, readers, axis and exit_height before the solve, so these three are the ones left out. The cost is one wasted whole-grid solve per typo; states= from get_qpi_bands removes it for the later calls but not for the first. Not recorded in OPEN.md.

Fix: Move the three checks above the qpi_bands call, with the atom index tested against system.structure and a named ValueError.

## Confirmed by the skeptic: optimizations (7)

### `defuqumat/kpm/conductivity.py:269`: KPM conductivity keeps one (ndir, ndir, N, N) moment block per k-point of the chunk, on the whole grid, with a chunk sized for the SCF

Slice `opt-memory`, optimization, severity `memory (an out-of-memory stop on a card for a small cell on a dense grid; no wrong number; the measured one-k benchmark cells do not reach it)`, status **confirmed**, known: new. Neither notes/MEMORY-AUDIT.md nor notes/GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md mentions kpm. PLAN.md P169 (line ~28045) records the contrary claim 'the result is ndir^2 N^2 whatever nk and R'. plans/kpm.md:44-45 sizes the DOS recursion as '3 R ndim per k-point in flight' and says nothing of the conductivity's N^2 block per k..

Inside `conductivity_moments`, `one_k` carries `mu` of shape (ndir, ndir, N, N) through a `lax.scan` over the random vectors and adds a fresh `block` of the same shape at every step. `sum_k` with a chunk above 1 goes through `_chunk_sum`, which vmaps `one_k` over the chunk and stacks every output before it sums. So there are at least two (c, ndir, ndir, N, N) complex arrays per chunk of c k-points: the scan carry and the step's block. The module docstring and PLAN P169 say the opposite: the moments are 'ndir^2 N^2 complex numbers however many k-points there are'. Two things make c large. First, `kpm_setup` takes `resolve_k_batch(calculation.k_batch)` (kpm/dos.py:395). On a card in the default memory mode, that value is `choose_k_batch`'s answer from the SCF estimate, which has no N^2 term, and it is None (the whole axis) whenever the SCF fits. Second, `whole_grid_calculation` moves to the whole Monkhorst-Pack grid with `at_kpoints` (driver.py:4190), which `copy.copy`s the calculation and never re-resolves `k_batch`. So a chunk chosen for the wedge is applied to a grid up to 48 times larger. Peak per chunk: c x [(ndir+2) N npol npwx + at least 2 ndir^2 N^2] complex numbers. For a small cell the second term dominates by about 9 N / (5 npol npwx). Fix direction: accumulate the N^2 block with `sum_k(..., batch=1)` (the scan branch keeps one copy), or contract the chunk axis inside the chunk with an einsum so that only one block exists per chunk. Alternatively, size the chunk from the KPM's own per-k bytes, and let `whole_grid_calculation` re-resolve the dial on the whole grid. The docstring and the P169 sentence should then be corrected.

Trigger: `Calculator.get_kpm_conductivity` (`run_kpm_conductivity`) at its defaults (directions x, y, z, so ndir = 3; broadening 0.02 Ry) on a card in memory mode, or in speed mode anywhere (on a CPU memory mode gives k_batch = 1, so `sum_k` takes the scan branch and the trigger does not reach). The case is Si2 at 12 Ry, where PLAN P169 measures N = 636 at 0.02 Ry, on an 8x8x8 grid: the wedge SCF fits whole, so k_batch = None, and the whole grid has 512 points. By arithmetic, not measured: one block is 9 x 636^2 x 16 B = 58 MB, and at least 2 x 58 MB x 512 is about 60 GB, against the 58 MB the docstring states. The measured P169 cases are 2x2x2 grids (8 points, about 0.9 GB), which is why it went unseen. No upstream guard: nothing in defuqumat/kpm/ estimates or refuses by memory (grep of MemoryError, refuse and estimate finds none).

```
defuqumat/kpm/conductivity.py:253-261: `block = jnp.stack(rows, axis=1)  # (a, b, n, m)` ... `return (mu + block, norms, dos), None` ... `(mu, norms, dos), _ = lax.scan(one_vector, zero, starts)`; conductivity.py:269: `mu, norms, dos, norm0 = sum_k(one_k, (indices, weights), batch=k_batch)`; defuqumat/batching.py:772-776: `_chunk_sum` = `jnp.sum(a, axis=0)` over `jax.vmap(fn)(chunk)`; batching.py:746-747: `if batch is None or batch >= nk: return _chunk_sum(fn, xs)`; defuqumat/kpm/traces.py:138: `return calculation.at_kpoints(for_spin(whole, calculation.system.nspin))`; driver.py at_kpoints body: `moved = copy.copy(self)` with no k_batch line before `return moved`; docstring conductivity.py:48-53: 'The moments are summed over k inside the walk, so the result is ndir^2 N^2 complex numbers however many k-points there are.'
```

Skeptic: The path is as the finder says, with four corrections. (1) Path: conductivity.py:253-256 builds `block` (ndir, ndir, N, N) and returns `mu + block` as the scan carry, :261 scans the random vectors, :269 calls `sum_k(one_k, (indices, weights), batch=k_batch)`. batching.py:747 sends `batch is None or batch >= nk` to `_chunk_sum`, and :772-776 is `jnp.sum(a, axis=0)` over `jax.vmap(fn)(chunk)`, so the vmap output (c, ndir, ndir, N, N) is stacked before it is summed. One stacked copy exists by construction. The scan carry under vmap is a second one of the same shape and `block` a third, but how many XLA keeps alive at once cannot be settled by reading, so I count one as certain and the factor 2 as likely. (2) How c becomes the whole grid: run_kpm_conductivity :721 calls `whole_grid_calculation`, traces.py:138 is `calculation.at_kpoints(...)`, and at_kpoints (driver.py:4190 to about 4305, `moved = copy.copy(self)` at :4233) has no k_batch line, so the dial is copied. kpm_setup dos.py:395 is `resolve_k_batch(calculation.k_batch)`, and batching.py:586-605 passes None through. On a card in default memory mode `resolve_k_batch_for` (driver.py:2399-2412) calls `choose_k_batch`, and sizing.py:1322-1324 returns `k_batch=None` whenever the wedge's SCF estimate fits. So the whole grid goes into one vmap. An integer chunk is not multiplied by the move, it only stays c. (3) The finder's trigger is too wide: `memory_preset` returns `k_batch: 1` on a CPU in both modes (batching.py:511-514), so 'speed mode anywhere' is wrong. The trigger is a card (memory mode when the wedge fits, or speed mode), or an explicit `k_batch=None` / `DEFUQUMAT_K_BATCH=all` on a CPU. (4) The docstring sentence 'the result is ndir^2 N^2 complex numbers however many k-points there are' (conductivity.py:48-53, PLAN.md:28045) is true of the result. The drift is that 'Per k-point in flight, (ndir + 2) N ndim' leaves out the N^2 block, and PERFORMANCE.md:11610-11612 repeats the same formula. No guard: nothing under defuqumat/kpm/ reads sizing, estimate_size or memory_stats (grep empty), and the guide's Limits note (docs/guide/optics.md:572-580) says only that the wedge is replaced by its whole grid. Not recorded: no kpm line in OPEN.md, notes/MEMORY-AUDIT.md or notes/GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md. Why it was not seen: PERFORMANCE.md:11574-11612 is one k-point on the card, where `sum_k` returns `fn` directly (batching.py:736), and tests/unit/test_kpm_conductivity.py runs 2x2x2 and 3x3x3 grids on a CPU, where the scan branch (:738) is taken. Size, by arithmetic and not measured: one block at ndir = 3 and N = 636 (PLAN.md:28090, Si2 at 0.02 Ry) is 9 x 636^2 x 16 B = 58 MB, so the one certain stacked copy is 3.7 GB on a 4x4x4 grid and 30 GB on 8x8x8, against the 58 MB the record implies. The N^2 block outweighs the families, 5 N ndim, when about 1.8 N > ndim per copy, so on small cells, which are the ones that take dense grids. Neither QE nor Elk has a Chebyshev conductivity, so there is no reference behaviour to copy.

Fix: Keep one block per chunk: vmap only the families over the chunk and contract the chunk axis inside the Gram product with the weights, or accumulate `mu` with `sum_k(..., batch=1)` while the families keep the chunk. Size the chunk from the KPM's own bytes per k-point, (ndir + 2) N ndim plus the ndir^2 N^2 blocks, and let `whole_grid_calculation` re-resolve the dial on the whole grid. Add the N^2 term to the 'per k-point in flight' sentence in the module docstring, PLAN.md P169 and PERFORMANCE.md.

### `defuqumat/wannier/kmesh.py:94`: kmesh_get recomputes a (1331, nk) distance table for every k-point and shell, for offsets that do not depend on k

Slice `wannier`, optimization, severity `perf (small at the meshes in use; grows as nk^2 and bites from about 8x8x8)`, status **confirmed**.

_shell_bvectors is called once per (k-point, chosen shell) in the assembly loop (line 262). Each call builds vkpp_all - kpt_cart[nkp], a (1331, nk, 3) array, and its norm, over every supercell translation and every mesh point. On a regular mesh the b-vectors of a shell are the same at every k (only nnlist and nncell change), so the table is rebuilt for no new information. For a 12x12x12 mesh (nk = 1728) one call is 1331 x 1728 x 3 doubles, about 55 MB of temporaries, and there are 1728 calls per shell. That is roughly 1728 x 6.9M distance evaluations per shell, before any state is solved. The cost grows as nk^2, and the mesh sizes Wannier interpolation wants are where it bites. A fix is to take the shell's b-vectors once from k = 0 (as _choose_shells already does) and find each k + b on the mesh by rounding k_frac + b_frac to the grid, which is O(nk nntot). This is array-size arithmetic from the code, not a timing.

Trigger: Any run_wannier on a dense mesh, e.g. divisions=(12, 12, 12): nk^2 x 1331 work in kmesh_get on every call, paid before the fixed-density solve.

```
defuqumat/wannier/kmesh.py:94 `dist = np.linalg.norm(vkpp_all - kpt_cart[nkp], axis=-1)  # (Ntrans, num_kpts)` ; :260-262 `for nkp in range(num_kpts): ... matches = _shell_bvectors(vkpp_all, kpt_cart, nkp, dnn[shell], multi[shell], kmesh_tol)` ; :210 `vkpp_all = trans_cart[:, None, :] + kpt_cart[None, :, :]  # (Ntrans, num_kpts, 3)` with NSUPCELL = 5 (11^3 = 1331 translations)
```

Skeptic: The code is as quoted: kmesh.py:262 calls _shell_bvectors once per k-point and chosen shell, and each call builds the full (1331, nk) table at kmesh.py:94 from vkpp_all (kmesh.py:210, NSUPCELL = 5 at :18). kmesh_get is called once per run_wannier (workflows/wannier.py:451), before the fixed-density solve. The reference does the same double loop but stops early: wannier90-3.1.0/src/kmesh.F90:288-310 scans the translations sorted by distance and leaves with `cycle ok` when the shell has its multi(ndnn) neighbours, so it touches a few tens of translations per k, not 1331. So the port does roughly 50 times the work of the Fortran, with the same nk^2 law. At the shapes that are used here it is not a cost: every test, notebook and guide snippet runs 4x4x4 (64 calls of an 85k-point table per shell) or a 2D graphene mesh, and on si8 or si16 a Wannier mesh is 2x2x2 to 4x4x4. From array sizes only, not timed: 8x8x8 is 512 calls of a 0.7M-point table (seconds), 12x12x12 is 1728 calls of a 2.3M-point table with about 55 MB of temporaries each (of the order of a minute or more per shell), which is then comparable to the solve of a two-atom cell on that mesh. PERFORMANCE.md has no figure for kmesh_get.

Fix: Take the shell b-vectors once at k = 0 and find k + b on the mesh by integer arithmetic on the grid, then sort each k's matches by (translation rank, k index) so that the order of nnlist stays Wannier90's, which the tests compare. A smaller change with the same order: keep the scan but cut the translation list to those within the shell radius plus the mesh extent.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1305`: get_born_charges re-runs the whole electric-field Sternheimer solve after get_dielectric_tensor, while PLAN.md says it hits a cache

Slice `opt-repeat`, optimization, severity `perf, with doc-drift in PLAN.md and notebook 19`, status **confirmed**, known: new. PLAN.md:8517 claims the cache exists, so the record is wrong too (doc-drift).

get_dielectric_tensor has no cache. It calls response.efield.dielectric_tensor straight away. That function defaults to born_charges=True (efield.py:297), so the first call already computes Z*. get_born_charges then calls get_dielectric_tensor again with the same options and solves the three field perturbations a second time, at the same tr2 (default_tr2 takes born_charges, and it is True both times). The second call repeats 100 per cent of the first. PLAN.md:8517 records the opposite ("it costs nothing, because get_born_charges delegates to get_dielectric_tensor and hits the same cache"), and notebook 19 has a comment saying it "reuses the solve above rather than running a second one". get_strain_response already has the one-slot, options-keyed cache that would fix this (calculator.py:1391 to 1438). The field response is a few nocc-sized blocks per direction, so it fits the same memory rule.

Trigger: notebooks/19_linear_response.md as committed: Calculator.from_file("tests/data/qe/si-epsilon.in").get_dielectric_tensor() and then .get_born_charges(). Same for the ultrasoft cell at :215 and :219. That is two identical field solves per cell, and the second is the whole cost of get_born_charges.

```
calculator.py:1286-1295 `def get_dielectric_tensor(self, **options): ... return dielectric_tensor(self.calculation, result.wavefunctions, ...)` (no slot); calculator.py:1304-1305 `options.setdefault("born_charges", True)` / `tensor = self.get_dielectric_tensor(**options)`; efield.py:297 `born_charges: bool = True,`; PLAN.md:8517 `delegates to get_dielectric_tensor and hits the same cache`; notebooks/19_linear_response.md:48 `field = silicon.get_dielectric_tensor()` and :137 `born = silicon.get_born_charges()`, also :215/:219 for the ultrasoft cell
```

Skeptic: The path is as claimed. calculator.py:1286-1295 calls dielectric_tensor directly and keeps nothing; the only slots in the file are _scf, _ultracell and _strain_response (grep at :533-534, :750, :842, :1428-1438). calculator.py:1304-1305 sets born_charges=True and calls get_dielectric_tensor again. efield.py has no cache at all (grep for cache, memo, weakref is empty). efield.py:297 has born_charges=True by default and efield.py:390 takes tr2 = default_tr2(calculation, density, born_charges), so both calls run the same solve at the same tr2. git log -S"self._dielectric" on calculator.py is empty, so no such slot ever existed and PLAN.md:8517-8518 ("hits the same cache", written at 23e6ec3a) was never true. notebooks/19_linear_response.md:135-137 carries the same false comment, and the notebook solves the silicon field three times (:48, :137, :289) and the ultrasoft one twice (:215, :219). No guard is in the way: any insulator the dielectric route accepts reaches it. The cost is real: PERFORMANCE.md:2880-2881 gives 13.3 s and 16.3 s warm on si-epsilon and :3238 gives 245 s on si10-epsilon, and eager.py:10-13 records that a second get_dielectric_tensor() runs the whole loop again. ph.x gets epsilon and Z* from one field solve in one run (I did not open the Fortran for this). The guide is not affected: docs/guide/phonons.md:50-53 reads eps.born_charges from the one call. The default result has no internals, so a kept DielectricTensor is a (3,3) and a (nat,3,3) array, and the memory rule is not at stake.

Fix: Give get_dielectric_tensor a slot keyed by the merged options, as get_strain_response does at calculator.py:1428-1438, cleared where _strain_response is cleared (:750, :842) and never filled when keep_internals=True. Then correct PLAN.md:8517 and the comment at notebooks/19_linear_response.md:135.

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1823`: get_stm, get_sts, get_vertical_transport and get_momentum_transport re-solve the whole grid on every call; only QPI has a states= reuse

Slice `opt-repeat`, optimization, severity `perf, narrower than claimed`, status **confirmed**, known: new. The QPI half was done at 8b4e5481 (P157). OPEN.md:4303 only notes that the bias check now comes before the re-solve..

With grid= (the documented route on a symmetry-reduced slab SCF), each of the four entry points runs its own fixed_density_states on the whole grid at the same density, nbnd and conv_thr. None of them takes the states back. P157 added qpi_bands / get_qpi_bands / states= for this exact reason: the commit message of 8b4e5481 says "the band solve is nearly all of get_qpi, and it does not depend on the impurity, the energies or the broadening". The tunnelling routes did not get the same option. In a tip-height scan, an exit-plane scan, a new energy window, or the image + spectrum + transmission triad on one slab, the band solve is repeated N times where one would do. Measured shares: the transport assembly after the solve is 0.85 to 2.24 s on a 100x100 map (PERFORMANCE.md:10820-10821). The QPI whole call is 15.7 s against 51 ms of assembly per energy (PERFORMANCE.md:11267-11268). run_momentum_transport's own docstring says "A whole grid of a real slab is an hour, most of it in a band solve". So every call after the first is about 95 to 99 per cent repeated work.

Trigger: A slab SCF with symmetry, then calc.get_vertical_transport(exit_height=0.38, height=h, grid=(12, 12, 1)) for h in (0.60, 0.62, 0.64). That is three whole-grid fixed-density solves of identical states. Or calc.get_stm(height=0.62, grid=g) then calc.get_sts(energies, tip=..., grid=g) then calc.get_momentum_transport(0.38, 0.62, grid=g): three solves of one k-set.

```
workflows/transport.py:240 `calculation, system, eigenvalues, wavefunctions = fixed_density_states(system, pseudos, result.density, kpoints, nbnd, conv_thr, k_batch, ...)` and :481 the same call in run_momentum_transport; workflows/stm.py:187 and :367 the same in run_stm / run_sts; none of run_stm (stm.py:70), run_sts (:273), run_vertical_transport (transport.py:89-121) or run_momentum_transport (:336) has a states= parameter; calculator.py:1807-1821 get_qpi_bands is the existing pattern
```

Skeptic: The path is real for three of the four entry points. With grid= each call runs its own fixed_density_states with the same arguments: transport.py:240 (run_vertical_transport), transport.py:481 (run_momentum_transport), stm.py:367 (run_sts), all on whole_grid (transport.py:220, :450, stm.py:350) at conv_thr=1e-6 (transport.py:117, :356, stm.py:295). None of the signatures has a states= parameter, and the calculator methods at :1749-1779 and :1823-1870 forward only options. QPI has it (qpi.py:85, :167-172, calculator.py:1807-1821, docs/guide/stm.md:524-530). No guard is in the way for a slab SCF with symmetry and grid=(n, n, 1). Three parts of the claim do not hold. First, get_stm is not in the same set: stm.py:170 builds denser_grid, the wedge of the run's own symmetry (nscf.py:655-683), not the whole grid, so its states are another k-set and the triad of the trigger is two solves of one k-set plus one of another. Second, the height scan is already one solve for momentum transport: height takes a sequence (transport.py:376, :384-389, :508). The scan repeats the solve only in get_vertical_transport (height: float, transport.py:96) and for a change of exit_height. Third, the 95 to 99 per cent share is measured for QPI only (PERFORMANCE.md:11267-11268); the transport table says the band solve is outside its numbers (PERFORMANCE.md:10817-10818), so for these routes there is only the docstring sentence. Energies are an axis in every route, so a new window repeats the solve only across calls. Without grid= there is no re-solve at all (transport.py:231-236, :463-470), so an SCF run on the whole grid without symmetry is a workaround, at the price of a more expensive SCF. pp.x reads one saved nscf run for every image, so QE does not repeat this. Nothing in OPEN.md or PLAN.md records it; OPEN.md:4300-4305 is only about the order of the bias check.

Fix: Add a caller-held value like QPIBands (qpi_bands and states=) to run_sts, run_vertical_transport and run_momentum_transport, with a get_*_bands on the calculator; not a facade slot, since the states are (nk, nbnd, npwx) on the whole grid. Decide the one conv_thr first: it is 1e-6 here and 1e-8 for QPI (qpi.py:82, :360).

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:9701`: The meta-GGA tau residual and the Hubbard ns residual are read with their own float() after the batched device_get

Slice `opt-jit-host`, optimization, severity `minor`, status **confirmed**, known: OPEN.md Part 'The host fetches of an SCF iteration' item 21 records the counts (27 -> 16 with U, 21 -> 12 meta-GGA) but does not name these two sites or say their inputs exist before the batched read. New: the two sites and the fact that they can be folded in..

The attempt's scalars are read in one jax.device_get at driver.py:9599 (OPEN item 21's fix). Two residual terms are still read afterwards, one blocking round trip each: float(_tau_accuracy(...)) at :9701 and float(calculation.ns_accuracy(...)) at :9690. tau_out already exists before :9599 (it is set at :9506 or from the streamed tuple), so _tau_accuracy can be dispatched before the batched read and read inside it. For the Hubbard term, occupation_matrix(wavefunctions, wg) needs only wg, so it can also be dispatched before :9599. The iteration-1 adjust_ns branch and the mixing_fixed_ns override are host decisions on the iteration number, not on device values, so they do not block this. Folding both in removes one host block per attempt on a meta-GGA run and one on a DFT+U run, out of the 12 and 16 that OPEN item 21 counts. What this is worth in time is not measured, and item 21 itself says the per-read cost is mostly waiting for compute that has to finish anyway.

Trigger: Every SCF iteration (every attempt) of a potential-only meta-GGA run (tests' si2-tb09.in, tb09/bj06) or of a DFT+U run (ni-kind1-force). Neither shows on benchmarks/si8-1k*.in or si16-1k*.in, which are LDA without U and already at 5 reads.

```
driver.py:9690  accuracy += float(calculation.ns_accuracy(ns_out - ns_state))
driver.py:9701  tau_accuracy_term = float(_tau_accuracy(
                    tau_out - tau_state, calculation.basis.dense,
                    calculation.system.cell))
while driver.py:9599 reads steps, unsettled, accuracy_terms, iteration_terms, ehart, etxc, epaw, paw_deband in one jax.device_get((...)).
```

Skeptic: The path is as quoted. /u/40/ladovj1/data/Documents/programs/claude/defuqumat/defuqumat/scf/driver.py:9598-9603 reads steps, unsettled, accuracy_terms, iteration_terms, ehart, etxc and PAW's two in one jax.device_get, and after it :9690 does `accuracy += float(calculation.ns_accuracy(ns_out - ns_state))` and :9701 does `tau_accuracy_term = float(_tau_accuracy(tau_out - tau_state, ...))`. Both producers return device scalars: ns_accuracy is ns_ddot (:3553-3555) and _tau_accuracy is jax.jit(tau_accuracy) (:299). The inputs exist before the batched read: tau_out is set at :9499 (streamed) or :9508 (the finder wrote :9506), and the Hubbard branches at :9670 and :9683 test only the iteration number, starting_density, starting_ns and mixing_fixed_ns, which are host values. The additions are host floats in the same order, so folding the two reads in moves no number. Reach: any potential-only meta-GGA SCF (tau_state is not None) and any DFT+U SCF (calculation.is_hubbard), with no refusal upstream. Record: OPEN.md item 21 (line 8588) is marked done and gives 27 -> 16 with U and 21 -> 12 meta-GGA, but its site list does not name these two reads; tests/unit/test_scf_host_reads.py pins only the 5 of silicon (line 105), so nothing pins the U or meta-GGA count. On the cost: it is one blocking read per attempt out of 12 or 16, each waiting for work that has to finish anyway (item 21's own bound, "probably small"). On a CPU the read of a finished scalar is free; on a card it is one scalar round trip against an iteration of at least tens of ms. It does not appear on si8-1k or si16-1k (LDA, no U). QE has no host and device split, so there is no reference behaviour. So the fact stands, the severity is minor and not perf. One addition: in the non-streaming case the Hubbard occupation_matrix at :9669 is dispatched after the park at :9523, so the move has to go above the park, not only above :9599 (see N1).

Fix: Dispatch _tau_accuracy(tau_out - tau_state, ...) beside _accuracy_split, and the Hubbard occupation_matrix and ns_accuracy before the park at :9523 (skip the dispatch where iteration 1 or mixing_fixed_ns makes the residual zero), put both scalars in the device_get tuple at :9598, and add a U and a meta-GGA count to tests/unit/test_scf_host_reads.py.

### `defuqumat/kpm/conductivity.py:159`: velocity_tangents stores ndir+1 whole-grid projector tables even on a lazy-projector calculation, and it is not selectable

Slice `opt-memory`, optimization, severity `minor (a stated trade with no measured number and no selector; it dominates only when nk_whole x nkb exceeds N, and one sub-claim is false)`, status **confirmed**, known: Partly known. notes/GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md item 7 (line ~758) records `at_kcart` under the velocity operator's jvp as 'the one traced mover still taken whole-k', for the response stack (item 2). PLAN.md P169 and the module docstring state the stored table. New: the KPM site, on the whole grid instead of the wedge, with the whole-grid atomic orbitals of the variate beside it, and no number in PERFORMANCE.md or either memory note..

`velocity_tangents` runs `calculation.at_kcart(kc)` on the whole k axis at once. It keeps the primal `moving` (a stored projector table even when the calculation rebuilds its projectors, as the docstring says) and appends one jvp tangent per direction. During each `jax.jvp` the primal of `inexact_parts` is rebuilt as well. The peak is therefore (ndir + 2) nk_whole npwx nkb complex numbers, plus the stored Hubbard orbitals when ns is given. In memory mode the SCF itself holds none of these tables, because it rebuilds per k. Here they sit on the whole grid that `whole_grid_calculation` built, so nk_whole is up to 48 times the wedge. When the control variate is on, `variate_corrections` also forms `atomic_orbitals(calculation)` whole-grid, (nk_whole, natw, npol npwx), next to them. The trade is stated in the module docstring, but it is not measured, not in PERFORMANCE.md and not selectable. CLAUDE.md asks for all three when the trade is large. Fix direction: build the moving parts and their tangents per k-chunk inside the `sum_k` body of `conductivity_moments`, with `at_rows` or the chunk's `at_kcart`, as the chunked force and stress already do. Alternatively, apply the velocity as a jvp of `at_kcart` restricted to the chunk's rows. Also pass the orbitals per k-point to `ritz_pairs` instead of materialising them all at once.

Trigger: `get_kpm_conductivity` on any platform and in any memory mode (velocity_tangents has no chunked branch), on a symmetric crystal whose wedge is expanded to the whole grid. The cost is largest where KPM is meant to pay, on a large cell where nkb is comparable to or larger than nbnd. With three directions, five whole-grid (nk, npwx, nkb) tables are in flight at the third jvp, where the default CPU memory-mode SCF keeps a one-k-point core.

```
defuqumat/kpm/conductivity.py:147-163: `def parts(kc): moved = calculation.at_kcart(kc) ...` / `moving = parts(kcart)` / `for direction in directions: ... _, tangent = jax.jvp(inexact_parts, (kcart,), (step,)); tangents.append(tangent)`; docstring :44-45 'One direction's tangent is one more projector table, nk npwx nkb complex numbers, and the three are held at once' and :70-73 'the primal of the moving parts is taken from at_kcart, which stores them, so the velocity holds one nk npwx nkb table besides its tangents'; conductivity.py:355: `orbitals = jnp.swapaxes(atomic_orbitals(calculation), 1, 2)  # (nk, natw, ndim)`
```

Skeptic: The path is real. conductivity.py:147-148 calls `calculation.at_kcart(kc)` on the whole k axis, :154 keeps the primal `moving`, and :160-163 takes one `jax.jvp(inexact_parts, (kcart,), (step,))` per direction and keeps each tangent. `at_kcart` builds a stored table: driver.py:4680-4683 calls `projector_core.at_positions(positions, qq=...)` with no `lazy=`, where at_kpoints passes `lazy=self.projector_storage == "rebuild"` (:4294-4297). The calculation is the whole grid from :721, and :775 calls `velocity_tangents` once, outside any chunk. `velocity_tangents` is not jitted, so each jvp builds the primal again and drops it. So ndir + 1 tables are resident and one more is transient, each nk_whole x npwx x nkb complex numbers. conductivity.py:355 also takes `atomic_orbitals(calculation)` on the whole grid, as dos.py:455 does and as kpm/control.py:33 states. No valid input is refused earlier, since the default call of `get_kpm_conductivity` on a norm-conserving crystal reaches it on any platform. Three corrections to the finder. (1) 'Not in PERFORMANCE.md' is false: PERFORMANCE.md:11610-11612 states 'the velocity's tangents, ndir projector tables (one more on a lazy set, whose primal is stored for the derivative)'. The module docstring (:44-45, :70-73) and PLAN.md:28049-28051 state it too. What is missing is a measured number and a way to select it, which .claude/rules/memory-design.md asks for when a trade is large. (2) Size: against the per-k recursion, (ndir + 2) N ndim, the tables are larger only when nk_whole x nkb > N, with N about 650 at 0.02 Ry. At the measured one-k cells they are a few per cent (by arithmetic, with npwx estimated from the cell volume: si16 at 12 Ry, nkb = 64, about 1.5 MB a table against about 80 MB of families). They become the peak for a larger cell on a k-grid, for instance si64 on 2x2x2, about 0.2 GB a table and 1 GB in all against 0.3 GB. Ultrasoft and PAW are refused (docs/guide/optics.md:575), so nkb is the norm-conserving count. (3) It is partly known: notes/GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md:759-760 and :626-631 record `at_kcart` under the velocity `jvp` as the one traced mover still whole-k, for the response stack, and say what it needs first (`Calculation.at_rows`, landed 2026-09-29). The KPM site on the whole grid is not in that note. QE builds `vkb` one k-point at a time, so the whole-k table is this code's deviation.

Fix: Build the moving parts and their tangents per k-chunk inside the walk of `conductivity_moments`, with `at_rows` and the chunk's `at_kcart`, as GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md already plans for the response stack, and hand `ritz_pairs` the orbitals of one k-point at a time. Until then, put a measured peak for a multi-k cell in PERFORMANCE.md and add the KPM site to GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md item 7.

### `defuqumat/magnetic/fold.py:454`: run_magnetizability always runs the -1 flux SCF, whose energy time reversal fixes equal to the +1 one in every regime the route admits

Slice `opt-repeat`, optimization, severity `minor (a control by design, not an oversight)`, status **confirmed**, known: new. The asymmetry is recorded at PLAN.md:30388 as a measured control, not as optional work..

chi = -(E+ - 2 E0 + E-)/dB^2 is evaluated from three SCFs on the magnetic box. refuse_regime (fold.py run_magnetic_scf, line 32 of its body) admits only spinless nspin = 1 norm-conserving runs with no DFT+U, spiral or meta-GGA. There H(-A) = H(A)^* with a real potential, so E(-1) = E(+1) exactly on a mesh that maps onto itself under k to -k, and the unshifted (2, 6, 6) default does. The docstring itself says asymmetry is "zero by time reversal". The one measured run (PLAN.md:30388) took 4, 7 and 7 SCF iterations, so the -1 SCF is about 7 of 18 iterations, roughly 39 per cent of the 5711 s call. E(+) - E(-) was 1.631e-7 Ry, against E+ + E- - 2E0 = 0.1233 Ry. Using 2 E+ in place of E+ + E- changes chi by 1.3e-6 relative, below the printed -11.401. The -1 run is a control (a gauge or sign defect would show in asymmetry). So the saving is to make it opt-in, for example asymmetry_check=True, and not the default.

Trigger: calc.get_magnetizability(kmesh=(2, 6, 6), nbnd=...) on the 24-atom diamond cell of the P177 record, which is the guide's diamond block

```
fold.py:454-455 `results = {p: run_magnetic_scf(system, pseudos, scf0, flux=(p, 1), kmesh=kmesh, nbnd=nbnd, conv_thr=conv_thr, **options) for p in (0, 1, -1)}`; docstring fold.py:451 `asymmetry is E(+) - E(-), zero by time reversal`; PLAN.md:30388 `the three SCFs in one call, 4, 7 and 7 iterations ... E(+) - E(-) = 1.631e-07 Ry ... 5711 s in all`
```

Skeptic: The path is real: fold.py:453-454 runs run_magnetic_scf for p in (0, 1, -1) in every call, and calculator.py:1676-1696 has no switch. The input is the guide's diamond block (docs/guide/magnetic-field.md:271). The time reversal argument holds for what the route admits: refuse_regime (magnetic/conventions.py:206-229) refuses spinors, nspin = 2, spirals, DFT+U, gamma-only storage, PAW, ultrasoft and meta-GGA, and representatives (fold.py:75-84) builds the unshifted mesh kappa = (i/N1, j/N2, l/N3), which maps onto itself under k to -k. The measured E(+) - E(-) = 1.631e-7 Ry against E(+) - E(0) = 6.2e-2 Ry (PLAN.md:30388) agrees with it. So the -1 SCF carries no information about chi, and it is 7 of the 18 SCF iterations of the one measured run. What lowers the severity is that the -1 run is deliberate and documented. The result type was chosen to carry the asymmetry (PLAN.md:30386-30388, "a float cannot carry ... the asymmetry beside it"), both docstrings promise it (fold.py:451, calculator.py:1685), and the guide prints it and explains it as the check of the route (magnetic-field.md:272, :277, :282-284). It is also the only control of the gauge and the sign that this route has, since no test runs the three SCFs: the only test hit is tests/unit/test_magnetic_facade.py:118, the refusal without nbnd. The 39 per cent is a share of iterations, not a timing: the 5711 s run was taken while the load rose from 3.6 to 12 or 13, and the zero-flux iterations need not cost the same as the others.

Fix: Add a keyword to run_magnetizability and get_magnetizability (for example check_time_reversal=True) that skips the -1 SCF when False, uses 2 E(+) in the second difference and returns asymmetry=None. Keep the control on by default; whether the default changes is the user's decision, and the guide block at magnetic-field.md:271-284 moves with it.

## Plausible: the path is real, the input that reaches it is not settled (3)

### `defuqumat/response/magnetoelectric.py:222`: The branch guard fires on every delta when symmetry pins the electronic phase at the fold, because the field step is not reduced modulo the quantum

Slice `topology`, bug, severity `spurious refusal with a misleading message (below crash; no wrong number)`, status **plausible**, known: partly known: notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1494 dropped 'magnetoelectric's branch guard raising on a base state that sits near the branch cut' as documented behaviour. New here: symmetry can pin the phase exactly at the fold, so reducing delta never helps and the message is misleading, and folding the step is a one-line cure..

step = phase(+B) - phase(-B) is taken on the raw total phases. Each channel's electronic phase is folded onto [-1/2, 1/2) by _fold (rint), and run_polarization does not fold again. If the electronic phase sits at exactly 1/2, the +B end becomes -1/2 + eps and the -B end stays 1/2 - eps. Then |step| = 1 - 2 eps quantum, so the guard raises 'Reduce delta' for every delta. Any operation that maps P to -P pins the phase at 0 or 1/2 of a quantum, for example I*T in a Cr2O3-type magnetoelectric or a mirror normal to gdir. The ionic phase is then pinned too, so the electronic phase can sit exactly at 1/2. The physical step is small, and folding it as step - quantum*rint(step/quantum) before the guard would give the right derivative. The guard would still catch a real large move.

Trigger: A noncollinear SOC magnet whose magnetic group holds I*T (centrosymmetric lattice, so the ionic phase is 0 or 1/2) and whose electronic phase along some gdir is 1/2. Calculator.get_magnetoelectric_tensor raises a RuntimeError for any delta, where the tensor component is allowed and finite.

```
magnetoelectric.py:222-224: `step = np.asarray(phases[+1.0][0]) - np.asarray(phases[-1.0][0])` / `largest_step = max(largest_step, float(np.max(np.abs(step)) / quantum))` / `phase_derivative[:, axis] = step / delta`; :226 `if largest_step > BRANCH_FRACTION: raise RuntimeError(... 'Reduce delta')`; topology/polarization.py:_fold `value - quantum * np.rint(value / quantum)`; workflows/polarization.py:210-215 'Not reduced again' `total = float(strings.total + ionic)`
```

Skeptic: The path is real. magnetoelectric.py:222-224 takes step = phases[+1][0] - phases[-1][0] on raw totals and :226-232 raises when |step|/quantum > BRANCH_FRACTION = 0.25 (:73). The totals come from run_polarization, where the spinor channel is folded once by _fold on 1.0 (topology/polarization.py:176 and :187-189, rint) and the total is not folded again (workflows/polarization.py:210-215). So two ends at 1/2 + eps and 1/2 - eps become -1/2 + eps and 1/2 - eps, the step is 1 - 2 eps for any delta, and 'Reduce delta' cannot help. The regime is always a spinor (nspin_mag = 4 is required at :179), so quantum = 1 equals the fold period and folding the step is exact. What I could not settle is a named cell. The finder's own example fails as stated: with I*T at the origin every band is a Kramers pair whose two phases are phi and -phi, so the electronic phase is 0 mod 1, not 1/2. The fold is reached when the pinning symmetry sits off the origin (nocc times its crystal coordinate equal to 1/2 mod 1), or for a slab centred at z = 1/2 with an odd nocc: the phase along gdir = 3 is nocc/2 mod 1 = 1/2, _phases walks all three gdir (:262-270) and largest_step is the max over them. With a mirror normal to z and B in the plane the two ends are 1/2 +- eps; with B along z both ends are exactly 1/2 and round-off decides the fold. A ferromagnetic 1H monolayer with odd valence (VSe2 with the committed datasets would be the candidate) is such a cell, but I could not check by reading that it is gapped under fixed occupations and converges at both fields, so I leave it as plausible. Nothing records this: notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1494 dropped only the 'near the cut' case as documented, the guide has no branch note for this quantity, and tests/regression/test_magnetoelectric.py:95 only asserts largest_step < 0.25 on AlAs.

Fix: Reduce the step before the guard: step = step - quantum * np.rint(step / quantum), then keep the BRANCH_FRACTION test on the reduced step. A unit test with two hand-made phase triples at +-(1/2 - eps) checks it without an SCF.

### `defuqumat/magnetic/fold.py:310`: Route P SCF solves one representative per Python call, with three blocking reads and one host upload per k per iteration

Slice `opt-jit-host`, optimization, severity `minor`, status **plausible**.

run_magnetic_scf loops over the representatives in Python and calls _solve once per k. Each call does three things: it uploads a freshly built residual_threshold with jnp.asarray(np.concatenate(...)) (fold.py:223), it runs davidson_eigensolver_all over a single index, which reads the per-k finiteness flag on the host (davidson.py:1425), and it reads int(left[0]), np.asarray(values[0]) and int(step[0]) (fold.py:229-233). The eigenvalues then go into a numpy array (:314). So each k-point's solve blocks the host before the next one is dispatched, and the k axis never goes through map_k, against the batching rule. Only one program is compiled (indices is an array argument), so this is dispatch and sync overhead, not recompilation. The residual_threshold array is the same at every k and every iteration and could be built once before the loop. The fold_check at :391 does the same thing over the whole closed mesh.

Trigger: run_magnetic_scf(flux=(p, q), kmesh=(2, 2), ...) with the default residual_threshold, at every SCF iteration, with nrep blocking calls. The cost is not visible on si8-1k or si16-1k (route P is the orbital-field workflow), and next to a Davidson solve on a magnetic box it is probably small. Not measured.

```
fold.py:310  for ik in range(nrep):
    start = start_block(...) if states[ik] is None else states[ik]
    values, vectors, step = _solve(hamiltonian, ik, nbnd, nsolve, start, thresholds_of(ethr), ...)
    eigenvalues[ik], states[ik] = values, vectors
fold.py:223  residual_threshold = jnp.asarray(np.concatenate([np.full(nbnd, float(residual_threshold)), np.full(nsolve - nbnd, 1e30)]))
fold.py:229  if int(left[0]): ...
fold.py:233  return np.asarray(values[0]), vectors[0], int(step[0])
```

Skeptic: The loop is real: /u/40/ladovj1/data/Documents/programs/claude/defuqumat/defuqumat/magnetic/fold.py:310-316 calls _solve once per representative, and _solve (:216-233) calls davidson_eigensolver_all with indices=np.array([ik]), which blocks at defuqumat/solvers/davidson.py:1425 (`failed = ~np.asarray(per_k)`), then reads int(left[0]), np.asarray(values[0]) and int(step[0]). It is reached from Calculator.get_magnetic_bands(scf=True) (defuqumat/calculator.py:1669-1672) after refuse_regime. Three parts of the claim do not hold. First, the upload at fold.py:223 does not happen at the default: run_magnetic_scf has `residual_threshold: float | None = None` (:241) and the branch at :220 is skipped; only fold_check (default 1e-9, :365) or a caller who passes the keyword reaches it. Second, the batching rule is not broken: the rule forbids a hand-written vmap over k, and this solve goes through map_k inside davidson_eigensolver_all (davidson.py:1179-1181). Third, one k-point in flight is the sized design: estimate_bytes is "the working set of one k-point's solve" (magnetic/blochgrid.py:868), refuse_memory uses it (fold.py:285-287), and the Calculator docstring says the memory of one k-point is estimated. On a CPU the default dial is one k-point at a time anyway, so nothing is lost there: the block at davidson.py:1425 waits for the solve itself, and the three later reads are of finished values. indices reaches the kernel as an array (davidson.py:1369), so one program is compiled, as the finder says. What I could not settle by reading is the case of a card with a small magnetic box and many representatives, where a k-chunk ('fit') could be faster than one call per k (the SCF's own figure on a 27-point mesh is 184.8 against 31.4 ms). No record in PLAN.md or notes/ORBITAL-FIELD-NEXT.md says route P was run or timed on a card, so this stays unmeasured. It is not visible on si8-1k or si16-1k.

Fix: Only if route P is timed on a card and the per-k calls show: hand all representatives to one davidson_eigensolver_all call with the default k_batch, size estimate_bytes for the chunk, and read `left` once after the call. In fold_check, build the residual_threshold array once outside the loop.

### `defuqumat/scf/continuation.py:703`: A single-precision source that went through a checkpoint loses the 1e-4 electron-count tolerance, because band_precision is only in the dropped history

Slice `scf-small`, hole, severity `minor (a refusal with a misleading message, never a wrong number)`, status **plausible**, known: partly: notes/incompat/I119.md:189 records the 1e-4 tolerance and that it is read off the history entry. The loss of that entry across a checkpoint is not recorded in OPEN.md, PLAN.md or the audits..

`_check_grid` decides whether the source ran its bands in single precision from `result.history[-1]['band_precision']`. `save_state` never writes `history`: it is in `_DROPPED`, and `band_precision` is not in `_SCALARS`. So a result read back by `load_state` has an empty history, `single_source` is False, and the source is held to `ELECTRON_TOL = 1e-6` relative instead of `SINGLE_SOURCE_ELECTRON_TOL = 1e-4`. When it misses, the error says the datasets differ by a `z_valence`, which is the wrong diagnosis. The counts measured in I119 still pass, but narrowly: 8.3e-7 on the one-electron spinor H atom against an absolute bound of 1e-6, a margin of 1.2x. So this is a wrong refusal waiting for a slightly larger float32 normalisation error, not a wrong number. A fix is to carry `band_precision` as a scalar in the checkpoint, or to read it off the `calculation` given to `load_state`.

Trigger: `DEFUQUMAT_BAND_PRECISION=single` (or `band_precision='single'`) on an SCF, followed by `Calculation.load`/`load_state` (driver.py:2216), the SCF checkpoint resume (driver.py:8393) or the relaxation resume (workflows/relax.py:493), and then `run_scf(starting_from=...)`. It matters on a cell whose float32 count misses by between 1e-6 and 1e-4 relative.

```
continuation.py:702-704 `history = getattr(result, "history", None) or [{}]` / `single_source = history[-1].get("band_precision") == "single"` / `tolerance = SINGLE_SOURCE_ELECTRON_TOL if single_source else ELECTRON_TOL`; checkpoint.py `_DROPPED = ("stress", "solver", "history", ...)` and `_SCALARS` without `band_precision`; driver.py:9950 is the only place `band_precision` is recorded, in a history entry
```

Skeptic: The path is real. continuation.py:702-704 reads the precision of the source off `result.history[-1]`. checkpoint.py:168 has `history` in _DROPPED, `band_precision` is not in _SCALARS (:114-130), and load_state builds its fields from _SCALARS only (:406), so `SCFResult.history` comes back as its default empty list (driver.py:2094). Then `or [{}]` makes single_source False and the source is held to ELECTRON_TOL = 1e-6 (:131). The three load routes reach the check: Calculation.load (driver.py:2208-2217), the SCF checkpoint resume (driver.py:8393 then :8490-8491, continued_state, continuation.py:1229) and the relaxation resume (relax.py:493). Nothing upstream refuses a single-precision run that is checkpointed. What I could not settle is a valid input that fails. The bound is relative to max(1, nelec), and the counts measured in I119 on the regimes that single precision still admits are 8.3e-7 of one electron (spinor H), 1.6e-6 of eight (2.0e-7 relative) and 2.9e-6 of six (4.8e-7 relative), all under 1e-6 (continuation.py:133-141). The two that missed, 1.2e-6 and 1.1e-6 (notes/incompat/I119.md, finding 2), are constrained-moment runs, and _require_band_precision_supported now refuses those in single precision (driver.py:3190-3207). So on every measured admitted cell the reloaded source still passes, the closest at 83 per cent of the bound. A cell with a larger float32 normalisation error (a larger sphere on a one-electron cell is the natural candidate) would be refused with the z_valence message, and for the checkpoint resume that means the resubmitted job stops. This is unmeasured. The loss across a checkpoint is not in OPEN.md, PLAN.md or the audits, and no test loads a single-precision state (the only user of the tolerance outside continuation.py is the I119 note).

Fix: Store the source's band precision in the checkpoint metadata as a scalar and have _check_grid read it from the result (a field, or a one-entry history rebuilt by load_state), not from the target calculation, whose precision can differ. Add a test that saves a single-precision state, loads it and continues.

## Raised by a skeptic or a second reader, not verified by anyone else (25)

### `defuqumat/realtime/propagate.py:1515`: The real-time checkpoint is written in place, so a kill during the write leaves a file the resume cannot open

Slice `realtime`, bug, severity `crash`, status **raised by skeptic**.

The checkpoint is written by np.savez straight onto its final name after every k-chunk, with no temporary file and rename (a grep for '.partial', 'os.replace' and 'rename' in defuqumat/realtime/ finds nothing, while the SCF's checkpoint.py does the rename). A run killed while the zip is being written leaves a truncated archive under the checkpoint name. The next run finds it at line 1455 and calls np.load, then reads saved['signature'], with no try around either, so it stops on a zipfile error instead of ignoring the file as it does for a wrong signature. The window is short, since the file holds only the current and the energy, but a kill by a time limit is the case the checkpoint exists for. Stated from reading; the exact exception type was not run.

Trigger: run_realtime or propagate with checkpoint='rt.npz' at a frozen potential on any cell, killed (Slurm time limit, OOM) while np.savez is inside its write; the resubmitted run raises at np.load instead of resuming or recomputing.

```
propagate.py:1455-1457 `if checkpoint is not None and Path(checkpoint).exists():\n        saved = np.load(checkpoint)\n        if str(saved["signature"]) == signature:`; propagate.py:1514-1516 `if checkpoint is not None:\n            np.savez(checkpoint, signature=np.asarray(signature), current=current,\n                     energy=energy, norm_drift=norm_drift, excited=excited, done=index + 1)`
```

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1670`: get_magnetic_bands(scf=True) silently drops chern and local_only, and scf=False drops conv_thr

Slice `magnetic`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **raised by skeptic**.

get_magnetic_bands takes chern, local_only and conv_thr as named parameters. The scf=True branch calls run_magnetic_scf without chern and local_only, so chern=True returns a MagneticSCF with no Chern numbers and no error, and local_only=True runs the full Hamiltonian with the nonlocal term. The scf=False branch does not forward conv_thr, so a conv_thr given there is never read (the solve uses run_magnetic_bands's own ethr). This is the same class as F2 and F3: a named facade parameter that one branch ignores. Fix: refuse chern=True and local_only=True when scf=True, and either forward conv_thr as ethr or refuse it when scf=False.

Trigger: calc.get_magnetic_bands(flux=(1, 6), kmesh=(2, 6), nbnd=..., scf=True, chern=True) on graphene, or the same call with local_only=True; calc.get_magnetic_bands(flux=(1, 6), nbnd=..., conv_thr=1e-10) with scf=False

```
calculator.py:1646-1647 `def get_magnetic_bands(self, flux=(1, 1), kmesh=(2, 2), nbnd=None, scf=False, chern=False, local_only=False, conv_thr=1e-8, **options):`; calculator.py:1669-1672 `if scf: return run_magnetic_scf(self.system, self.pseudos, state, flux=flux, kmesh=kmesh, nbnd=nbnd, conv_thr=conv_thr, calculation=self.calculation, **options)`; calculator.py:1673-1674 `return run_magnetic_bands(self.system, self.pseudos, state, flux=flux, kmesh=kmesh, nbnd=nbnd, chern=chern, local_only=local_only, **options)`
```

### `defuqumat/wannier/disentangle.py:68`: A frozen window with its ends swapped freezes nothing and the run goes on

Slice `wannier`, hole, severity `missing-refusal`, status **raised by skeptic**.

With dis_froz=(a, b) and a > b, frozen_states is True (engine.py:132) but the test at disentangle.py:68 is true for every band, so the frozen mask stays empty and the disentanglement runs as if no frozen window was given, with no message. Wannier90 refuses this in param_read (wannier90-3.1.0/src/parameters.F90:1689-1690, 'check disentanglement frozen windows'), and the port carries neither this check nor the one for dis_win (parameters.F90:1676-1677). It is the same family as F1: the checks of param_read on the windows were not carried over when the file input was taken away. Found by reading, not run.

Trigger: Silicon as in docs/guide/wannier.md, get_wannier(8, ['Si:sp3'], num_bands=12, divisions=(4, 4, 4), dis_win=(None, 17.0), dis_froz=(6.4, -10.0)): the valence bands are not kept exactly and nothing says so.

```
defuqumat/wannier/disentangle.py:68 `if e[i] < dis_froz_min or e[i] > dis_froz_max:` followed by `continue` ; defuqumat/wannier/engine.py:132-133 `win_min, win_max, o.dis_froz_max is not None, froz_min,` / `0.0 if o.dis_froz_max is None else o.dis_froz_max,` ; defuqumat/workflows/wannier.py:586 `window["dis_froz_min"], window["dis_froz_max"] = dis_froz` with no order check before or after
```

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:9669`: The Hubbard occupation matrix reads the wavefunction store after the loop has parked it

Slice `opt-jit-host`, optimization, severity `memory`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: new: grep of OPEN.md, PLAN.md, notes/GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md and notes/MEMORY-AUDIT.md for the park comment and for the occupation matrix beside the park found nothing.

The SCF loop parks the store at driver.py:9523 and its comment says this is the last read of the store in the iteration, so that the energy terms, v_of_rho and the mixer run with the wavefunctions off the card. A DFT+U run that does not stream reads the store again at :9668-9669, after the park and after the batched fetch, with no fetch_wavefunctions before it. With wfc_store='device' the park is a no-op (batching.py:1221) and with 'stream' the streamed pass has already produced streamed_ns, so only wfc_store='host' is affected: there park_wavefunctions returns the pinned-host array (batching.py:1242-1251) and occupation_matrix is called on it. I could not settle by reading what JAX does with a pinned-host input to this jitted call: either the whole store, nk x nbnd x npol x npwx complex numbers, is copied back to the card once per attempt inside the window the park was meant to free, or the call fails. Not run, by the rule of this audit. tau_out and becsum_out are built before the park (:9499-9508), so the Hubbard term is the only one I found; I did not check the torque terms further down. The fix is the same move F1 asks for: build ns_out before :9523.

Trigger: Any DFT+U SCF (HUBBARD card, calculation.is_hubbard) run with wfc_store='host' on an accelerator that has pinned-host memory, at every attempt of every iteration. On this CPU workstation _host_sharding() gives no pinned memory and the store stays on the device with a warning, so no CPU test reaches it. Not on si8-1k or si16-1k (no U).

```
driver.py:9516  # **The last read of the store in this iteration**, so back to the buffer it goes.
driver.py:9523  wavefunctions = park_wavefunctions(wavefunctions, wfc_store)
driver.py:9668  ns_out = (streamed_ns if streaming
driver.py:9669            else calculation.occupation_matrix(wavefunctions, wg))
batching.py:1221  if psi is None or where == "device": return psi
batching.py:1251  return jax.device_put(psi, sharding)
```

### `defuqumat/kpm/traces.py:383`: The exact unfolding builds the unit start vectors of every k-point at once as a dense (nk, width, ndim) array

Slice `kpm`, optimization, severity `memory`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: new; my grep of PLAN.md P168 and OPEN.md found no size for it.

With trace='exact', the default, run_kpm_unfolded allocates a float64 host array of shape (nk, width, ndim) and then a complex copy of it for the device. Here width is the number of primitive-cell plane waves and ndim the number of supercell plane waves (both doubled for a spinor). Each row has one nonzero entry. The recursion itself holds about three blocks of (width, ndim) per k-point in flight, so with a small k chunk the start array is nk / 3 times the working set of the recursion. By the formula, a 64-atom silicon supercell at 12 Ry on a 60-point path is of order 1 GB complex plus half of that on the host; I did not measure it. The memory rule asks for the peak in terms of nk and npwx, and PLAN P168 states none for this array. An integer index array (nk, width) and the unit vectors built inside one_k remove the factor ndim.

Trigger: calc.get_kpm_unfolded(primitive_lattice, kpoints) with the default trace='exact' on a large supercell and a long k-path; the size grows as nk x npw_primitive x npw_supercell x 24 bytes.

```
traces.py:381-387 `width = int(counts.max()); starts = np.zeros((nk, width, ndim)); for ik in range(nk): columns = np.flatnonzero(subset[ik]); starts[ik, np.arange(columns.size), columns] = 1.0` and traces.py:394 `starts = jnp.asarray(starts, dtype=hamiltonians[0].dtype)`
```

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:4432`: The k-chunk is sized on the SCF's k-set and follows the calculation to a larger k-list; for_bands re-resolves it only for more bands

Slice `opt-memory`, optimization, severity `memory`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: new for the k count; notes/GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md:352 records the band-count half, fixed by for_bands.

`at_kpoints` copies the calculation and never re-resolves `k_batch`. On a card in default memory mode, `choose_k_batch` returns `k_batch = None` when the SCF's wedge fits, and None means the whole axis of whatever list comes next. The only re-resolution is `for_bands`, which returns `self` when the band count is not larger than the SCF's. So a consumer that moves to a larger k-list at the same band count runs every k-point in one vmap, with a chunk that was sized for the wedge. F1 is one case of this (the KPM routes never call `for_bands`). The other KPM traces on the whole grid (kpm/traces.py:138, then :148 and :400 with `k_batch=batch`) and an NSCF or DOS on a denser mesh at the SCF's band count take the same dial. I read the dial path only. I did not follow the NSCF solve far enough to say whether another guard (the streamed store, the speed-mode fit check) catches it there, so for the NSCF this is plausible and not settled. notes/GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md:352-358 records the same defect for the band count ('The dials are sized for the SCF's bands') and says nothing about the k count.

Trigger: A card, default memory mode, an SCF whose wedge fits (so `k_batch = None`), then `get_kpm_conductivity` or a KPM PDOS/LDOS on a symmetric crystal (the whole grid is up to 48 times the wedge), or a fixed-density solve on a denser mesh with `nbnd` not above the SCF's. On a CPU the preset is one k-point and nothing changes.

```

### `defuqumat/kpm/moments.py:186`: spin_moments runs the Chebyshev recursion three times, once per Pauli matrix, on the same start vectors

Slice `kpm`, optimization, severity `perf`, status **raised by skeptic**.

cross_recursion is called three times with the same apply and the same start; only the bra changes. Each call has its own lax.scan with N - 1 applications of H, so the spin trace costs 3 (N - 1) applications per k and per random vector. One scan that takes the three brackets of each Chebyshev vector needs N - 1. The scalar trace with the doubling trick costs N / 2, so spin_resolved=True adds about six times the cost of the DOS itself where two times is enough. Three separate scans are three loops in the compiled program, and I do not expect XLA to merge them; I did not measure it. QE has no equivalent.

Trigger: Every get_kpm_dos(spin_resolved=True). The cost is n_random x 3 (N - 1) Hamiltonian applications per k-point, with N in the hundreds to thousands at the default broadening, on any spinor cell.

```
moments.py:186-187 `return jnp.stack([jnp.real(cross_recursion(apply, start, pauli(start, axis), n_moments)) for axis in range(3)])`; moments.py:150-154 `following = 2.0 * apply(current) - previous ... lax.scan(step, (start, first), None, length=n_moments - 2)` inside each cross_recursion
```

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1497`: get_electrostriction on an empty slot solves the six strains and does not keep them, so get_elastic_constants solves them again

Slice `opt-repeat`, optimization, severity `perf`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: notes/AUDIT-2026-09-18.md hole.8 records it as accepted: "only costs a second strain response, a time cost rather than a wrong number". Not in OPEN.md or PLAN.md as an item to do..

With an empty slot get_electrostriction passes strain=None, electrostriction solves the strain response itself (electrostriction.py:1041-1045), and the calculator does not store it. A later get_elastic_constants calls get_strain_response (calculator.py:1445-1446), finds the slot empty and solves the six strains again. The reverse order reuses the slot, so the cost depends on the order of two calls. The strain response is "the expensive half" by the code's own docstring (electrostriction.py:950-951, calculator.py:1394-1395). A way around it exists inside one call, elastic=True (electrostriction.py:952-956). The two solves are not the same options (re-diagonalised states and no degeneracy_tol in electrostriction), so storing the response back needs a decision about the key; it is the same decision as N1.

Trigger: On a fresh calculator, calc.get_electrostriction() and then calc.get_elastic_constants(): two strain responses of six strains each, where the reverse order runs one

```
calculator.py:1497 `options.setdefault("strain", self._strain_response)`; electrostriction.py:1041-1045 `if strain is None: strain = strain_response(calculation, psi, eigenvalues, density, becsum, verbose=verbose, walk=walked, **response_options)`; calculator.py:1445 `response = self.get_strain_response(`
```

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:2699`: The constructor comment and the guide say a new geometry rebuilds the projectors in double, but at_positions keeps the single core and at_strain(_moving) casts back to single

Slice `driver-b`, bug, severity `doc-drift`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: new; the guide sentence and the constructor comment are the only records and both state the opposite of the code.

The comment at driver.py:2699-2701 names at_positions as a mover that rebuilds the core in the cell's precision, and docs/guide/performance.md:381-383 says a calculation moved to a new geometry runs fully in double. at_positions (3747 to 3844) never rebuilds the core: its only projector line reuses self.projector_core, which the constructor cast to the band precision, so the moved copy stays single. at_strain(_moving=True), the route at_cell takes, casts the rebuilt core back to the band precision by design (4091-4100). So for a new geometry the code keeps single, the opposite of what the comment and the guide state; only at_kpoints and at_spiral_q go to double. No number is wrong, since forces, stress and relaxation are refused in single (forces/energy.py:480-536), so the statement is reached only by a caller who moves the atoms by hand and runs an SCF.

Trigger: calc = Calculation(system, pseudos, band_precision='single'); run_scf(system_moved, pseudos, calculation=calc.at_positions(new_positions)) runs its bands in single, where the guide says double. Same for calc.at_cell(new_at).

```

```

### `defuqumat/magnetic/conventions.py:83`: The error text says a field along -a3 is the cell with a3 reversed, which does not reverse the field and gives the cell the plaquette guard rejects

Slice `magnetic`, bug, severity `doc-drift`, status **raised by skeptic**.

MagneticCell.build refuses p < 1 and tells the reader that a field along -a3 is the cell with a3 reversed. Reversing a3 changes the sign of both a3 and det(at), so b1 and b2 of inv(at).T are unchanged and a(r) = 2 pi n_phi (b1.r) b2 is the same vector field: the field in space does not move. The reversed cell is left-handed, so on the Peierls route plaquette_check then raises (see F5). The advice is therefore wrong twice. Route P has its own way (p < 0 in magnetic_cell, blochgrid.py:55 and :655), which is the correct pointer. Fix together with F5: say that the field is along a1 x a2, and point to p < 0 on route P.

Trigger: run_landau_levels(wannier, flux=(-1, 40)) raises with this advice; following it (the same crystal with a3 reversed, then flux=(1, 40)) reaches the plaquette ValueError of F5 on any Wannier set with self-hops at +-a1 and +-a2

```
conventions.py:81-83 `raise ValueError(f"the flux p/q needs positive p and q, got {p}/{q} " "(a field along -a3 is the cell with a3 reversed)")`; conventions.py `b_chem`: `return jnp.linalg.inv(self.cell.at).T`; conventions.py `a`: `return (TPI * self.flux * self.scale) * (r @ b[0])[..., None] * b[1]`; conventions.py:194-195 `orientation = np.sign(np.dot(np.cross(lattice[0], lattice[1]), lattice[2]))`
```

### `tests/regression/test_anisotropy.py:316`: No test compares a torque at the system's own orientation on a symmetry-reduced k-set with the nosym run, and the committed grid cannot show the difference along z

Slice `magnetism-response`, hole, severity `test-gap`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: new (it belongs with F1; notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:168 records the 45 degree null only).

The only wedge test of the torque is the refusal of the turned branch. The docstring says that what would show the defect is a magnetic group the torque's axial perturbation is not invariant under, and that there is no such cell here. The committed tetragonal cobalt cell with the moment along z has such a group (the fourfold axis mixes the transverse components), but its 3 3 2 unshifted grid makes every k-point a fixed point of the mirror z -> -z, so each k-point gives zero transverse torque and the test cell is a null by construction. A grid with a kz layer away from 0 and 1/2 is needed for the check to be able to fail (the trap of a null result that cannot be told from a pass).

### `defuqumat/scf/driver.py:1821`: default_nbnd drops NINT(1.2*nelup) outside nspin = 2, so a smeared run with a large odd nelec gets one band fewer than pw.x (two as a spinor), independent of the rounding

Slice `driver-a`, bug, severity `minor`, status **raised by skeptic**.

setup.f90:381-383 takes the MAX with NINT(1.2*nelup) in every regime, and nelup = (nelec+1)/2 for an odd integer nelec. The last return of default_nbnd has only round(1.2*nelec/2) and occupied + 4. Once 1.2*nelec/2 wins over occupied + 4 (nelec above about 44), the 0.6 that the odd electron adds changes the rounding for nelec = 5, 7 or 9 mod 10. By hand: nelec = 45 gives 27 here and 28 in pw.x; 47 gives 28 and 29; 49 gives 29 and 30. This is a second defect inside F1 and not the half-to-even one: 47 is 3 mod 4, and replacing round by _nint does not repair 45 or 49 either. The run is still correct, only its default band count differs from the one pw.x prints.

Trigger: nspin = 1 or noncolin, smearing or tetrahedra, no nbnd, odd nelec of 45 or more with nelec mod 10 in 5, 7, 9. For example five Co atoms with a 9-electron dataset (nelec = 45): 27 bands here, 28 in pw.x, and 54 against 56 as a spinor.
