### `defuqumat/system/symmetry.py:1155`: The symmetry phases are built complex128 by literal, so sym_rho promotes a single-precision density to double

Slice `traps`, bug, severity `minor`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: new. OPEN.md:1913 (S1, closed 2026-10-09) is the change that wrote the dtype=complex line, and notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1139 prices the memory of the same routine, not its dtype. The class is on record: AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:965 (frequencies pinned to float64) and :1331 (a float64 zeros block)..

symmetry_maps returns its phases with a literal dtype: jnp.ones(..., dtype=complex) for a symmorphic group and jnp.asarray of a NumPy complex128 array otherwise. x64 is on, so both are complex128 whatever Cell.precision says. apply_symmetry_maps multiplies them into rho_g, and the walked form asks for jnp.result_type(rho_g, phases), so a complex64 density comes back complex128 at every SCF iteration and everything built from it follows. No number is wrong in float64, where the site is inert. What is lost is the single-precision mode on the dense grid, silently. I did not run it, and I did not check that an SCF on a SINGLE system is not refused earlier, or that the density is float32 when it enters sym_rho.

Trigger: A system built in single precision with the point group on, for instance build_system(parse_pw_input(text), precision=SINGLE) as tests/unit/test_sizing_per_dataset.py:273 builds it, on any cell with more than the identity (silicon, pw_scf/scf.in), then run_scf. Both the symmorphic and the nonsymmorphic branch do it.

```
defuqumat/system/symmetry.py:1154-1157: `if not np.any(translations): return jnp.asarray(permutations), jnp.ones((len(rotations), 1), dtype=complex)` / `phases = np.exp(-2j * np.pi * (miller @ translations.T)).T` / `return jnp.asarray(permutations), jnp.asarray(phases)`. Consumer, symmetry.py:1109-1111: `if _walks_operations(permutations.shape[0], rho_g): return _walked_scalar(rho_g, permutations, phases)` / `return jnp.mean(phases * rho_g[permutations], axis=0)`, and :736-737 `jnp.result_type(rho_g, phases)`. Callers on the SCF path: scf/driver.py:3082 `self._symmetry_maps = symmetry_maps(dense, self.symmetries) if use_symmetry else None`, driver.py:436 `rho_g = apply_symmetry_maps(r_to_g(rho, fft_index), permutations, phases)` and :520.
```

### `defuqumat/workflows/shg.py:124`: get_shg and get_shift_current cannot run on a ground state with a magnetic field or a potential-only meta-GGA, and the error names a remedy neither entry point accepts

Slice `optics`, hole, severity `minor`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: new. OPEN.md mentions run_shg and run_shift_current only for the doubled constructor (line 1433) and the SHG items near 8816 to 9056; the field sweeps in notes/incompat (I58, I64, I65, I73, I106, I118) do not name SHG or the shift current; tests/unit/test_sibling_refusals.py:437 covers the Sternheimer stack only. The guide's SHG and shift-current Limits paragraphs (docs/guide/nonlinear.md:477-494, as far as I read them) list neither a magnetic field nor a meta-GGA..

run_shg and run_shift_current have no field, field_scale or tau argument (a grep for 'field' and 'tau' in both workflow files returns nothing), so Calculator._call_options, which forwards a state argument only when the name is in the callee's signature, cannot hand them SCFResult.magnetic_field, field_scale or tau. Both call fixed_density_states without them, and fixed_density_hamiltonians then raises: for any calculation.magnetic_field (also at reducebf = 1) a ValueError saying to pass field = scf_result.magnetic_field and field_scale = scf_result.field_scale, and for tb09 or bj06 a NotImplementedError saying to pass tau = scf_result.tau. A user who follows the message gets a TypeError. run_conductivity takes and forwards all three, so this is the sibling that diverged (its own comment at workflows/conductivity.py:163-183 describes the same history). It is not a wrong answer, the error fires before the diagonalisation; it is a regime that is refused by accident and under a misleading message. TB09 is the functional one would choose for an optical gap, so the SHG case is not exotic. If the arguments are added, the second potential build at workflows/shg.py:141 and workflows/photocurrent.py:139, calculation.potential(density) with no field and no tau, must take them too, or it becomes the wrong-answer form (driver.py:4949 falls back to the input field at scale 1.0).

Trigger: Calculator.get_shg(nbnd=...) or get_shift_current on (a) an insulator with occupations = 'fixed' and B_field set (nspin = 2 or noncollinear), or (b) silicon or AlAs with input_dft = 'tb09'. I did not read the input checks that admit B_field beside fixed occupations, nor the wedge's magnetic-group refusal, so case (a) is stated for a whole grid (nosym); case (b) needs neither.

```
workflows/shg.py:124-127 `fixed_density_states(system, pseudos, density, nbnd=nbnd + 1, conv_thr=conv_thr, k_batch=k_batch, ns=ns, becsum=becsum, calculation=calculation,)` and :141 `potential = calculation.potential(jnp.asarray(density))`; workflows/photocurrent.py:123-125 and :139 the same without becsum; workflows/nscf.py:344-354 `if calculation.functional.is_meta and tau is None: ... raise NotImplementedError(... tau = scf_result.tau ...)` and :357-365 `if calculation.magnetic_field is not None and field is None: raise ValueError("a fixed-density run of a calculation with a magnetic field or a constrained moment needs the field the SCF ended with: pass field = scf_result.magnetic_field and field_scale = scf_result.field_scale ...")`; calculator.py:356-362 _STATE_ARGUMENTS and :2909-2913 `if name in parameters and name not in merged`
```

### `defuqumat/calculator.py:1497`: get_electrostriction reads the strain slot without comparing the options that filled it

Slice `opt-repeat`, hole, severity `minor`, status **raised by skeptic**, known: notes/AUDIT-2026-09-18.md hole.8 (line 367-375) names the direct read and judged it the weaker half; the fix of 2026-09-18 keyed get_strain_response only and left this setdefault as it was. Not in OPEN.md..

get_strain_response returns its slot only when _same_options(merged, self._strain_response_options) holds (calculator.py:1428-1431), and its docstring names the unkeyed reuse as a defect. get_electrostriction bypasses this: it hands electrostriction whatever the slot holds. A slot filled at a loose tr2 is then used for the third derivative, next to a field solve at the default tr2, and nothing on the result says so. The only checks downstream are the route check (electrostriction.py:1037-1040) and require_converged_responses (electrostriction.py:883, a refusal of a solve that did not converge), and a response that converged at its own loose tr2 passes both. The slot's response is also built on the SCF's states with degeneracy_tol merged in (calculator.py:1422-1427), while electrostriction's own solve is on re-diagonalised states (electrostriction.py:1004, :1042-1045), so the two orders of calls do not build the tensor from the same response. I could not settle the size of the change in the tensor by reading.

Trigger: calc.get_strain_response(tr2=1e-6) and then calc.get_electrostriction() on the same calculator, for example on tests/data/qe/si-electrostriction.in

```
calculator.py:1497 `options.setdefault("strain", self._strain_response)`; calculator.py:1428-1431 `if self._strain_response is not None and _same_options(merged, self._strain_response_options): return self._strain_response`; electrostriction.py:1037-1041 `if strain is not None and is_host_store(strain.dpsi[0, 0]) != bool(walked): strain = None` / `if strain is None:`
```

## Refuted (2)

- `defuqumat/ultracell/driver.py:2235` (ultracell): A TB09 + DFT+U ultracell never mixes tau, so the whole loop runs at the tiled reference tau Refuted because: The if/elif chain is as the finder describes: defuqumat/ultracell/driver.py:2235 `elif ns_state is not None:` packs the density and ns only, :2247 `elif tau_state is not None:` cannot run when ns_state is set, and :2280-2281 `tau_state = new_tau` sits in the converged branch only. (Two of the finder's line numbers are off: tau_state is tiled at :1930-1931, not :1958, and ns_state is set at :1654, not :1963.) But the trigger is not a valid input. A meta-GGA with a Hubbard U is refused when the Calculation is built: defuqumat/scf/driver.py:2545-2546 `if self.functional.is_meta: self._require_meta_supported(system)` in Calculation.__init__ (def at :2423), and :3290-3302 `if getattr(system, "hubbard", None): raise NotImplementedError(f"{self.functional.name} with a Hubbard U is not implemented: ...")`. The finder checked require_an_ultracell_regime (driver.py:523-627) and require_an_ultracell_functional (ultracell/potential.py:175-225), which indeed do not name the pair, but not this one. The refusal is hit twice before the loop: the unit-cell reference the trigger needs ("converged by run_scf") builds a Calculation and raises, so no reference with both reference.tau and reference.ns exists, and run_ultracell itself builds its Calculation through fixed_density_states (driver.py:1470, workflows/nscf.py:225 `Calculation(system, pseudos, k_batch=k_batch, david=david)`), which raises again before line 1641. HubbardInput (hubbard/manifold.py:151) defines no __bool__ or __len__, so a present HUBBARD card is truthy. docs/guide/ground-state.md:285 lists a meta-GGA with a Hubbard U among the refusals. The claim that run_scf converges this pair correctly is therefore also false: _mix can pack all three, but the constructor never lets the pair in.
- `defuqumat/stress/energy.py:108` (forces): Stress (and so vc-relax) under lelfield is not refused and gains an origin-dependent ionic field term Refuted because: The finder read require_a_differentiable_cell but did not follow refuse_sawtooth to its end. defuqumat/stress/energy.py:110 calls refuse_sawtooth(calculation, "the stress", ...). In defuqumat/scf/sawtooth.py:176-183 that function raises for tefield and otherwise falls through to `refuse_berry_field(owner, quantity)` at line 183. sawtooth.py:194-200 then raises NotImplementedError when `getattr(owner, "berry_field", None) is not None`, and it even has a branch for quantity == "the stress" that cites the commented-out terms of stress.f90. Calculation.berry_field is set at scf/driver.py:3104-3107 whenever system.berry_field is not None, so a lelfield Calculation always carries it. Every stress entry goes through the guard: compute_stress at stress/__init__.py:132 (before the state is even unpacked), strained_energy and strained_energy_terms at stress/energy.py:86 and :101, and stress/autodiff.py:56. run_vc_relax calls compute_stress at workflows/vc_relax.py:324 and :424, and Calculator.get_stress reaches the same function. So the P145 regime gets a refusal by name at the first stress call, and the origin-dependent ionic term is never differentiated with respect to strain. The guide (stress.md:208), PLAN.md:245 and berryfield.py:96 are right as written. I did not find a test that asserts this refusal (two greps of tests/ for lelfield or Berry on a line with stress or vc found nothing), so that part is not settled.

These check out by my reading: - The doubling-trick indexing and the scan layout. - The Jackson factors (Weisse eq. 71) and the leading-order width; the Lorentz factors and LORENTZ_HWHM. - The closed-form count, with g_0 = 1, so the count above the interval is mu_0. - The stochastic scaling w_k n_live and the exact scaling w_k, in the DOS, the conductivity and the variate. - for_spin on every rebuilt k-set (threaded_calculation, whole_grid_calculation, unfolding). - The S^(-1/2) H S^(-1/2) fold against the five terms of _applied. This includes the null directions of a rank-deficient B, where W, D' and F all annihilate R^dagger e = 0. - S^(1/2) = 1 + U[(1+W)^-1 - 1]U^dagger for the raised PDOS vectors. - The unbiasedness of both control variates. The random phases are rebuilt with the same fold_in(key, channel) and fold_in(key, ik). - The a, b order in kubo_greenwood, the T = 0 and T > 0 DC limits and -f' = f(1-f)/T. - ritz_gap_level's channel-major ordering at nspin = 2. - The wedge-to-whole-grid move for the PDOS, LDOS and conductivity, and the refusal order in require_a_kpm_regime.  Noted but not reported: - The conductivity guard hardcodes 1e-6, where guard.TOLERANCE gives 1e-3 for float32. That is a performance mode only, and I did not measure it. - The guards fire only when a moment or norm exceeds mu_0. So an eigenvalue just outside the interval, with stochastic weight 1/n_live, passes below about cosh(N sqrt(2 eps)) ~ n_live. I did not quantify this; the 2 per cent Lanczos margin makes it unlikely. - control_corrections divides by cut_half = (top - cut)/2, which is zero or negative if E_F + filter_cut reaches the top of the interval. I found no valid default that gets there.  Not checked: - Whether fft_index is still a box index under the stick layout. point_vectors and primitive_subset rely on it. - The units and sign of the k step in velocity_tangents, and the spinor attributes (kinetic, projectors, mask) that _moving and point_vectors read. - The test files themselves. I only grepped them for spin_resolved.  I ran no Python and no pytest, and I changed nothing in the repository.
- `magnetic`: I read these in full: magnetic/conventions.py (gauge, sign, magnetic translations, refuse_regime), magnetic/peierls.py, magnetic/landau.py, workflows/landau.py, workflows/magnetic_bands.py and fold.py except fold_check and _solve. In blochgrid.py I read the docstring, momentum_multipliers, wavefunction_box, filter_to_box, the build of MagneticGridHamiltonian, apply, precondition, _gauge_phase, local_term and _two_grid, the kinetic term, magnetic_cell, estimate_bytes, refuse_memory, start_block and dense_matrix. I also read the Calculator's get_landau_levels, get_magnetic_bands and get_magnetizability, the Limits note of docs/guide/magnetic-field.md, and the units.py flux helpers. I checked these by hand and found them right: the Peierls sign (electron, pi = -i grad + a), the magnetic Bloch-sum factor exp(-i a(P).c_nu) on the ket column (derived from the dressed orbital under T(P)), that T(q a1) and T(a2) commute, the u-gauge and h-gauge multipliers 2 pi (m2 + kappa2 + p xi1) and 2 pi (m1 + kappa1 - p xi2), the metric kinetic sum, the flux area Omega/|a3|, free Landau (2n+1) B_ph, the fold weights 2q/(N1 N2 N3) and the coverage of the q translates (gcd(p, q) = 1), the density normalisation, the eband/q and deband terms against QE at convergence, and the cgs units of the magnetizability. The rational_flux p = 1 branch, which gives a large relative error near n_phi of 0.5 to 1, is stated in its own docstring and is not reported. I did not read route U: hybrid.py, ultracell_field.py, ultracell_nonlocal.py, gipaw.py and the body of landau_ultracell.py past its signature and argument checks, so the two newest commits (14e8514b, 4b643476) are not audited. I also did not read interpolate, restrict, _fine_maps, GridProjectors, grid_projectors and reference_matrix in blochgrid.py. No code was run, so every memory figure in F1 is a count from the code, not a measurement.

Checked by reading and found consistent: (1) M_mn across the G-sphere. kmesh gives k + b = k_nnlist + G with nncell = G. overlaps aligns i's Miller index G onto j's G + shift, which is c_k(G)* c_k'(G + G0), the right u_{k+b}. _difference asserts b per group. The US/PAW q_ij(b) comes from the shared, grouped b. (2) The b-vectors and B1 weights are computed in 1/angstrom from bg (2 pi included) divided by BOHR_TO_ANGSTROM, with the B1 test on the assembled list. (3) The frozen window in dis_windows is contiguous and inside the outer one. (4) The phases of D and d: W_jn = int Y_j(R d) Y_n(d), the e^{-i Rk.T} site phase, and the sphere gather k+G -> R(k+G) with e^{-i(k'+G').t} and its bijection refusal. The US/PAW d uses vkb at k' on the rotated coefficients. wann_main uses only D. (5) Spinors: the spinor branches of guiding_on_mesh and projection_matrices cannot be reached behind run_wannier's npol == 2 / spiral refusal, and band_representation, pao_matrices and SCDM each refuse npol != 1 too. (6) Interpolation: the ws_distance pair sign (tau_n - tau_m) and the 1/(ndegen count) weights.  Dropped as already recorded: SCDM at nspin = 2 and the PAW d matrix (PLAN.md P165 and P164, outstanding). Dropped as deliberate: zona in 1/bohr as pw2wannier90 reads it. I did not check whether Wannier90's own ws_distance moves the centres into the home cell before the search. - `realtime`: Read in full: realtime/propagators.py, pulse.py, spectra.py, propagate.py, orders.py, hubbard.py. Also selfconsistent.py lines 128 to 952 (the refusals, _KPart, _Run, the block, both drivers), hierarchy.py lines 1 to 330 (docstring, BiCGStab, the per-k solve and the projector split), the head of radial.py (the clamp at s_max), and workflows/realtime.py lines 69 to 468 and 550 to 604 (little group, k-set, state solve, run_realtime, run_realtime_dielectric, run_harmonic_orders).  Checked and found sound: - Unitarity. The Taylor4 bound |dt rho| <= 2 sqrt 2 is the one refused against, as dt_Ha <= 2 bound / reach. The centre shift is applied as S^-1(H - c S) on the augmented generator. The norm-growth check runs after each block. Padded zero steps are the identity. - Gauge and current. kappa = A/c with H(k + kappa), E = -dkappa/dt by jacfwd, and the current is -grad/(2 Omega). The midpoint rate has the kick's step taken out and is crossed by the RK4 jump. - Symmetry. The field's little group excludes t_rev ops and keeps only ops that fix every polarisation vector, intersected with the grid stabiliser. The current is averaged as a polar vector, the density is completed with the little group, and spinor m is rotated as an axial vector. - Spinors. terms.potentials is a 1-tuple of the (4, ...) array, so v + |m| does enter the upper spectral bound. - Overlap. The hierarchy's P and complement split is correct algebraically for both S = 1 and S0. PAW ddd(t) is in the difference form. - Fourier window. fourier_component refuses a step that does not divide the period, and harmonic_spectrum windows the whole run. - Dropped as known (notes/incompat/I99.md, 'What is left'): propagate_orders_self_consistent starts its tower at v_scf. Its first-order tangent is 2 e.j_para, which time reversal makes zero on a mesh closed under -k, so run_realtime_dielectric at order 1 is exact. The kappa^2 rho term at order >= 2 needs a shape already on at the start, which no workflow builds.  Not read: - dense.py. - hierarchy.py beyond line 330: the self-consistent hierarchy, the GMRES outer solve and the choice of alpha. - The body of radial.py (_fit, _clenshaw). - selfconsistent.py lines 1 to 127. - workflows/realtime.py lines 468 to 550 and 604 to 848: run_hhg, chi2_from_orders, chi3_from_orders, run_third_harmonic, run_nonlinear_spectrum. The SI conversions and the 1/(p! 2^p) prefactors there were not audited and are the most likely unread place for a silent wrong number.

- `traps`: I found no trap site that meets the bar, so the findings list is empty. This is a grep-driven reading audit at b5b88c5c, with no Python run and about 25 tool calls. It is not a full sweep, and the gaps are listed at the end.  What I checked, and why each candidate was dropped:  Hardcoded dtypes. There is no jnp.complex128, jnp.float64, jnp.complex64 or jnp.float32 anywhere in defuqumat/ outside the config.py docstring. There are 197 complex-literal lines, and I read about 40 of them, mostly in wannier/, workflows/, scf/orientation.py, magnetic/ and kpm/. They sit in NumPy host code: Wannier I/O and disentanglement, the Peierls tight-binding, the dense reference of blochgrid.py:945-966, and the realtime spectra post-processing. A bare 1j scalar is weakly typed in JAX, so it does not upcast a complex64 array. Only a literal frozen into an array can upcast. I did not trace the callers of the two candidates of that kind, scf/orientation.py:160 (the sigma array) and workflows/spiral_soc.py:113.  Clamps. I read every jnp.clip, jnp.maximum and jnp.minimum in the package (36 sites). The ones in batching, davidson, mixing and pseudo/projectors act on integer indices or on solver thresholds that are never differentiated. The occupations.py clips are at an overflow bound, where the true derivative is about exp(-200). The xc/functional.py maximum ties only at exactly RHO_THRESHOLD. scf/fields.py:641 and :647 sit in site_residuals, a diagnostic that is not differentiated; the _unit_cosine scale defect there is already in notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:383. kpm/conductivity.py:318 is the KPM kernel window, which is not differentiated.  abs and sqrt. xc/mgga.py:276 is sqrt(max(sigma, 0)), but x_red enters only squared. The caller tb09_potential masks inactive points with a two-sided where, and tb09_coefficient already has the cone guard of I120. A trigger would need sigma exactly zero at an active point, which I cannot name without running. forces/spiral.py:636 and :641 use abs(psi)**2 on frozen psi, and the gradient goes through the kinetic term only. stress/analytic.py:176, :191 and :441 are the cross-check transcription, not the gradient. pseudo/harmonics.py:94 and pseudo/projectors.py:617 guard |k+G| = 0, and the Ewald, vdW and analytic-force distance sqrt calls are all behind a where. _polar_angle (atan2) and _unit_cosine (where on present) in scf/fields.py are guarded.  Hand-rolled vmap over k. response/chunked_strain.py:386 maps over a chunk that local(big, rowset) has already cut, so the k dial is not bypassed. scf/driver.py:1164 and :1174 map over the row set of a chunk. topology/berry.py:288 and topology/states.py:372 are tight-binding ModelStates, not plane-wave states. scf/density.py maps over the spin channel. An unfiltered vmap grep over magnetic/, kpm/, ultracell/ and wannier/ finds only blochgrid.py:170-173 and :534-537, which map over grid rows, not over k.  Wedge detected by a spread in the weights. kpoints.py:604 is the documented fallback under is_reduced. system/wedge.py:182 tests the uniformity of orbit shares, which is a different question. scf/driver.py:493 compares the k-set against a rebuilt spin-space-group wedge. transport.py:984 and gvectors.py:333 apply ptp to coordinates.  np.asarray and float() on a differentiated quantity. The sites in hubbard/energy.py, scf/potential.py, scf/fields.py, forces/energy.py and scf/ewald.py are host-side setup, or the QE-transcribed cross-checks (qe_hubbard_potential and its two siblings). The only autodiff in magnetic/, ultracell/, kpm/, wannier/ and topology/ is the KPM velocity jvp (kpm/conductivity.py:162 and :184) and a model jacfwd. Neither has a host conversion inside the differentiated function.  Stencil containing its own centre. magnetic/fold.py run_magnetizability uses E(+1) - 2E(0) + E(-1) on one magnetic box for all three fluxes, which is recorded in PLAN P177. A second stencil, magnetic/landau.py:97, holds its own centre: bands([K, K + dk, K - dk]) for v_F at the zone corner. Whether it is P48 depends on whether bands can be a plane-wave band call rather than a Wannier interpolation, and I did not trace its callers. It is the first thing for a follow-up to check.  Not reached: the np.asarray sites in pseudo/augmentation.py (48 hits) and forces/spiral.py (12 hits); the abs and sqrt sites in xc/functional.py (14) and scf/potential.py (7), which top the count; realtime/ beyond propagate.py:717; the remaining 157 or so complex-literal lines; and a systematic map of who calls each jitted function.
- `magnetism-response`: I read all of tddft/spinkernel.py (B_xc/m, the node limit, the kernel matrix's G'-G order, the Hubbard channel, goldstone_residual). In tddft/spinchi0.py I read require_a_transverse_regime, larmor_frequency, hubbard_pairs, commensurate_shift, augmentation_factors and the whole transverse_response / _one_k_terms walk, including the degenerate-pair branch and the spinor (I59) layout. In tddft/kernels.py I read alda_matrix, the GGA column route and refuse_a_polarized_alda. In workflows/magnons.py I read _for_spin, run_spin_susceptibility from the regime checks on, _locate, run_magnon_dispersion and _default_nbnd. In workflows/anisotropy.py I read the result classes, _refuse_system, _reference_axis, _with_quantization_axis, _with_rotation, _turned_on_its_own_wedge, _turn, run_torque, run_orientation_torque and _orientation_torque, and the head of relax_orientation. I also read all of system/spiral.py and the contraction and refusals of workflows/spiral_soc.py.\n\nFor the four named checks: (1) The nspin=2 kernel where a channel vanishes. The transverse kernel is finite at full polarization, and the double where keeps NaN out of the ratio. The node limit is used only inside the density window. The polarized charge-channel 'alda' is refused by moment. I did not trace the unpolarized charge-channel ALDA in a cell with vacuum. (2) The Goldstone mode. The identity is read at omega=0 with eta=0, at the Larmor point under a field, and the Hubbard channel's halves are carried. I found nothing wrong. (3) The generalized Bloch phase. In system/spiral.py the rotated-frame argument is consistent: a nonsymmorphic operation carries Rot_z(q.f), the moments are compared as they are, and there is no time reversal. I did not read workflows/spiral.py at all. (4) The anisotropy sign. On the result classes K1 = -torque/sin(2 theta) agrees with E = K1 sin^2 theta, and the easy axis is the argmin of the band energy. I did not read the sign inside forces/torque.py's torque_at_angle.\n\nNot read: all of tddft/chi0.py (the newest commits, I94 to I96 and I8), tddft/dyson.py, the body of workflows/tddft.py, workflows/spiral.py (relax_spiral_q, dE/dq), and the force-theorem and relaxed-anisotropy routes of anisotropy.py (run_force_theorem, _force_theorem, frozen_expectation, run_relaxed_direction).\n\nOpen questions I dropped. First, the spiral_soc wedge: spiral_spin_orbit_energy sums an axial per-k vector with no symmetrisation, but builder.py:2584 refuses an lspinorb spiral without nosym from an input file. I did not check whether System.with_spin or from_keywords can reach that state. Second, under tot_magnetization with smearing (two Fermi levels), the degenerate-pair branch of _one_k_terms substitutes slope_up where f_up - f_dn does not vanish. It needs an up and a down band degenerate to 1e-8 Ry, so I left it out. I ran no code. Nothing here is measured."

It says that the bands past the cutoff are solved on an indefinite operator, and only a measurement settles whether that costs anything. The advisor was rate-limited, so the finding is unreviewed.  I read sternheimer.py lines 240-1220 and 1436-1790. That covers the solver's __init__ (masks, per-channel counts, density weights), chunk_arrays and on_chunk, `_operator`, both branches of `project`, `_smeared_projection`, the preconditioner, the CG in solve_at, solve, solve_many and the array forms, density_parts, response_density and response_becsum, the raw becsum, localdos, fermi_level_shift and its states form, the gap check at the cut, `_alpha_pv`, the regime guard, smearing_of and make_sternheimer. In efield.py I read lines 200-800 and 942-1380: default_tr2, the dielectric_tensor loop and its convergence test, `_WholeField`, the sign flip in `_solve_stored`, `_bare_plus_induced`, the collinear and spinor forms of the augmentation dipole and of ultrasoft_position, `_assemble_overlaps` with symmatrix and its nosym guard, zstar_eu, the becsum symmetrisation, the textured-spinor and shifted-nosym refusals, and `_streams`. I also read born.py lines 123-730, all of mixing.py, walk.py lines 55-130, and chunked.py lines 98-480 and 728-760 (StreamedField and the extras pass). Outside the slice I read Calculation.symmetrize_directional in scf/driver.py.  Checked and found correct, with no finding: - d/dk sign and index order: `[H - eps S, r] = -i(dH/dk - eps dS/dk)`, and `_solve_stored`'s minus gives +P_c^+ rhs, so the solve returns P_c r psi. - `_smeared_projection`: wwg[j,i] order, the 0/0 limit and the alpha_pv cut on j. - `_alpha_pv` on both branches. - The dielectric assembly factor 4 x 4pi/Omega with wg, and the skip under nosym. - `ddv_scf` joint/npert/npol matches dfpt_kernels' `dr2 < npert tr2/npol`. - The `_multiplier_response` row mask and column weight, used by both routes. - The collinear and spinor (`abij`) contractions in ultrasoft_position, consistent with born's `_augmentation_expectation`. - Wedge symmetrisation: the charge is treated as a polar vector field, and at nspin_mag = 4 the magnetisation gets the polar x axial double rotation (textured spinors are refused before it in any case). - The Born-charge wedge completion through `_full_zone_field_response`, with the becsum double count removed. - fermi_level_shift takes the charge from channel 0 for a spinor. - Two Fermi levels with smearing are refused. - Padded chunk rows carry zero weight in every streamed sum. - Gamma-only storage is refused for the field, so the uncorrected plane-wave sums in overlaps are not reached. - Mixing complex phonon-at-q potentials is safe, because phononq splits real and imaginary parts. Anderson-mixing the one-centre potential instead of dbecsum changes the convergence metric, not the answer.

I read photocurrent.py lines 255-910: dipole_matrix, band_velocity_difference, generalized_derivative, shift_integrand, _sigma_at_k, the shift_current driver, all refusals and the wedge docstring. Checked and found consistent: - Drude units: plasma2/2 converts Ry^2 to Ha^2, and the interband sum is a ratio v^2/E^2, so it needs no conversion. - Tetrahedron delta at nspin=2: the weights sum to 1 per channel, so spin/ntetra is right, and dividing by w_k on a wedge is right. - The smeared delta's (E_F - e) sign. - Rule D4: the Drude block over |gap|<=tol is the complement of the interband >tol. band_velocity_difference's multiplet is pairwise, not a partition, but it stays invariant under rotations inside an exact multiplet, so I dropped it. - Wedge time-reversal images: the conductivity is transposed; the shift current is even under time reversal, and I checked the r(-k)=conj(r), r^{c;a}(-k)=-conj algebra; SHG takes the real part of its coefficients when the group has antiunitary elements. - The generalised velocity's e_m dS ordering matches the einsum layout. - Gamma-only storage with augmentation cannot occur, since it is substituted. - The shift current refuses US/PAW, metals, and DFT+U on US/PAW or noncollinear runs. - `strain`: Read in full: response/chunked_third.py, the walked d(eps)/dx; the elastic.py entry, the walked route and its refusals; the piezo.py result class and to_voigt; the polar-crystal and dataset guards; _Regime; the entry guard; clamped_ion_piezoelectric and _polar_norm_conserving; piezoelectric_from_strain_response; the piezoelectric_tensor and _piezoelectric_from_states driver; workflows/piezo_ladder.py from _rung to the end; the strain.py docstring and strain_response; the electrostriction.py result class, its driver, refined_states, the compliance and Voigt helpers and _require_closed_for_elastic. I also read the Calculator getters for strain, elastic, piezo and electrostriction. I read only the docstring and index of chunked_strain.py.  Conventions I checked, and none is a finding. The piezo Voigt form e_iJ = e_ijk with no factor 2 is correct for the engineering strain. C_IJ = C_ijkl is correct, and so is S = inv(C_voigt) with _compliance_tensor dividing by 2 per shear index. _to_voigt with no factors is correct for d(chi)/dx contracted over all nine pairs. strain_tangent's (E_ab+E_ba)/2 gives the symmetric-strain derivative, and the 0.5(col+col.T) on the stress index matches it. m and q swap the pair order to strain-first, and M = -S:m. The walked column, -4pi(dF/Omega - F dOmega/Omega^2), matches eps = 1 - 4pi F/Omega. The clamped-ion status of C, e, m, q and p is stated on each result. No relaxed-ion claim is made anywhere.  Leads I could not close within the budget, so they are not reported as findings: (a) _second_order_energy_at (electrostriction.py:392-393) and chunked_third global_ build PAW's D_ij with moved.onecenter from mixed_becsum. That is the raw sum over the k-set, never symmetrised. On a symmetry-reduced k-set with PAW and elastic=False, which _require_closed_for_elastic does not refuse, the primal D would then not be the ground state's, since D is nonlinear in becsum. Every augmented electrostriction test in tests/regression/test_electrostriction.py is nosym. I did not find whether a guard upstream (require_a_symmetrisable_response, require_a_sternheimer_regime) refuses PAW on a wedge. (b) The C_ijkl in elastic.py is (1/Omega) d2E/dx dx with at_strain applying (1+x). Off equilibrium this differs from the stress-strain constants by terms in the residual stress. The docstring defines it that way and validates against a five-point energy difference, so it is consistent, but the comparison with measured C_11 does not mention it. (c) _polar_norm_conserving with full_zone=False calls jnp.zeros_like(shifts). That would fail if _full_zone_field_response returns a tuple, which I did not check. It is a test-only flag.  Not read: the bodies of chunked_strain.py and of susceptibility_strain_derivative and _position_response, piezoelectric_zstar_eu_style and its _screened_strain_term and _multiplier_strain_term, constraint_strain_term, the self-consistent loop and multiplier_response in strain.py, and the metal-regime guards there (I88).
