In noncollinear.py I read the fields, _rows and _pair for spirals, space, _split and _join masking, apply_projected, _project, _unproject, _local_block, _nonlocal, diagonal, overlap_diagonal and precondition. In basis/ I read fftgrid.py whole and compared it with QE realspace_grid_init (fft_types.f90:1093, grid_set strict '<' at :1171, 2 nb + 1) and with allowed and good_fft_order (fft_support.f90); they match. In gvectors.py I read generate_gvectors (the '<=' cut, the round(g2, 12) tie sort), displaced_gvectors, _displaced_members, box_holding_displaced (I proved that a fold implies span > n on some axis, so the widening is complete) and _half_sphere. I also read the planewaves.py selection, the builder.py smooth-prefix assertion, the interpolate.py slicing and the fft.py scatters (padding entries point at G = 0 or slot 0 and are always masked before use; I found no padded row reaching a result).  Checked and found clean: the Davidson restart and collapse ordering, the locking semantics (not sticky, as in cegterg), the preconditioner (g_psi's new branch, padded entries harmless), the npwx mask through start, expansion, H application and the final states, the gamma G = 0 factors, and the FFT grid choice.  Considered and dropped: - The collinear diagonal einsum uses conj on the first projector, which is D^T. It is exact for real symmetric D and the spinor form is right, and it only touches the preconditioner. - Hamiltonian.matrix falls back to npwx FFT applications when ecutrho < 4 ecutwfc, although the modular gather is the same discrete convolution the FFT applies. Its docstring claim 'exact only if' looks wrong, but I did not check whether any non-test path reaches resolves_differences=False, so I do not report it. - A smooth-cutoff tie within the 1e-12 rounding of the sort would trip the builder's prefix AssertionError, a crash and not a silent error. I judged it too improbable to report without a concrete cell. - chunked_phonon's npw=None k+q Hamiltonians feed the Sternheimer solve, not Davidson.  Not reached: sticks.py and fft.py beyond the scatter and gather helpers, sample.py, gradients.py, the threshold schedule itself (scf/driver.py, outside the slice), resolve_degeneracy_tol's body, and a check of every caller of rayleigh_ritz for nvec < nbnd. No Python or tests were run, by the rules of the task.
- `topology`: This was a reading audit only: I ran no Python and no tests. I could not call the advisor (rate-limited), so these findings had no second review. Read in full or nearly in full: response/geometry.py, workflows/geometry.py (both routes of P161), topology/wilson.py, topology/links.py, topology/invariants.py, topology/berry.py (lines 100-383), topology/kubo.py (lines 60-286), topology/parity.py, topology/polarization.py, workflows/polarization.py, workflows/topology.py (DFTSource, _check_gap, _occupied_bands, the three entry points), topology/orbital_magnetization.py (header, the dense sums, the assembly), workflows/orbital_magnetization.py (lines 95-164), response/effmass.py (lines 40-112 and 320-663), response/magnetoelectric.py (lines 1-75 and 170-271). Outline only: response/nesting.py, topology/mesh.py. Not read: topology/states.py (overlaps and Miller-index alignment, parity_matrix, transport_plan), topology/augmentation.py, workflows/nesting.py, the streamed orbital-magnetization sums (lines 298-385), and response/velocity.with_connections. So I did not check by reading the e_n/e_m and K/K^dag placement that geometry.py says it shares with kubo_from_matrices. Checked and found clean: in the Wilson loop each link is unitarised and the loop closes with the reciprocal shift. The Z2 crossing parity is right for an even band count, and the largest-gap reference-line defect is already recorded (wilson.py docstring, PLAN P16). The polarization branch follows QE: placement around the complex average, a second pass against the first string, one fold per channel, and the doubling only at nspin = 1. The polarization quantum is 2 only for nspin = 1 with all-even valences, and 1 for spinors and for nspin = 2. The Chern number is returned as a float and never rounded, the FHS route is exact, and the Kubo routes count the singular points. The parity Z2 checks Hermiticity and +-1 eigenvalues and enforces Kramers parity at a degenerate TRIM, and shifting the centre by half a lattice vector leaves every product unchanged. In the quantum metric the Kubo and overlap normalisations agree at nspin = 1, 2 and 4 (wk sums and nwann). The overlap route rebuilds the tensor as S^-1 C S^-T, which is correct, and bg includes 2 pi. The metal refusal works on the fillings, the weight excludes occupied/occupied pairs so no multiplet enters, and the wedge average fits a covariant symmetric g(k). In the effective mass, the multiplet sums and the masks are applied before the bands slice.
- `transport-stm`: I read these fully or by function: stm/qpi.py (all of it), workflows/qpi.py (all of it), workflows/stm.py (run_stm body, run_sts, _finish_spectrum, sample_spectrum, _spectrum_energies, _refuse_a_reduced_k_set, _tip_orbit, _plane, _tip_energy, _tip_width, refuse_an_ultracell_result, _refuse_what_has_no_fermi_level with the I118 field change), transport/green.py:160-456 (amplitude_weights, channel_basis, multiplets, transmission, spin_transmission), workflows/transport.py (run_vertical_transport body, most of run_momentum_transport, _resolved_degeneracy_tol, _geometry, _assemble, _band_window, _flat_axis, _tip_sphere, _diagnose, _assemble_momentum, _top_multiplet_share, _top_multiplet_mask, _tip_points, _check_bias_axis, _energies, _vertical_wedge, _completed_on_a_wedge, _refuse_a_k_set_this_cannot_sum), transport/momentum.py:107-200, stm/spectrum.py (accumulate_spectrum, spectrum_weights), the bias branch of stm/image.py tunnelling_weights, workflows/sfac.py (all of it) and diffraction/structure_factor.py:1-60 and 177-356.  I checked these by hand and found them correct. The QPI T-matrix, the Bloch factorisation of G0(r, phi) with its phases, the Landauer delta T = 2 Re[G0* H] + K |G0|^2 with the S index order, fourier_map's tiling and q axes, the _divisions unitarity test (it also catches a time-reversal half grid), the twisted boundary of a shifted grid (the maps stay periodic because g_to and g_from carry opposite phases), the conjugation order in transmission and spin_transmission, the wedge completions of STS and the vertical map (antiunitary operations dropped, weights consistent with or without inversion), and the image-geometry pairing of exit plane and tip.  Two leads are known or unverified. First, np.real(weights) into momentum_weights on method='resolvent' (workflows/transport.py:1120, momentum.py:177) throws away the imaginary part. This is already recorded in notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md:1509, so I did not report it. Second, run_structure_factors on a spin spiral: _magnetization_field returns density[1:], which on a spiral is in the turning frame, and I found no spiral refusal in sfac.py or structure_factor.py. I did not check the Calculator facade or the builder for an upstream refusal. OPEN.md:6731 mentions structure factors next to spirals, so it may already be recorded. This is unverified and is not in the findings.  Not read: transport/substrate.py (exit_overlap, fold_onto_plane, volume_overlap), stm/plane.py, most of stm/image.py, momentum.py beyond 107-200 (MomentumTransport, fold_into_zone, pocket_mask, decay_constants), _constant_current, whole_grid, the acceptance helpers (_substrate_acceptance, _tip_acceptance, _collinear_acceptance), _refuse_an_augmented_plane, _warn_if_the_slab_is_not_between, structure_factor.py:60-176 (the HVectors and StructureFactors classes), and the run_stm signature and docstring. I went over the call budget at about 30 calls against 25. I ran no Python and no tests, so every finding comes from reading the code.
- `xc-io`: I read in full: xc/gga.py; xc/noncollinear.py (parity_form); xc/functional.py lines 400-460, 520-900 (the unpolarized and spin GGA gates, _sanitise, the zeta clamp and cut in _spin_correlation_energy, the grad_m energy and exchange) and 1056-1122 (safe_modulus, local_spin_frame); io/pwin.py; io/keywords.py; and the callers scf/potential.py:555-612 and paw/gradient.py:280-310. I read by index or grep only: lda.py (clamp_polarization only), mgga.py (the guards only), vdw/grimme.py (docstring, kernel and builder; I checked the sqrt mask at :227-229 and the sentinels at :288-289, both fine), io/qe_density.py (structure, gamma_only completion and becsum reshape, which PLAN.md P160 records as measured), and the celldm against CELL_PARAMETERS logic in system/builder.py:2128-2218, which is outside the slice. These checked out clean: the unpolarized GGA gate on |rho| and sigma; spin exchange scaling; the spin correlation clamp and the cut at |zeta| > 1; safe_modulus and the local_spin_frame direction at |m| = 0; the parity form's unit vectors at zero moduli; and the card option parsing, including `CELL_PARAMETERS (alat= x)`. I did not read: lda.py beyond the clamp; the mgga.py and qe_density.py bodies; functional.py:918-1055 (resolve_functional and the refusal of an unimplemented functional); io/elk.py, io/elk_density.py, io/qeref.py, io/output.py, io/dynmat.py; vdw/analytic.py and vdw/registry.py; and INPUT_PW.txt itself. I ran no code, so F1 is argued from the arithmetic of lines 863-866 and not measured. The advisor first suggested dropping F1, but its objection (S - 2A >= 0, and n - s < 0 gated to zero) does not touch the 0/0 at safe_s = 0, so I kept F1. My reconciliation call was rate-limited.

## What to address, in order

This is the list for a later session that can run the code. The order is that of
`CLAUDE.md`: a silent wrong number first, then a crash on a valid input, then a missing
refusal, then cost, then the tests and the docs. Each item names the entry above that
holds the code path and the trigger. The first step of every item is the same: make the
trigger into a small input, run it, and see whether the number moves. An item that does
not reproduce goes back to this file with the reason, and an item that does gets its
number in `PLAN.md` before it is fixed.

Silent wrong numbers:

1. `kpm/dos.py:357`: `spin_dos` sums over the symmetry-reduced wedge with the plain
   weights. The magnetization density is an axial field, so the wedge sum is not complete.
   Either complete it as the charge DOS is completed or refuse a reduced k-set. No test
   reads `spin_dos` at all (`kpm` N3).
2. `workflows/conductivity.py:225`: a run with two Fermi levels (`tot_magnetization`)
   gives one mean Fermi level to both spin channels, so each Drude term sits at the wrong
   energy.
3. `workflows/pdos.py:592`: the PDOS tetrahedra take the rotations of the group that
   ignores `nosym`, with time reversal forced on. This is wrong for a `nosym` run and
   under `noinv`.
4. `response/phononq.py:1733`: the phonon at `q != 0`, and the electron-phonon coupling
   built on it, refuse a wedge only through `use_symmetry`, which is false when the group
   is the identity alone. A P1 crystal still has its k-set halved by time reversal, and at
   `q != 0` the partner -k does not give the same term, so D(q) is wrong.
5. `response/phononq.py:1637`: at `q != 0` only the Ewald sum is moved to `q`. The
   Grimme-D2 Hessian stays at Gamma.
6. `calculator.py:1497`: `get_electrostriction` pairs a strain response solved on the
   SCF states with the refined states as primal. No test hands it what the Calculator
   hands it (`strain` N1).
7. `magnetic/fold.py:457`: the magnetizability takes the total energy of an SCF that did
   not converge, with no check and no warning. No test asserts this energy (`tests` N1).
8. `wannier/engine.py:132`: `dis_froz=(x, None)` turns the frozen window off, while
   `run_wannier` documents an open upper end.
9. `scf/tetrahedra.py:372`: the clamp acts on products of energy differences, so it
    fires on a live branch once a band spans less than about 1e-4 Ry across one
    tetrahedron. This touches flat bands only.
10. `calculator.py:2824`: `with_spin(4)` keeps a calculator-level `nbnd` unchanged in the
    spinor run, where it wins over the doubled `system.nbnd`. A smeared metal then has
    half the bands it should.
11. Diagnostics only: `scf/residual_split.py:75` and `:165` read a collinear (up, down)
    density as (charge, magnetization), and `workflows/transport.py:823` with
    `transport/momentum.py:195` decide degenerate blocks at 1e-6 instead of the
    resolved `degeneracy_tol`.

Crashes on a valid input:

12. `scf/driver.py:1809`: `default_nbnd` rounds half to even, and outside `nspin = 2` it
    drops `nelup` and `neldw` and the `NINT(1.2*nelup)` term of `setup.f90`. A spinor at
    fixed occupations with `nelec` = 5, 9 or 13 raises at the first filling, and a smeared
    run gets one or two bands fewer than `pw.x`. `notes/AUDIT-2026-09-20.md` called this
    function faithful, and `PLAN.md` records the short count of the cobalt spinor (16
    against 18) as `pw.x`'s.
13. `scf/driver.py:9309` and `:7265`: under k-point pools, `band_precision='mixed'`
    crashes at the switch to double, and a Newton-Krylov warm-up runs the whole k-set on
    rank 0 and then crashes.
14. `magnetic/peierls.py:458`: on a left-handed cell the field is along -a3, and the
    plaquette guard raises after every solve. The error text then suggests a remedy that
    does not reverse the field (`magnetic` N2).
15. `system/symmetry.py:153`: `fft_factors` reads a translation component of about 1e-6
    as a denominator of 1e6, while the symmetry search accepted it as zero at 1e-5.
16. `workflows/vc_relax.py:470`: vc-relax checks the detected group even under `nosym`,
    which `run_relax` was already fixed not to do.
17. `xc/functional.py:863`: the `grad_m` exchange gives NaN where the charge is negative
    and |m| is exactly zero.
18. `io/keywords.py:407`: `from_keywords` crashes when only some atoms carry a starting
    moment.
19. `realtime/propagate.py:1515`: the real-time checkpoint is written in place, so a kill
    during the write leaves a file the resume cannot open. At `:1455` the resume never
    works for `nspin = 2` or for a path that does not end in `.npz`.

Missing refusals:

20. `calculator.py:1630`: `get_landau_levels(route='ultracell')` drops `zeeman=True`,
    `nlevels` and `kappa` without a word, so the documented refusal of `zeeman` cannot fire.
    Three slices found this one independently (`calculator` F3, `magnetic` F2,
    `docs-holes` F2). Beside it, `get_landau_levels(wannier=...)` drops every extra
    keyword (`:1639`), and `get_magnetic_bands` drops `chern`, `local_only` and
    `conv_thr` depending on `scf` (`:1670`).
21. `wannier/disentangle.py:59` and `:68`: an outer window with no eigenvalue at some k
    takes every band below `dis_win_min`, and a frozen window with its ends swapped
    freezes nothing.
22. `response/shg.py:797`: SHG at `nspin = 2` does not refuse a fixed filling that stops
    inside a degenerate shell, while the shift current does.
23. `workflows/orbital_magnetization.py:140`: the orbital magnetization of a smeared metal
    is not refused. Only the direct gap at the mesh points is checked.
24. `workflows/qpi.py:207`: QPI without `energies=` on a fixed-occupation run returns
    NaN maps.
25. `projwfc/projections.py:501`: the projection of a `nosym` spin spiral pairs 2 nk rows
    of orbitals with nk rows of states, and the refusal points the user to that route.

26. `workflows/anisotropy.py:1045`: the torque's `nosym` refusal fires only when the
   texture turns, so a torque at the system's own orientation is summed on the magnetic
   wedge. The skeptic rates it a missing refusal: the wrong number is argued from symmetry,
   and the trigger as the finder wrote it gives zero either way. The committed test grid cannot see the difference (`magnetism-response` N2).
27. `io/pwin.py:279`: a comma-separated value list in a namelist, such as
    `starting_magnetization = 0.5, -0.5`, is cut to its first value without a word. The
    run then starts from another magnetic state than the one written. The skeptic rates it a missing
    refusal.

Cost, time and memory:

28. `calculator.py:1305`: `get_born_charges` solves the electric-field Sternheimer
    again after `get_dielectric_tensor`, while `PLAN.md` says it is a cache hit. The same
    for `get_electrostriction` and then `get_elastic_constants`, which solve the six
    strains twice (`opt-repeat` N2).
29. `kpm/conductivity.py:269`: the KPM conductivity keeps one (ndir, ndir, N, N) moment
    block per k-point of the chunk, with the chunk sized for the SCF. On a card this is an
    out-of-memory stop. `kpm/moments.py:186` runs the Chebyshev recursion three times on
    the same start vectors, once per Pauli matrix.
30. `scf/driver.py:4432`: the k-chunk is sized on the k-set of the SCF and follows the
    calculation to a larger k-list. `for_bands` sizes it again only when more bands are
    asked for.
31. `calculator.py:1823`: `get_stm`, `get_sts`, `get_vertical_transport` and
    `get_momentum_transport` solve the whole grid again on each call. Only QPI takes
    `states=`.
32. Smaller items: `magnetic/fold.py:310` (route P, three blocking reads per k per
    iteration), `scf/driver.py:9701` (two extra host reads per iteration),
    `wannier/kmesh.py:94` (a distance table rebuilt per k and per shell), and
    `magnetic/fold.py:454` (the -1 flux SCF, which time reversal fixes equal to +1).

Tests:

33. The newest phases are not seen by the fast gate: the plane-wave quantum metric of
    P161 (`tests/unit/test_quantum_metric.py:87`), the route P SCF and the magnetizability
    of P177 (`tests/unit/test_magnetic_bloch.py:303`). The levels of P176 step 3 are
    bounded from below only, so an upward error passes
    (`tests/unit/test_magnetic_ultracell.py:524`), and two of the three fast crosschecks
    of P177 read committed `.npz` files and run no defuqumat code.
34. `tests/unit/test_hubbard.py:447` tests the orbital renormalisation with the code's own
    cutoff on a file whose tail is zero, so it cannot see `pseudo/upf.py:408`, where the
    beta-chi overlap stops at each projector's cutoff while QE integrates to `kkbeta`.


Smaller items, not ranked. Each is in the sections above under its file and line; the
status is the skeptic's, and "unchecked" means a skeptic or the second reader raised it
and nobody else read it.

- `scf/driver.py:1802` (driver-a N2, unchecked): The docstring of default_nbnd says degspin is 1 with two channels; in setup.f90 it is the constant 2 in every regime.
- `calculator.py:604` (calculator F2, confirmed): Calculator.estimate reads k_batch=None and band_batch=None as 'not given' and sizes the calculator's default chunk.
- `calculator.py:832` (calculator F4, confirmed): A setup-only option passed to get_scf on a cache hit is silently dropped, origin_tangent included.
- `calculator.py:686` (calculator N2, unchecked): `from_keywords(nbnd=...)` and `from_keywords(nbands=...)` set two different band counts with different behaviour downstream.
- `ultracell/driver.py:2129` (ultracell F2, confirmed): A meta-GGA ultracell's convergence test leaves out the tau residual that run_scf converges on.
- `scf/driver.py:9669` (opt-jit-host N1, unchecked): The Hubbard occupation matrix reads the wavefunction store after the loop has parked it.
- `scf/driver.py:4892` (driver-b F1, confirmed): at_spiral_q and at_kpoints rebuild the projector core in double but keep the single-precision labels, floors and plane chunk.
- `scf/driver.py:2699` (driver-b N1, unchecked): The constructor comment and the guide say a new geometry rebuilds the projectors in double, but at_positions keeps the single core and at_strain(_moving) casts back to single.
- `realtime/pulse.py:131` (realtime F2, confirmed): One kick, three time tolerances: an on-grid kick at t >= 128 can switch on one step late, or be refused on US/PAW.
- `kpm/conductivity.py:159` (opt-memory F2, confirmed): velocity_tangents stores ndir+1 whole-grid projector tables even on a lazy-projector calculation, and it is not selectable.
- `system/symmetry.py:1155` (traps F1, unchecked): The symmetry phases are built complex128 by literal, so sym_rho promotes a single-precision density to double.
- `response/sternheimer.py:589` (sternheimer F1, confirmed): Metal branch of the projector solves the bands past nbnd_occ with a nonzero right-hand side and an operator the level shift does not make positive definite.
- `workflows/shg.py:124` (optics N1, unchecked): get_shg and get_shift_current cannot run on a ground state with a magnetic field or a potential-only meta-GGA, and the error names a remedy neither entry point accepts.
- `scf/continuation.py:703` (scf-small F2, plausible): A single-precision source that went through a checkpoint loses the 1e-4 electron-count tolerance, because band_precision is only in the dropped history.
- `calculator.py:1668` (docs-holes F3, confirmed): get_magnetic_bands and get_magnetizability run the SCF before the regime refusal.
- `solvers/davidson.py:617` (hamiltonian F1, confirmed): The subspace tangent width eta becomes ethr, not the 1e-5 empty-band unit, when a per-band ethr vector has no empty band.
- `response/magnetoelectric.py:222` (topology F3, plausible): The branch guard fires on every delta when symmetry pins the electronic phase at the fold, because the field step is not reduced modulo the quantum.
- `workflows/qpi.py:212` (transport-stm F4, confirmed): Three QPI input checks fire only after the whole-grid band solve.
- `tests/unit/test_degeneracy_tolerance.py:66` (transport-stm N1, unchecked): The P149 transport test checks multiplets() at the resolved tolerance, not that the transport uses it.

Docs that say something the code does not do: `docs/guide/anisotropy.md:145`,
`docs/guide/magnetic-field.md:313`, `README.md:845` (the 3D Z2 row),
`topology/links.py:36` (`SINGULAR` is never used), `topology/polarization.py:256`,
`workflows/polarization.py:241`, `wannier/engine.py:17` (bohr against angstrom),
`cli.py:351`, and the `PLAN.md` line on the Born charges cache. Each one is in the
sections above.

## What was fixed on the branch `audit-fixes`

One line for each item, in the order of the work. "Before" is what the new test read on
the old code, and "slow" names a test that is over the gate's five seconds and so runs
only in the slow set.

- Item 1 and the rest of the KPM slice (`kpm` F1 to F4, N1 to N3). The spin DOS now runs
  on the whole grid of a reduced k-set, and `trace = "exact"` is exact for it too. One
  recursion now gives the three Pauli components instead of three recursions.
  `reconstructed()` rebuilds `spin_dos` from the kept `spin_moments`, the unfolding
  refuses an odd `n_moments`, and its exact start vectors are built per k-chunk from their
  indices. Test: zincblende Se2, fully relativistic, shifted 2x2x2 mesh, exact trace. Before
  the fix the spin DOS read 2.3 states/Ry against a DOS peak of 19.9, and after it the
  spin DOS is below 1e-8 of the DOS (`test_the_spin_dos_of_a_spinor_without_inversion_is_zero`,
  slow, 25 s). The reconstruction and the refusal have fast tests. The four KPM files,
  48 fast tests, pass.
- Item 2 (`optics` F1). `run_conductivity` now hands `(ef_up, ef_dw)` to the Drude term
  and the scissor of a run with two Fermi levels, and it refuses one number there, as
  `run_nesting` does. The fast test uses a stand-in calculation. No converged
  `tot_magnetization` metal was run, so the size of the old error is not measured.
- Item 3 (`hubbard-projwfc` F1). The PDOS tetrahedra take `system.grid_symmetry()`, the
  group the k-set was built with, and agree corner for corner with the DOS's on `nosym`
  aluminium and `noinv` AlAs, where the old call differed
  (`test_the_projected_dos_folds_its_tetrahedra_with_the_runs_group`, fast).
- Items 4 and 5 (`phonon` F1, F2). The phonon at `q != 0` now refuses any reduced k-set
  by `is_reduced`, which catches a P1 crystal on its time-reversal wedge (3x3x3, 14 of 27
  points) where `use_symmetry` is False. It refuses a dispersion correction by name,
  because the frozen Hessian holds the D2 pair sum at `q = 0` only. Summing D2 at q
  (`d2ionq_disp`) is the missing term, and it is not written. Fast tests: one on a real P1
  cell, one on a stand-in, and the D2 refusal. The slow files that reach this guard and
  were not run are `tests/regression/test_phonons_at_q.py`, `test_phonons_at_q_nlcc.py`,
  `test_electron_phonon.py` and `test_streamed_phonons.py`.
- Item 6 (`strain` F1, `opt-repeat` N1). `get_electrostriction` no longer takes the strain
  response cached on the SCF states, so its tangent and its primal are on the same
  refined states, and the options it is given reach the response it solves. The cost is
  one more strain response after `get_elastic_constants`. A source test pins it. The size
  of the old error was not measured: the A/B on `si-electrostriction.in` is two full
  electrostriction runs.
- Item 7 (`magnetic` F4, `opt-repeat` F3, part of `magnetic` N1). `run_magnetic_scf` warns
  by name when its loop ends unconverged, and `run_magnetizability` raises if any of its
  field runs did not converge. `time_reversal_check=False` skips the -1 flux run. It is
  off by default, so the control stays, and the default is yours to change. Fast tests
  use a stand-in SCF.
- Items 8 and 21 (`wannier` F1, F2, F4, N1). `run_wannier` and `run_engine` now make Wannier90's
  `param_read` checks: a frozen window with a lower end and no upper end, and either window
  with its ends swapped, both refused before the band solve. `dis_windows` refuses an outer
  window that holds no eigenvalue at some k. The engine docstring now says angstrom, which is
  what the workflow passes. Fast tests on the committed Si reference and on a two-k array.
  Before the fix, each input ran on without a word.
- Item 9 (`scf-small` F1). The tetrahedron integrand and Bloechl's weights now divide by
  the true product on the selected branch and by one on the dead branches, in place of a
  1e-12 floor on the product. Before the fix, corners at (0, 1, 2, 3) x 1e-5 Ry gave the
  fraction 2.7e-5 at 0.3e-5 Ry, where the closed form is 4.5e-3. After it, the fraction
  matches the closed form to 1e-9 down to spans of 1e-7 Ry, and the gradient stays finite
  on a doubly degenerate tetrahedron (`tests/unit/test_tetrahedron_flat.py`, fast). The
  slow file `tests/regression/test_tetrahedra.py` reaches this code and was not run.
- Item 10 (`calculator` F1, and the `with_spin` half of N2). `Calculator.with_spin`
  rescales the calculator's own `nbnd` as `System.with_spin` rescales the system's, and
  an `nbnd` passed to `with_spin` reaches both. Before the fix, `nbnd=8` stayed 8 in the
  spinor run, and now it is 16 (fast test in `test_calculator.py`).
- Item 11 (`mixing` F1, F2; `transport-stm` F1, F2, N1). The residual split and the
  `becsum` magnetic residual now rotate a collinear `(up, down)` pair into charge and
  moment, so an equal change in both channels reads as charge. The four incoherent sums of
  the vertical map pass the resolved `degeneracy_tol`. `run_momentum_transport` takes
  `degeneracy_tol`, resolves it as the vertical map does, and hands it to the incoherent
  column, and the `PLAN.md` P149 sentence that said it decides no block is corrected. Fast
  tests: two channel tests on arrays, and one check that every incoherent call carries the
  tolerance. All failed on the old code.
- Item 12 (`driver-a` F1, N1, N2). `default_nbnd` is now `setup.f90`'s rule line for
  line: `NINT`, the unconstrained `nelup = INT(nelec + 1)/2` in every regime, the metallic
  20 per cent of each count, and the spinor doubling last. The size estimate uses the same
  rule, since it calls the same function. Hand-worked cases: nelec = 9 as a smeared spinor
  gives 18 (it was 16), and a fixed spinor with 5, 9 or 13 electrons gives 6, 10 and 14
  bands (they were 4, 8 and 12, which raised at the first filling). `tests/unit/test_default_nbnd.py`
  is fast. A run that leaves `nbnd` unset with an odd `nelec` now has one or two more empty
  bands than before.
- Item 13 (`driver-c` F1, F2). The Newton-Krylov warm-up calls `residual.step` again,
  which under pools is the pooled collective that I53 measured, and serially it is the
  same pass as `step_states` without the eigenvalues. `step_states` is kept for the LDOS
  warm-up, which is refused under pools by name, as is `band_precision='mixed'`, whose
  switch casts a `PoolStore` with `jnp.asarray`. Fast tests: the refusals on the
  stand-in pools, and the serial LDOS and anderson warm-ups, which still agree. Nothing was
  run at two pools. The slow test that would show the warm-up working again is
  `tests/unit/test_parallel.py::test_newton_krylov_under_pools_reproduces_one_pool`.
- Item 14 (`magnetic` F5, N2). `MagneticCell.build` refuses a left-handed cell by name
  for every route, because the field is along a1 x a2 and the conventions take the
  orientation from a3. The error for p < 1 no longer advises reversing a3, which leaves
  the field where it was. It now points to p < 0 on route P. Fast test in
  `test_magnetic_conventions.py`. Making the conventions orientation-free, so that a
  left-handed cell runs, is not done.
- Item 15 (`system` F1). `fft_factors` and `symmorphic` test a zero translation at the
  search's 1e-5, QE's `eps2`, and the factors accept only 2, 3, 4 and 6. hcp in the 2c
  setting written to six decimals read `(1000000, 1000000, 2)` before the fix, and reads
  `(1, 1, 2)` now (fast test in `test_symmetry.py`). The stored translations keep their
  1e-6 residue. Snapping them to zero in the search, as QE's zero-translation first
  attempt does, is not done.
- Item 16 (`forces` F2). vc-relax's `checkallsym` returns at once when the run does not
  use its group, as `run_relax` does. Before the fix, a nosym silicon cell strained 3 per
  cent along a3 raised "the cell step broke a symmetry". With symmetry on it still raises,
  and the message now names `nosym` as the way to strain one axis (fast test in
  `test_geometry_invalidation.py`).
- Items 17, 18 and 27 (`xc-io` F1, F2, F3). The `grad_m` exchange takes its finite step
  only where `|m| > 0` and `|m| > 1e-4 |n|`, so a negative charge with no magnetization
  takes the slope, zero there, where it divided 0 by 0. In `from_keywords`, an atom with
  no moment in a magnetic crystal gets a zero row of `STARTING_MOMENTS`. A namelist body
  with text outside any assignment, such as the second value of
  `starting_magnetization = 0.5, -0.5`, is refused with the indexed spelling as the remedy.
  None of the 791 committed and QE test-suite inputs trips it. Fast tests for all three,
  and the NaN test failed on the old code.
- Item 19 (`realtime` F1, N1). The real-time checkpoint name is normalised to what
  `np.savez` writes (`<name>.npz`, `<name>.channel<c>.npz` for two channels). The write
  goes to a temporary name and a rename. A file that cannot be read is skipped with a
  warning and the run starts over. Fast tests on the helpers. The slow test that resumes
  a whole propagation, `test_a_checkpoint_resumes_after_the_chunks_it_records`, was not run.
- Item 20 and the facade half of the magnetic slice (`magnetic` F2, F3, F6, N1;
  `calculator` F3; `docs-holes` F1, F2). `get_landau_levels` refuses `zeeman=True` before
  it picks a route. Each route refuses the other's options. `energy`, `qmax`, `cutoff`,
  `degeneracy_tol`, `vectors` and `bonds` reach `run_landau_levels` by name, and with
  `wannier=` given a leftover option is a `TypeError`. `nlevels` reaches the ultracell.
  `get_magnetic_bands` refuses `chern` and `local_only` with `scf=True`, and `conv_thr`
  without it. The no-cone error names `dirac=False`. The Limits note of the guide says all
  of this, and the left-handed refusal of item 14. Fast tests in `test_magnetic_facade.py`.
- Item 22 (`optics` F2, N1). `second_harmonic` calls the shift current's filling check,
  which takes the quantity's name, so a fixed `nspin = 2` filling that stops inside a
  shell is refused for SHG too. `run_shg` and `run_shift_current` take `tau`, `field` and
  `field_scale`, hand them to the band solve and the potential, and so get them from the
  facade. Fast tests: the refusal through stand-ins, and the signatures. No TB09 or field
  ground state was run through SHG.
- Item 23 and the rest of the topology slice (`topology` F1 to F4, N1). The orbital
  magnetization refuses an occupation other than `fixed` or `from_input` by name, as the
  polarization does and as P64 and the guide say. A smeared insulator has to be rerun with
  fixed occupations. The magnetoelectric field step is reduced modulo the quantum before
  the branch guard, so a phase pinned at half a quantum no longer reads as a jump of a
  whole quantum. The docstrings of `links.py` (no raise on a singular link; the unused
  `SINGULAR` is removed), of `Polarization.total_phase` (not reduced) and of
  `_refuse_ungapped` (which invariants make that refusal) now say what the code does.
  Fast tests in `tests/unit/test_topology_audit.py`.
- Items 24 and 25 (`transport-stm` F3, F4; `hubbard-projwfc` F2). `run_qpi` refuses at the
  door, before the band solve: `exchange` at `nspin = 1`, `sigma <= 0`, an atom index past
  the cell, and a run with no Fermi level when `energies=` is not given, where the axis was
  NaN. `atomic_projections` refuses a spin spiral whatever `symmetrize` says, because the
  orbitals have 2 nk rows against nk rows of states, and its message no longer sends the user
  to `nosym`. Fast tests: a stand-in door for QPI, and the nosym zigzag spiral.
- Item 28, first half (`opt-repeat` F1). `get_dielectric_tensor` keeps its result in one
  slot keyed by its options, cleared with the SCF and with the strain slot, and never filled
  under `keep_internals=True`. So `get_born_charges` after it is a cache hit, which is what
  `PLAN.md` and notebook 19 already said. The fast test counts the calls to a stand-in
  solve. The second half, `get_electrostriction` then `get_elastic_constants` solving the
  strains twice, stays: after item 6 the two responses are on different states by design.
- Item 29 (`opt-memory` F1; `kpm` N1 was done with item 1). The k-chunk of the KPM
  conductivity's moments is capped so that three copies of its `(chunk, ndir, ndir, N, N)`
  blocks fit 1 GiB (`MOMENT_BUDGET`, `moment_chunk`). On a CPU the dial is one k-point, so
  nothing changes there. The module docstring now counts the per-k block. Fast test of the
  cap, and the 19 fast KPM conductivity tests pass. Item 30, the general case (`at_kpoints`
  does not size the dial again for a larger k-list), is not fixed: it needs a decision on
  where the KPM and NSCF routes size their own chunk, and it touches only a card.
- Unranked `calculator` F2, F4, N1 (the `cli.py` help). `Calculator.estimate` reads an
  explicit `k_batch=None` or `band_batch=None` as the whole axis, not as "not given", and
  `defuqumat size --k-batch all` exists. `get_scf` adopts a setup-only option on a cache hit
  too: the SCF stays, and the calculation the responses use is rebuilt. To keep `get_scf`
  and `get_landau_levels` inside the 20-line delegation budget, the slot resets are
  `_drop_states` and the Landau routing is the module-level `_landau_levels`. The stricter
  namelist parser of item 27 found two tests in `test_state_across_boundaries.py` that
  parsed a template with its `{extra}` field unfilled, which the old parser skipped without
  a word. Both now fill it.
- Unranked `ultracell` F2 and `driver-b` F1, N1. The ultracell's stopping test adds the
  box analogue of `tauk_ddot` for a potential-only meta-GGA, checked against Parseval in
  `tests/unit/test_ultracell_audit.py`. No TB09 ultracell was run. `at_spiral_q` casts its
  rebuilt projector core to the band precision, so a single-precision spiral scan stays
  single (fast test in `test_band_precision.py`). The constructor comment and the guide
  now say what each mover does: positions, cell and spiral stay single, a new k-set goes
  to double.
- Unranked `opt-jit-host` N1, and half of F1. The Hubbard occupation matrix of a run that
  does not stream is built before the store is parked, not after. The meta-GGA tau
  residual is dispatched there too and read in the batched `device_get`, so it costs one
  blocking read less per attempt. The Hubbard `ns_ddot` read stays a separate `float()`,
  because the iteration-1 `adjust_ns` and `mixing_fixed_ns` decide its value on the host.
  The host-read, meta-GGA and Hubbard fast tests pass. No count was taken on a card.
- Unranked `traps` F1 and `sternheimer` F1. `apply_symmetry_maps` casts its complex128
  phases to the density's dtype, so a single-precision density is no longer promoted to
  double at every iteration (fast test). The smeared branch of `SternheimerSolver.project`
  zeroes the rows past the cutoff, as `cgsolve_all` stops at `nbnd_occ` and as the
  two-sphere solver already does. Measured on `al2-metal.in` at Gamma, on cores 0 to 3,
  before and after: the optical modes 146.709211, 146.713118 and 311.033502 cm^-1 are
  unchanged to the printed digit. The three acoustic modes, which should be zero, move
  from (0.666854, 1.592681, 1.711706) to (0.666929, 1.592713, 1.711735) cm^-1. The call
  took 62.0 s against 58.7 s, but the gate ran beside both, so that is not a timing.
- Item 26 (`magnetism-response` F1, N1). `run_torque` and the orientation torque refuse a
  reduced k-set at any orientation, the system's own included, unless the group is the
  identity and the inversion of `with_turning_moments`. The Limits note of the anisotropy
  page now says which calls need `nosym` (the torques and the projected decomposition off
  the axis), and that `get_anisotropy` does not. Fast setup test in
  `tests/unit/test_torque_wedge.py`. The size of the old residue on a 3x3x3 grid was not
  measured. The slow files that call the torque, `tests/regression/test_anisotropy.py`,
  `test_orientation_paw.py` and `test_orientation_torque_cost.py`, were not run. They use
  nosym cells except where they assert the refusal, and the new message carries "needs
  nosym" for those.
- Item 34 (`pseudo-paw` F1, N1). `_renormalize_orbitals` integrates `<beta|chi>` over the
  dataset-wide `kkbeta`, widened to the augmentation sphere for PAW, as
  `upf_check_atwfc_norm` does, and no longer over each projector's own cutoff. On
  `Si.pbe-n-kjpaw_psl.0.1.UPF` the projector cutoff is 841 and `kkbeta` 849, and beta is not
  zero in between: with the old range the norm in QE's metric is 0.9999954, after the fix it
  is 1 to 1e-6. The new test fails on the old code and passes now
  (`test_a_paw_orbital_is_normalised_over_kkbeta`), and the old Ni test, which repeated the
  code's own range, now integrates over `kkbeta` as well. The Hubbard unit files and the fast
  spinor PDOS tests pass. The slow `projwfc.x` comparisons on PAW files
  (`tests/regression/test_pdos.py`, `test_spinor_pdos.py` slow part) were not run; the move
  in the orbitals is at the 5e-6 level and goes toward QE.
- Unranked `docs-holes` F3 and F4. `get_magnetic_bands` and `get_magnetizability` call
  `refuse_regime` before the zero-field SCF, as `get_landau_levels` already did. The option
  checks of `get_magnetic_bands` moved to `_magnetic_bands_door` to keep the body under its
  20 lines. Fast test in `test_magnetic_facade.py`: the ground state is replaced by a guard,
  which fired on the old code for all three calls and is not reached now. The README row of
  the Z2 invariants names `calc.get_z2_3d()` beside `calc.get_z2()`.
- `magnetism-response` F2 and N2, docs only. The magnon crossing is read at `omega + i eta`.
  Measured on `h-fcc-magnon-field.in` at `q = 0` (Larmor point 0.02 Ry, 40 frequencies, on
  cores 0 to 3): 0.018375, 0.018247, 0.018215, 0.018207 Ry at `eta` = 4e-3, 2e-3, 1e-3,
  5e-4, which is `0.018204 + 10.7 eta^2`. So the shift is second order, 4.3e-5 Ry at the
  default, and 2 per cent of the 1.8e-3 that the Goldstone truncation costs on this cell
  (`lambda` = 1.0184 at the Larmor point). The three docstrings of `magnons.py` and the P63
  section of `PLAN.md` now say so. The code is unchanged, because the unbroadened samples
  would put the poles of a finite k-mesh on the real axis. Not measured near a Stoner onset,
  where the shift is largest. N2: the docstring of the slow torque test no longer says that
  no committed cell has the right group; since item 26 there is no wedge torque to compare.
- Unranked `hamiltonian` F1. The tangent width of Davidson's subspace solve is now
  `tangent_width(ethr)`, which floors a per-band vector at `EMPTY_ETHR_FLOOR` as it floors a
  scalar. A row with every band occupied gave 1e-13 before, and gives 1e-5 now (unit test in
  `test_subspace_eigh_tangent.py`). No differentiated consumer passes a vector, so no
  derivative changes; the primal does not see `eta`. P148 has a correction line. The fast
  Davidson and degeneracy unit files pass.
- Unranked `realtime` F2. One slack for a kick, `realtime/pulse.py:switch_slack`, 1e-12 of
  the kick time (at least 1e-12), shared by `Kick.kappa_at`, the on-grid test of
  `kick_jumps` and the window of `conductivity_from_kick`. Reproduced first: at `dt = 0.3`,
  `times[431]` is 129.29999999999998, and a kick at 129.3 switched on at 432; an augmented
  run then refused it as off the grid. The fast test checks the switch, the jump index (430,
  the step that ends on 431) and that the conductivity window starts on the same sample. It
  fails on the old code and passes now, with the 37 fast real-time unit tests. The on-grid
  acceptance of `kick_jumps` is now the same slack, tighter than the old `1e-9 dt + 1e-12`,
  so a kick typed a few ulps off still passes and one 1e-10 off a point is refused. Every
  real-time regression file is slow and none was run (`test_realtime.py`,
  `test_realtime_regimes.py`, `test_realtime_augmented_update.py`).
- Unranked `calculator` N2, the other half: not reproduced after item 10.
  `from_keywords(nbnd=12)` sets the calculator's `nbnd` and `from_keywords(nbands=12)` the
  system's, and `with_spin(4)` now gives 24 in both. No change.
- Item 32, third part (`wannier` F3). `kmesh_get` keeps only the supercell translations that
  can reach the chosen shells, a prefix of the sorted list. On the fcc mesh, beside a test run
  so as an order only: 24.4 -> 1.05 s at 8x8x8 and 251 -> 11.0 s at 12x12x12, with every
  output array equal bit for bit at 4x4x4, 8x8x8 and 12x12x12. The 60 fast Wannier tests pass.
  The pair is in `PERFORMANCE.md`. No new test, since the old and new arrays are identical.
- Unranked `scf-small` F2. `SCFResult.band_precision` is a field now, set by `run_scf` and
  carried by the checkpoint as a scalar, and `_check_grid` reads it before falling back to the
  last history entry. Before, a single-precision source read back from a file had an empty
  history and was held to the 1e-6 count tolerance instead of 1e-4. Fast test in
  `test_band_precision.py`: a single-precision silicon SCF, saved and loaded, with its density
  scaled by 1 + 5e-6, passes the check, and the same state marked double is refused. It fails
  on the old code (no such field) and passes now, with the fast checkpoint and continuation
  files (123 passed). An old checkpoint has no such key and loads with `None`, the old
  behaviour.
- Unranked `opt-jit-host` F2, not changed. The `residual_threshold` upload is `nsolve` floats
  beside a whole Davidson solve, and only `fold_check` or a caller who passes the keyword
  reaches it; one k-point in flight is the sized design of route P (the estimate is one
  k-point's). The skeptic's condition for a change, a timing on a card, was not met.
- Item 33 (`tests` holes on P161, P176, P177). P176 step 3: the projected-trial test bounds
  the n = 0 level from above too, at 0.2 and 0.09 B_ph for J = 1 and 2, where it reads 0.130
  and 0.058 (fast, 20.5 s with the rest of that test, which it adds no run to). P177: a new test
  runs the route P SCF itself at +1 and -1 flux on two-atom silicon, from a uniform density,
  on the odd 9 x 9 x 9 box, and asserts `|E(+) - E(-)| < 1e-9` and the energy; 8.9 s, so it is
  marked slow, after one run. While writing it I ran the experiment P177 left open: at
  `conv_thr = 1e-10` the even default box 8 x 8 x 8 gives `E(+) - E(-)` = 2.1e-5 Ry, 10 x 10 x
  10 gives 6.1e-6, and the odd 9 x 9 x 9 gives 1.6e-12, so the asymmetry is the Nyquist slot of
  an even box and not the SCF floor (now in P177). The stub tests of `run_magnetizability`
  came with item 7. The two crosscheck tests that read committed files are labelled as data
  checks, in the file and in the P177 tally. P161: two slow tests compute the plane-wave
  metric of silicon (norm-conserving, 12 Ry, whole 2x2x2 grid; ultrasoft at Gamma, so the
  connection stage runs) and assert symmetry, positivity, isotropy and a pinned trace. They
  cost 6.5 s and 8.5 s in a probe with the same calls, above the gate's five seconds, so they
  are slow and **were not run as tests**; the numbers are the probe's (isotropy to 1.6e-8 and
  1.2e-7, traces 25.827379755580736 and 75.65657570777523 bohr^2). So the gate still has no live
  test of the metric assembly or of the fold loop: the cheapest of each is above five seconds.
- Unranked `opt-memory` F2, recorded and not changed, by fable's decision. Measured on si16 at
  12 Ry on the whole 4x4x4 grid: the velocity's stored table is 96.0 MiB, its three tangents
  288.1 MiB, the resident set grows by 512 MiB across `velocity_tangents` and peaks 846 MiB above
  its start, in 11.0 s. The numbers and the rule (the tables are the peak when `nk_whole nkb > N`)
  are in `PERFORMANCE.md`; the host-loop design is in `notes/GPU-MEMORY-NEXT.md` item 7.
- Item 30 (`opt-memory` N1), by fable's decision. `Calculation.at_kpoints` re-resolves the
  memory dials for the new k-set when the dial is the whole axis, the backend is a card, the
  new list is longer than the one the dials were sized on, and the list is not a block of a
  longer one. The re-resolution is `Calculation._redialled`, now shared with `for_bands`, so
  a stated dial comes back unchanged. Fast test on a stand-in card: `k_batch = None` on the
  SCF's set becomes 4 on the 64-point grid, a stated `None` stays, a CPU is untouched; it
  fails on the old code. The fast files that move calculations across k-sets pass (103 and
  16). Not run on a card.
- Item 31, by fable's decision. One value type for the whole-grid states of the tunnelling
  routes and QPI, `workflows/transport.py:WholeGridStates`, built by `whole_grid_states` (the
  block that was repeated in `run_sts`, `run_vertical_transport`, `run_momentum_transport` and
  `qpi_bands`), and `QPIBands` is now the same type. The three routes take `states=`; with it
  a `grid`, `shift` or `kpoints` is refused, and `nbnd`, `conv_thr`, `k_batch` are not read.
  The states record their own `conv_thr` and `ethr`, and the transport's block tolerance is
  resolved from them. `Calculator.get_tunnelling_states` is the facade call; `get_stm` stays
  out, its k-set being the wedge of the denser grid. `qpi_bands` keeps its 1e-8 and its
  reduced-set refusal, and `run_qpi` repeats the refusal on states from elsewhere. A fix comes
  with it: `qpi_bands` now hands the ground state's field, `field_scale` and `becsum` to the
  solve, as the routes did; before, a QPI ground state under a field or a constrained moment
  was re-solved under the input field (size not measured). Measured on the hydrogen sheet at
  4x4x1 with a probe: the vertical map, the momentum weights and the spectrum equal bit for
  bit with `states=` and with their own solve, at most 0.1 s against 0.9 to 1.0 s a call on an
  idle machine. A
  fast door test is in `test_transport_machinery.py`; the equality test is slow
  (`tests/regression/test_transport.py::test_one_band_solve_serves_the_three_tunnelling_routes`),
  written from the probe and not run as a test. The guide has a section ("One band
  calculation for several maps") with an executed snippet, and the README row names the call.
  The fast files that read these entry points pass (212, and 52 with the guide check).
- Unranked `magnetic` F1 and `strain` F2, which the log had dropped (found by checking every
  non-refuted id of the results against this log; the eight others it did not name by id are
  covered above: `tests` F1 to F4 and N1 by item 33, `opt-repeat` F2 by item 31, `opt-repeat`
  N2 by item 28's second half, `strain` N1 by item 6's source test). Route P's dense fallback
  is refused by number where it is reached: `blochgrid.dense_fallback_bytes(ndim)` counts six
  `(ndim, ndim)` complex128 copies from the code, 33 GB at `ndim = 18432`, and a fallback above
  `memory_limit` raises `MemoryError` with the remedies. It is not added to the estimate made
  before the run, so a solve whose Davidson settles is not refused. Fast test in
  `test_magnetic_bloch.py`, with a limit between the Davidson estimate and the fallback. The
  piezoelectric polar guard's docstring says it refuses PAW too, since
  `require_a_measured_dataset` refuses nothing after I89, and its message names "an ultrasoft
  or PAW dataset".
- `scf-small` F2, second half. The checkpoint the SCF loop writes itself is an
  `_InProgressState`, not an `SCFResult`, and it carried no `band_precision`, so a resume from
  it lost the looser count tolerance again. It carries it now; the test runs an interrupted
  single-precision SCF with `checkpoint_dir` and reads the file back (`None` before, `'single'`
  now). The relaxation's checkpoint saves an `SCFResult` and was covered by the first half.
- Item 31, checked against the old code. The `grid=` calls of the vertical map, the momentum
  weights, the spectrum and a QPI map on the hydrogen sheet give equal arrays, bit for bit, at
  `f0d70b09` (before the refactor, in a worktree) and at the head, on cores 0 to 3. The earlier
  comparison of `states=` against `grid=` ran the new code on both sides and could not have
  shown a change, so this is the check that counts. `run_qpi` now refuses a `grid`, `shift` or
  `kpoints` beside `states`, as the three routes do and as the guide says. The deliverable left
  is a notebook: `get_tunnelling_states` is in the guide and the README and in no notebook.
